# Extraction Iteration Ledger

Working memory for the prompt-iteration loop. READ TOP TO BOTTOM BEFORE EVERY ITERATION.
Rules:
- One entry per iteration, newest LAST. Max ~15 lines per entry.
- Never re-try a hypothesis whose Verdict is REVERTED — the Lesson line says why.
- Full historical detail (iterations 001–011) lives in extraction-eval-log.md; do not append there.
- Fixtures frozen at commit 99b396b (be2611f + JSON comma syntax fix). Scorer/fixture changes require user approval and get their own entry with Verdict ORACLE-CHANGE.
- On feature CLOSE or any P1/P2 prompt/schema/flow change: also update the pipeline sequence diagram `docs/superpowers/diagrams/menu-extraction-pipeline.md` (in the MAIN repo) + its `~/Downloads` copy. It is the fresh-context source of truth for the flow — "Diagram discipline" in the roadmap.

## Do-not-repeat summary (iterations 001–011)

- 001 (`dd9d450`) — single strict structured-output baseline. Strongest overall; options dimension failed (missed targets + false positives), everything else mostly passed.
- 002 (`40f7f09`) — option definition + variant folding. REVERTED: variant folding inflated item counts (El Marcos 46, Nikkori 125) and produced up to 19 option false positives on Nikkori. Do not re-add variant-folding language.
- 003 (`f90991f`) — numbered-item/column completeness rules. REVERTED: did not fix Casa Nostra count, introduced section-header items on El Marcos.
- 004 (`cf741a0`) — nearest-subheading-over-parent-heading rule. REVERTED: Nikkori dropped to 107/120 items and option false positives grew.
- 005 — offline re-baseline + fixture forensics (no paid run). Found Casa Nostra ground truth was wrong (23 was correct, not 33). Iteration 001 outputs re-scored best under corrected fixtures.
- 006 — prompt diet (strip folding sentences). REVERTED: El Marcos lost a section, Nikkori fell to 106/120.
- 007 — nullable `item_number` diagnostics field. REVERTED: El Marcos collapsed to 40/45 items.
- 008 — no entry found in old log (numbering gap; treat as unused).
- 009 — two-pass indexed options extraction. REVERTED: option false positives exploded (El Marcos 14, every menu ≥2).
- 010 — priced-children sections + concept-based two-pass options. REVERTED: Nikkori 99/120 items, section_context worse; two-pass approach abandoned.
- 011 — single-pass 001 baseline + nutrition-material option rule. REVERTED: Brasero Two 18/25 items, El Marcos 4 missed option targets.
- Cross-cutting lessons: (a) variant-folding and two-pass options both trade item accuracy for option coverage and always net-lose; (b) Nikkori item count is highly sensitive to any prompt addition (99–125 range observed); (c) fixtures were edited alongside prompts through 011, so historical rankings are not apples-to-apples — Task 4 offline re-score supersedes them.

## Iteration 012 — orientation + subheading cues + priced-line-is-item (bundled "A")
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: 3 bundled edits — (1) subheading has no price/weight, may be laid out as columns/grids, governs its own block; (2) each separately priced line is its own item named by its printed text, never fold, never name after subheading; (3) an ingredient list joined by "y"/"and" is not options.
- Result: items 3/6, categories 6/6, section_context 3/6, options 2/6, image_quality 6/6 | **20/30 per-menu passes** | option recall 7/21 (brasero 5/5, casa-nostra 2/3, el-marcos 0/11, brasero-two 0/2)
- Failures: brasero-two items 37/25 (CERDO/RES/POLLO/ATÚN meat grid dropped entirely; other rows over-split); el-marcos 44/45 + 1 leaked section-header item, 0 options, section_context missing Pa' los Bukis/spurious Norteños; nikkori 113/120 + section_context worse (6 wrong maps, spurious ROLLOS/POSTRES)
- Verdict: REVERTED (20/30 sits at the bottom of the iter-001 noise band 20–22; did NOT clear the +2 floor — needed ≥23)
- Lesson: abstract orientation/anti-fold/subheading prose is net-neutral — it cannot flip icon-grid parsing (grid went from folded-into-4 to dropped-to-0) or subheading-named items (Revueltos/Fritos still named by subheading, 0 options). Bundling 3 hypotheses masked which helped (Churrasquería split) vs hurt (grid dropped). Next: ONE hypothesis — a few-shot worked grid example, not more prose.
- Archive: live run in scratchpad/iter012-eval.log; ~/Downloads/MenusTesting/{el-marcos,brasero-two}.current-prompt.actual.json

## Iteration 013 — few-shot: swap abstract fold rule for a worked grid example
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: replaced the "same base dish printed several times → ONE item + options" fold rule (baseline lines 32-34) with a concrete example — under a "STEAKS" subheading, "Ribeye 25"/"Sirloin 20" → two items named by line text, section_title=subheading, not folded.
- Result: items 3/6, categories 5/6, section_context 3/6, options 2/6, image_quality 6/6 | **19/30 per-menu passes** | option recall 7/21
- Failures: brasero-two items 44/25 (over-split — but section_context now has Cerdo/Res/Pollo/Atún, "missing: none"); nikkori items 108/120 + section_context far worse (13 wrong maps) + **14 option false-positives** (spirit lists Vodka/Ron/Tequila shredded); el-marcos categories now FAIL (spurious "other").
- Verdict: REVERTED (19/30 < baseline noise band 20–22; worse than iter-012's 20)
- Lesson: **the few-shot achieved its target (brasero-two grid sections appeared) but generalized to ALL subheading+priced-line layouts, detonating Nikkori's spirit/beverage lists.** The meat-cut grid and Nikkori spirit lists are VISUALLY IDENTICAL but need OPPOSITE treatment (split vs fold); the disambiguating signal is not in the image. **The meat grid is NOT prompt-solvable without breaking Nikkori — it is an oracle-level conflict.** Stop trying to fix the grid via prompt; the lever now is fixture/oracle decisions + the pending options rebuilds.
- Archive: live run in scratchpad/iter013-eval.log

## Iteration 014 — deterministic promoteSections() in postprocess (NOT a prompt change)
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: NONE. Code-only: added promoteSections() to postprocess.ts. A price===null item whose options have NO serving-format token (copa/botella/mxn/ml…) is a folded section → un-fold: each option becomes an item under section_title=folded name; drop placeholder. Format-priced items (wines) left intact. Runs stripMenuNumbers → promoteSections → filterServingFormatOptions. Self-check in import.meta.main.
- Result: items 3/6, categories 5/6, section_context 3/6, options 3/6, image_quality 6/6 | **19/30 per-menu passes** | option recall 7/21 (brasero 5/5, casa-nostra 2/3, rest 0)
- Failures: brasero-two items 43/25 + section_context spurious ENTRADAS/ESPECIALIDADES/Churrasquería/ENSALADAS/CALDOS (ALL page-1 content — fixture only covers page 2); el-marcos items 39/45 (model under-extracted this run; promote did NOT fire there — REVUELTOS/FRITOS kept options=priced) + categories spurious "other" ($94 POR NIÑO) + options; nikkori items 116/120 + section_context spurious ROLLOS/POSTRES + 13 option FP (baseline spirit-folding, unchanged by promote)
- Verdict: BLOCKED-ON-ORACLE (code kept, not reverted). The rule provably WORKED and was harmless: brasero-two grid folded by model this run → promote created Cerdo/Res/Pollo/Atún as sections (verified in actual.json, null-price+options remaining=[]); Nikkori wines NOT promoted (guard held, items stayed in noise band, 0 leaked section-header items vs iter-013's 1); brasero+mochomos still 5/5; el-marcos untouched by promote. Score can't rise because brasero-two's section_context is scored against a page-2-only oracle — a fixture defect, not a rule defect.
- Lesson: the folded-section fix belongs in postprocess, not the prompt — it fixes the grid with ZERO Nikkori collateral (unlike prompt iters 012/013). But it is UNSCORABLE until the brasero-two oracle is expanded to cover page 1 (user already approved this direction). Also: promote only fires when the MODEL folds the grid into null-price+options; run-to-run the model sometimes over-splits instead — a postprocess rule can't recover a grid the model didn't fold. NEXT: brasero-two oracle expansion (ORACLE-CHANGE), then re-score with promote in place.
- Archive: live run in scratchpad/iter014-eval.log

## Iteration 016 — force high-detail vision for dense menu text
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: NONE. Request change: set every `image_url.detail` to `"high"`.
- Hypothesis: explicit high-resolution image processing recovers Nikkori's dense roll rows without changing extraction semantics or regressing the five passing menus.
- Pre-run gate: items 5/6; only Nikkori fails at 44/48.
- Result: Nikkori emitted 181 total entries: 45 food and 136 drinks. The food count landed within tolerance but duplicated all six desserts and still omitted/misread rolls.
- Verdict: REVERTED. Explicit high detail caused severe over-extraction and a false-positive count gate.
- Lesson: do not retry `detail: "high"`; count tolerance alone cannot prove every food item appears exactly once.

## Iteration 017 — food-only extraction pass
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: extract food, sides, and desserts only; explicitly skip beverages.
- Hypothesis: removing Nikkori's 66 expected drinks from the response lets GPT-4o complete the dense 48-item food grid without changing image processing.
- Pre-run gate: items 5/6; only Nikkori fails at 44/48.
- Result: Nikkori fell to 37/48 food items. Desserts were complete, but the roll grid dropped 11 items and still duplicated `Nico`.
- Verdict: REVERTED.
- Lesson: do not retry a food-only instruction on the full dense image; reducing requested scope did not improve visual enumeration.

## Iteration 018 — two overlapping vertical crops in one call
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: provided photos may overlap; extract duplicate printed items only once.
- Input change: replace Nikkori's 1196×1896 full-page image with two 1196×1050 crops overlapping by 204 px.
- Hypothesis: larger effective text recovers the dense food grid while overlap protects items crossing the split; both crops remain one model call.
- Pre-run gate: items 5/6; only Nikkori fails at 44/48.
- Result: `finish_reason: length`; the response truncated at 65,192 JSON characters and could not be parsed.
- Verdict: REVERTED.
- Lesson: overlapping crops cannot share one full-menu GPT-4o response; duplicated visual coverage exhausts the output budget. Any crop strategy needs separate calls and deterministic merging.

## Iteration 019 — top food crop only
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: NONE. Input: Nikkori's 1196×1050 top crop only.
- Hypothesis: if larger effective text is the missing lever, the isolated crop should recover all 42 printed rolls in one normal-size response.
- Acceptance for this diagnostic: 42 food rolls, no duplicates; desserts are outside this crop and intentionally ignored.
- Result: 37/42 rolls. It improved several names (`Ipanema Roll`, `Nikkori Maki`, `Van Halen`, `Roiz`, `Amazonas Top`, `Maíz Roll`, `Tricolor`) but omitted Roca Roll, Nevada, California, Orange Roll, Kurimi Roll, and Cosmo de Pollo, while duplicating Dinamita.
- Verdict: REVERTED.
- Lesson: top/bottom cropping improves text recognition but does not solve five-column enumeration. The next credible approach is separate calls for overlapping vertical column crops, which changes cost and merge architecture.

## Iteration 020 — separate left/right food-column calls
- Date: 2026-07-04 | Prompt change: NONE. Input: two 698×1050 crops with 200 px horizontal overlap, sent in separate calls.
- Hypothesis: each call sees at most three menu columns, recovering all 42 rolls; the overlap permits deterministic deduplication of the center column.
- Acceptance for this diagnostic: merged outputs contain all 42 printed rolls exactly once after deduplication.
- Cost: two GPT-4o calls for this Nikkori-only experiment.
- Result: left call returned 27 rolls plus 3 cropped section headers; right returned 17 rolls. After excluding headers and merging overlap duplicates (`Lomo Salteado`, `Mangudo` as `Mangud`/`Manguo`), the union contains all 42/42 printed rolls.
- OCR names improved materially: Ipanema Roll, Nikkori Maki, Ko Ebi Roll, Van Hallen, Orange Roll, Cosmo de Pollo, Maíz Roll, Tricolor, and Marco Roll were recovered.
- Remaining name errors: Nevada→`Nevadal`, Roiz→`Poli`, Mangudo→`Mangud`/`Manguo`, Unagui Masago→`Unagi Masago`.
- Verdict: ACCEPTED as a diagnostic only; no production architecture or cost change approved yet.
- Lesson: dense five-column menus require separate region calls. One full-page call, high detail, food-only prompting, and multi-crop single calls all failed.

- Date: 2026-07-05 | Prompt change: NONE. Input change: production-like 1024px/JPEG 70 full image and full-height left/right crops.
- Matrix:

  | Variant | Exact roll recall | Misses | Extras | Duplicates | Latency | Calls |

  | Three raw | incomplete | — | — | — | middle crop truncated | 3 |
  | Two compressed run 1 | 11/42 | 31 | 24 | none | 38,020ms | 2 |
  | Two compressed run 2 | 11/42 | 31 | 25 | none | 35,280ms | 2 |

- Crop-count decision: keep `DENSE_CROP_COUNT = 2`. Three crops fail the mandatory no-truncation condition in all three compressed runs and therefore cannot justify the extra `$0.03` call.

## Iteration 022 — four overlapping 2×2 compressed crops
- Date: 2026-07-05 | Prompt change: NONE. Input change: four 718×1138 crops at X `0/478` and Y `0/758`, each compressed to a maximum 1024px edge and JPEG quality 70 before a separate call.
- Acceptance: at least two of three runs recover all 42 exact printed roll names, contain zero unresolved normalized duplicates, and complete all four calls without timeout or truncation.
- Results:

  | Run | Exact roll recall | Misses | Extras | Duplicates | Summed latency | Calls |
  | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | 1 | 32/42 | 10 | 15 | none | 47,677ms | 4 |
  | 2 | 33/42 | 9 | 14 | none | 45,436ms | 4 |
  | 3 | 32/42 | 10 | 15 | Nikkori Roll | 45,325ms | 4 |

- Stable OCR substitutions included Filadelfia→`Fildelfia`/`Fidelifila`, Van Hallen→`Van Halen`, Nevada→`Nevadal`, California→`California I`, Kurimi Roll→`Kurimu Roll`, and Cosmo de Pollo→`Pollo de Pollo`.
- Completion: all 12 calls returned `finish_reason: stop`; no timeout or truncation occurred.
- Cost: 12 GPT-4o calls, approximately `$0.36`.
- Verdict: REJECTED, 0/3 passing runs.
- Lesson: reducing both crop dimensions fixes the truncation/timeout failure but does not meet exact printed-name accuracy under production compression. Do not wire Tasks 7–9 without a new extraction/name-verification design.
- Archive: `/Users/santiagoaguirre/Downloads/MenusTesting/nikkori.grid-compressed-r{1,2,3}-{1,2,3,4}.actual.json`

## Iteration 023 — Full uncompressed page, detail auto vs high (isolate the compression variable)
- Date: 2026-07-07 | Prompt change: NONE. Request change: threaded optional `detail` through `runExtraction`; default production path still omits it. Input: full raw `NikkoriMenu.png` (3.0 MB, native ~1196×1896), no client compression.
- Hypothesis: the `compressImage.ts` clamp (longest edge→1024 + JPEG q0.7 on every image) — not crop geometry — is what breaks Nikkori; removing it may pass the full page and make cropping unnecessary.
- Confound controlled: `extract.ts` sends NO `detail`, so every prior iteration ran `detail:"auto"` (uncontrolled). This iteration threads an optional `detail` param through `runExtraction` and tests two variants: (a) detail auto (production-faithful minus compression); (b) detail "high" (removes the resolution confound).
- Ceiling caveat: OpenAI high-detail normalizes the shortest side to 768px regardless of input size, so a raw full page is still only ~768px across 5 columns (~150px/col). Prediction: likely still undercounts and may reproduce iter-021's full-image timeout; running to prove compression-alone was/wasn't the culprit.
- Acceptance: food count ∈ [45,51] AND exact roll recall ≥40/42 (trustworthy, not dessert-duplication inflated) AND all calls `finish_reason: stop`. If met → compression was the culprit, cropping unnecessary.
- Baseline to beat: compressed full image = 44/48 food (iter 015); timed out after 120s under production-like compression (iter 021).
- Results: `detail:auto` timed out after 120s (no actual.json). `detail:high` returned `finish_reason:stop` in 42,555ms with 110 total items, 41 food items, and exact roll recall 25/42; layout was `dense:true,crop_direction:none`.
- Verdict: REJECTED. Full raw page does not meet the count gate or trustworthy roll-name proxy, and `auto` is not shippable under the current timeout.
- Lesson: removing client compression alone is not enough; the full-page resolution budget still misses dense-grid roll names. Do not pursue unbounded-time full-page auto for Feature 1.
- Archive: `/Users/santiagoaguirre/Downloads/MenusTesting/nikkori.raw-full-high.actual.json`

## Iteration 024 — 2×2 crops with compression removed
- Date: 2026-07-07 | Prompt change: NONE. Input change: four raw `NikkoriMenu.grid-raw-{1..4}.png` 2×2 crops with NO client compression, at `detail:"high"` — the exact cells iter-022 ran COMPRESSED (32–33/42).
- Hypothesis: OpenAI applies the 768px short-side normalization per image, so an uncompressed 2-column crop reaches the model at ~2× the effective text resolution of the full page. Isolates "remove compression while cropping" — the only variable that differed from iter-020's 42/42 raw diagnostic.
- Merge: same `mergeItemSources` dedup pipeline as iter 022.
- Acceptance (mirrors iter 022): ≥2/3 runs recover all 42 exact printed roll names, zero unresolved normalized duplicates, all 4 calls `finish_reason: stop`, no timeout/truncation.
- Cost: 4 calls/run × 3 runs = 12 calls ≈ $0.36.
- Results:

  | Run | Exact roll recall | Misses | Extras | Duplicates | Summed latency | Calls |
  | --- | ---: | --- | ---: | --- | ---: | ---: |
  | 1 | 39/42 | Nevada, Kurimi Roll, Salmón Especial | 8 | none | 69,560ms | 4 |
  | 2 | 40/42 | Nevada, Kurimi Roll | 12 | none | 60,457ms | 4 |
  | 3 | 40/42 | Nevada, Kurimi Roll | 8 | none | 63,680ms | 4 |

- Completion: final rerun completed all 12 calls with `finish_reason:stop`. An earlier first attempt timed out before producing a scored run and was superseded by the completed rerun.
- Verdict: REJECTED, 0/3 passing runs. It improves over compressed 2×2 but never reaches the required 42/42 exact roll recall.
- Lesson: raw 2×2 high-detail crops are close but consistently misread stable aliases (`Nevada`→`Nevadal`, `Kurimi Roll`→`Kurimu Roll`) and sometimes miss `Salmón Especial`; name normalization/correction or a different oracle-aware extraction design is now the likely lever, not more compression/crop geometry.
- Archive: `/Users/santiagoaguirre/Downloads/MenusTesting/nikkori.grid-raw-high-r{1,2,3}-{1,2,3,4}.actual.json`

## Iteration 025 — offline re-score of iter-024 archives under clarified count±3 + no-dup bar
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Prompt change: NONE. Offline only ($0): `scripts/eval-adaptive-crops.ts` merged each run's 4 archived raw-2×2 crops via `mergeItemSources`, filtered drinks, reported merged food count + normalized-name duplicates.
- Clarified bar (user, 2026-07-06): Nikkori food count within ±3 of 48 → [45,51] AND no duplicated food items. Exact roll-name spelling NOT required (Nevada→Nevadal, Kurimi→Kurimu are acceptable misreads). Supersedes the 42/42 exact-recall bar iters 016–024 were rejected against.
- Result: r1 actual=47 dups=[] PASS; r2 actual=52 dups=[] FAIL (+1 over the 51 ceiling); r3 actual=48 dups=[] PASS → **2/3 pass** under the clarified bar.
- Failure mode (r2): crop-boundary over-split produced near-variants `Kurimu Roll` + `Kurimu Roll I` and `Salmón Samba I` that escape exact-normalized dedup but inflate the count past 51. NOT undercount, NOT a true duplicate by exact name — a roll appearing twice under two spellings (violates "appears exactly once").
- Verdict: DIAGNOSTIC (offline, no prompt change, archived data — not the live 3/3 the exit gate needs).
- Lesson: the remaining Nikkori lever is a tighter merge/dedup in `mergeItemSources` (collapse near-variants — e.g. a trailing lone roman-numeral/`I` or single-token suffix — so `Kurimu Roll I`→`Kurimu Roll`), which fixes r2's overcount AND enforces "appears exactly once." NOT more crops/compression/prompt edits. Next: add the no-dup check to `eval-extraction.ts` items scorer, then eval 026 (live 3/3 `--gate items` with dense-crop routing).

## Iteration 026 — tighten mergeItemSources: a null section is compatible, not distinct
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Code change (NOT prompt): in `src/lib/adaptiveExtraction.ts` `duplicate()`, the near-name (edit-distance) merge required identical `section_title`; a crop that emits `section_title:null` was treated as a different section and blocked the merge. Now a null/empty section on either side is compatible. TDD: added failing test "merges near-name variant when one source omits the section", then fixed.
- Root cause (from r2 crop trace): iter-024 crop 3 emits `section_title:null`; `Kurimu Roll I`/`Salmón Samba I` (spurious trailing " I" OCR) matched their sectioned copies on price+category+edit-distance≤2 but were rejected on the section mismatch → survived as extras → overcount 52.
- Eval (offline re-score of iter-024 archives, $0, same `eval-adaptive-crops.ts`): r1=47 PASS, r2=48 PASS (was 52), r3=48 PASS → **3/3 under the clarified count±3 + no-dup bar**. r2 `missing` UNCHANGED [Nevada, Kurimi Roll] before/after → collapsed only spurious variants, lost zero oracle rolls. All 11 unit tests pass.
- Verdict: ACCEPTED (offline). Turns iteration 025's 2/3 into 3/3 with no roll loss and no regressions.
- Lesson: the last Nikkori blocker was a merge-dedup gap (null section blocking variant collapse), NOT resolution/OCR. This is offline validation on archived crops — NOT the exit gate. To CLOSE Feature 1: (1) add the no-dup check to `eval-extraction.ts` items scorer; (2) iteration 027 = LIVE 3/3 `--gate items` on all 6 menus with dense-crop routing wired into the live path (~$0.90).

## Iteration 027 — LIVE 3× exit-gate attempt, all 6 menus, dup check active
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Code: `scripts/eval-027-live.ts` (5 menus single production call; Nikkori = 4 uncompressed 2×2 tiles @`detail:"high"`, merged). Items scorer now also fails same-name+same-price duplicates (step 1).
- Result: **0/3 consecutive GATE PASS.** Run 1 PASS (all 6). Run 2 FAIL (el-marcos). Run 3 FAIL (el-marcos).
- Per menu: brasero 28/28 ×3; brasero-two 45/47/45 (+1/+3/+1 — passed but touched the +3 edge); casa-nostra 23/23 ×3; mochomos 22/22 ×3; **nikkori 49/48/50, 0 dups — PASS all 3 (historical blocker SOLVED live & stable)**; el-marcos 32(0 dup, PASS)/36(1 dup, FAIL)/36(1 dup, FAIL).
- Verdict: FAIL gate (0/3). But Nikkori passes live. New blocker = el-marcos intermittent EXACT duplicate (same name+price twice) on the NON-dense single-call path, which has no dedup (only the crop/merge path dedups). The step-1 dup check correctly exposed a pre-existing latent duplicate that count-only scoring had been hiding.
- Lesson: dedup is inconsistent across paths — dense menus dedup via `mergeItemSources`, single-call menus don't. Candidate fix: add exact (name+price) dedup to `postprocessItems` (general, safe — different-price dishes like Revueltos untouched). Also brasero-two is count-unstable near +3 (watch). Confirm the el-marcos duplicate is an artifact (not two real dishes) before/with the fix; re-run 3× after.

## Scorer refinement — duplicate = name + price + DESCRIPTION (ORACLE-CHANGE, user-approved)
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Scorer change only; NO prompt/extraction change. Supersedes the step-1 (name+price) duplicate definition and the iter-027 "add postprocess dedup" candidate (rejected — it would have DELETED real variants).
- Investigation: eval 027's el-marcos duplicate was `CHILAQUILES (70gr.) @138` ×3 — three preparations (Tradicionales / Regionales / Divorciados) with DISTINCT descriptions (found in `el-marcos.current-prompt.actual.json`). Not a spurious double-extraction — a menu item with variants. Folding variants into one item + options is Feature 2's job.
- Change: the items-dimension duplicate key went `name@price` → `name@price@description`. A true duplicate = same dish listed twice (name, price AND description identical, e.g. Nikkori `Kurimu Roll @169` ×2). Same name+price, different description = a variant, NOT a duplicate. (Revueltos 78/84/90 was already safe via distinct prices.)
- Effect: Chilaquiles-split runs (36 food, inside 34±3) will pass on count with 0 flagged dups; true identical duplicates still caught. TDD: added "different descriptions = distinct variants" self-check, kept "same name+price+desc = duplicate"; `--self-check` passes. Also: `eval-027-live.ts` now logs duplicate names inline and dumps failing menus' `actual.json`.
- Verdict: ORACLE-CHANGE.
- Next: re-run eval 027 (3× live) — expect el-marcos green; watch brasero-two's +3 edge.

## Iteration 028 — LIVE 3× re-run after the duplicate ORACLE-CHANGE
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Code: `eval-027-live.ts` (same recipe) + refined dup check (name+price+description) + per-menu failure dumps (`<menu>.eval027-r<run>.actual.json`).
- Result: **1/3 consecutive GATE PASS** (run 3 clean). The duplicate problem is GONE — 0 duplicate items on el-marcos all 3 runs; the Chilaquiles-variant ORACLE-CHANGE worked.
- Per menu: brasero 28/28 ×3; brasero-two 45/43/43 (all pass, no +3 this session); casa-nostra 23/23 ×3; mochomos 22/22 ×3; **nikkori 51/48/49 — PASS all 3 (one +3 edge); solved live across BOTH sessions (iter 027 & 028)**; el-marcos 30(FAIL)/30(FAIL)/32(PASS).
- el-marcos NEW failure mode (runs 1&2, identical, from dumped actual.json): count 30/34 = −4 undercount AND 1 phantom section-header. Both = "PA' LOS BUKIS" (a SECTION) extracted as a food item @94 + "$94 POR NIÑO" (price note) as a null-price item → ~28 real dishes + 2 junk = 30.
- Photo adjudication (ElMarcosMenu.png): el-marcos food count is CONVENTION-DEPENDENT — ≈29 by dish-name (folding variants) vs ≈35 by priced-line (Revueltos has 3 price lines, Fritos 2, Hot Cakes 2, Waffles 2, Plato Surtido 2; Chilaquiles = 1 name / 3 preps @138). Fixture=34 assumes a mostly-split convention. The model non-deterministically folds/splits → lands 30–36, straddling the ±3 band [31,37]. "Pa' los Bukis" is a $94/niño kids COMBO section, not a dish.
- Verdict: FAIL gate (1/3). Nikkori remains solved; el-marcos is the SOLE blocker.
- Lesson: el-marcos's count instability IS the variant fold/split ambiguity — entangled with Feature 2 (options). It likely will NOT stabilize under Feature 1 alone until a canonical fold/split convention is set. DO NOT prompt-tune (roadmap lesson — trades menus). Open user decisions: (a) adjudicate/reset the el-marcos oracle (34 vs ≈29–35); (b) whether Feature 1 can close el-marcos before Feature 2, or el-marcos gets deferred/paired with Feature 2; (c) how to treat the "Pa' los Bukis" combo + strip the "$94 POR NIÑO" junk.

## Iteration 028b — el-marcos real-vs-junk offline diagnostic (free, $0)
- Date: 2026-07-06 | Offline analysis of 4 saved el-marcos extractions (`scratchpad/diagnose-elmarcos.ts`): classify food items into section-header junk / price-note junk / real dishes; fold variants by distinct name; measure stability.
- Finding: **distinct real dish-NAMES per run = 29 / 29 / 28 / 28 (STABLE).** Raw item CARDS = 29 / 36 / 28 / 28 — the 36 is variant-SPLITTING (same ~29 dishes packaged as more cards, e.g. Revueltos/Fritos/Hot Cakes/Waffles multi-price lines). Junk: "PA' LOS BUKIS" (section) + "$94 POR NIÑO" (price note) in 2/4 runs. Core dish set present in EVERY run = 27; the 3 "unstable" names are just the yogurt spelling wobble (yogurt/yogurth = one dish) + the Pa'los Bukis combo line.
- Conclusion: **el-marcos has NO Feature-1 completeness gap** — it reliably finds ~28–29 real dishes every run. Its gate failure is 100% (a) variant fold/split = Feature 2, and (b) section/price junk = Feature 3. The scorer counts raw cards (swing 28–36) against oracle=34 (a split-convention count) → the mismatch is not Feature 1's responsibility.
- Implication: counting raw cards CANNOT stabilize (28–36 is too wide for any fixed ±3). Real options: (A) count DISTINCT dish-names (variant-robust) and set el-marcos oracle ≈29 → Feature 1 then measures completeness only, immune to Feature 2's fold/split; or (B) defer el-marcos to close alongside Feature 2. Correcting the oracle NUMBER alone while still counting cards will NOT work.

Chain: stripMenuNumbers → foldVariantCards → promoteSections → filterServingFormatOptions.

## Iteration 042 — three deterministic levers (fold triples, header-echo filter, any-match expectations) ($0)
- Date: 2026-07-10 | All TDD; NO prompt change.
- L1 postprocess `foldVariantCards`: 3+ same-name cards with pairwise-distinct descriptions fold as a variant family EVEN at equal prices — OCR double-reads come in pairs, so the Nico pair guard is preserved (self-check for both shapes).
- L2 postprocess `dropHeaderEchoes` (new, after promoteSections): drop a price-less, description-less, option-less item whose name equals another item's section_title — the header-echo class (kills nikkori's 9 pseudo-items at tile level, where drink items carrying those sections still exist; also el-marcos' stray-header class from F2 gotchas).
- L3 scorer wrongMappings: an expectation is satisfied when ANY name-matching food item carries the expected section (crop overlap / stable misreads produce impostor same-name cards; the true item's mapping is what the check is about).
- Offline validation: el-marcos gate dumps r1+r2 → options 19/19 PASS both (L1 confirmed). nikkori gate dumps stay red offline — BOTH are double-postprocess artifacts: the merged dump lost the drink items L2 keys on (live runs per-tile), and re-folding merges the cross-tile Fire Dragon pair that live keeps separate (the dump itself contains both cards = live fold never saw them together). Standard archives: no regressions (only the two known stale-archive artifacts).
- Verdict: ACCEPTED (L1 offline-proven; L2/L3 unit-proven, live-confirmed at the next gate).

## Eval 048 — production wiring of the per-page recipe: code landed + gate attempt 1 (0/3, upstream drift)
- Date: 2026-07-10 | Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-10-per-page-multi-photo-wiring-design.md`; plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-10-per-page-multi-photo-wiring.md` (both this worktree). Pre-release critical-path #1.
- Code (4 commits, all TDD/deno-test green): `mergeItemSources` moved to `supabase/functions/analyze-menu/merge.ts` (retyped on `ExtractedMenuItem`, 6 tests moved); shared `extractWithRetry` (one retry on timeout / finish_reason=length — now PRODUCTION, was eval-only) + `runPagedExtraction` in `extract.ts` (1 photo ⇒ 1 call default detail; N photos ⇒ N parallel `detail:"high"` calls → merge → ONE unified menu payload: usable=AND, issues deduped, layout=first dense page's else page 1's, raw_response=JSON array of per-page payloads); edge `stage:"extract"` now calls `runPagedExtraction` (response shape unchanged, client untouched); eval runner single/multi-page branches collapsed into the same shared call (DENSE_TILES branch untouched). Multi-page detail LOCKED to `high` (gate-proven iter-036); the cheaper `auto` A/B deferred to the post-release cost pass (user 2026-07-10: speed/cost not pre-release concerns). Unified single-menu guarantee (user 2026-07-10): N pages are ONE menu — merge collapses cross-page duplicates; enrichment keeps running once per scan on the merged list.
- Also landed: two pre-existing F4 test drifts fixed (missing `grams` in postprocess_test helper + extract_test expected item — suite didn't typecheck on HEAD); the pre-existing working-tree Gemini-enrichment removal rode along in the index.ts commit (flagged in its message).
- Smoke: brasero-two (only multi-page menu) via shared path, EVAL_RUNS=1 — all 5 dims PASS, Taco Loiro "A elegir" present, 47/44 in-band.
- Gate attempt 1: 0/3. ALL failures on el-marcos (single-page — its request is byte-identical to the old path; the new code cannot be the cause), each run a different known-class signature: r1 options (2 missed + 1 FP + Hot Cakes jamón price null); r2 grams (printed weights NOT transcribed — model flipped to title-case names without "(70gr.)" text; Plato Surtido 5gr digit misread); r3 options (Plato Surtido option line dropped) + spurious section "Norteños" (anglicization/variant class ≈ nikkori "Rolls") + grams all-null again. Other 5 menus: green in every run. Verdict: upstream GPT-4o transcription drift (seed 17/temp 0 is best-effort; eval 047 passed 3/3 with the identical request hours earlier).
- el-marcos solo probe (3×, ~$0.09): 2/3 PASS — grams recovered in all 3 runs (drift subsiding); r3 dropped Plato Surtido's option line again (same class as the UNCHECKED with-option price 82 — the model often skips that printed line entirely).
- Next: gate attempt 2 (restart-count protocol — plan's known-nondeterminism rule). If it fails on the same class: STOP and hand the user the decision (rerun later vs oracle ruling on Plato Surtido options / "Norteños" tolerance).

## Oracle ruling — Plato Surtido options → UNCHECKED (ORACLE-CHANGE, user-approved 2026-07-10, option 3)
- User picked option 3 (ruling + one gate rerun). Scorer gains explicit `unchecked: true` on an `items_with_options` entry: tolerated both ways — extracted options are consumed (never false positives), absent options are never a miss. NOT implemented by removing the entry (removal flips to FP when the model DOES transcribe the line) nor by `options: []` (existing semantic "require some option" kept intact, self-check line ~1050). ## Iterations 049–050 — P1 hardening: printed weights + y/and joins (user-approved prompt work, 2026-07-10 evening)
- User redirected: fix the fragility properly, prompt changes allowed, cost unconstrained (test/perfect phase). Systematic debugging on the el-marcos weight-drop mode.
- Root cause (el-marcos grams): GPT-4o non-deterministically flips into a document-global "normalize" transcription mode — title-cases the all-caps menu AND discards parenthetical weights ("(70gr.)"). Diagnostic probe ×8 (fingerprint logging added to runExtraction — one console.log): both caps and title-case styles came from the SAME `system_fingerprint` (fp_1e36f93198) → sampling nondeterminism at temp 0, NOT backend drift; the weight-drop submode is bursty (3/10 runs 20:30–21:45, 0/8 at 22:0x). "Wait for a window" rejected — a real user can't.
- Iter 049 (P1 v1): added "always transcribe [weights] verbatim … exactly where it is printed". el-marcos fixed (gate attempt 4: el-marcos 3/3 all dims) BUT the layout-literalism phrase caused a NEVER-BEFORE-SEEN mode on brasero-two r2: "TACO LOIRO (sirloin)" returned as a section heading with variants as 3 bare items (sirloin@135/picaña@140/pollo@150) → items 49/44 + option target gone + spurious section. Frozen gates caught the dimension trade exactly as designed. Attempt 4: 1/3.
- Iter 050a (P1 v2, single variable): removed the layout phrase — "keep it verbatim in that item's name or description". Probes: brasero-two 6/6 (fold intact), el-marcos 4/4 (weights kept). ## Eval 056 — ticket #3 compression ladder + q90/q95 probes: NO 2048px JPEG setting clears the row; passthrough is the winner (~$2.50)
- Date: 2026-07-12 | Ticket #3 (client compression fidelity, spec `2026-07-12-client-compression-fidelity-design.md`, plan same date). Executed by a separate LLM off the plan; probes designed/adjudicated here. This branch's intake code already stores originals; full 8-file slice sync is part of the ticket.
- **4-arm ladder** (`probe-fidelity.ts` rewrite: original / 1024q70 / 1536q80 / 2048q85, all 6 menus, detector assertions, payload logging, PROBE_MODES/PROBE_MENUS filters). Results: original 6/6 clean (nikkori dense-signals). 1024q70 reproduced T5: brasero FAIL options+grams (Pollo 55-vs-45, Puré/Chamorro grams null), el-marcos FAIL options+section_context+grams (3 price mismatches, 7 missed targets, omelette spurious section, Pastel Azteca grams null). 1536q80 + 2048q85: better but both FAIL brasero options (STABLE Chorizo extra price 25→55 digit misread) + el-marcos options; 2048q85 also mochomos section_context (Tacos de Camarón al Chipotle→item not found). Payloads: originals 1.1–7.5M b64 chars (all under the 10M per-photo cap); 2048q85 0.57–1.4M.
- **q90/q95 brasero ×3**: q90 FAIL 3/3 (same Chorizo 55); q95 PASS 3/3 → quality, not just dimension, drives the loss.
- **q95 full row**: brasero/brasero-two/casa-nostra clean, nikkori dense-signals, but el-marcos options FAIL + mochomos section_context FAIL. **Ambiguity-rule re-probe ×3 (el-marcos+mochomos)**: el-marcos options 3/3 IDENTICAL signature (jamón 94-vs-90 AND jamón null-vs-78, every run — note 94 = a DIFFERENT price bleeding in, not a digit flip); mochomos 3/3 identical (Tacos de Camarón al Chipotle never found). 4/4 stable incl. the full-row run = REAL compression losses at 2048/q95, NOT flakes (el-marcos categories flaked once — that one IS a flake). JPEG-at-2048 lever exhausted.
- **Ruling (user, 2026-07-12): ORIGINAL-BYTES PASSTHROUGH is the phase-1 upload path** when the photo fits a 9M-char base64 budget (all fixtures + typical 12MP camera JPEGs do); fallback for oversized photos = 2048/JPEG q0.95 (best measured compressed setting; its el-marcos/mochomos limits ledgered here as the fallback's known limits). Harness `productionPhotoData` mirrors the same rule → gate phase-1 input = passthrough = the proven original arm.
- Noticed, NOT changed (works in production, T9-verified): client tile uploads are PNG bytes sent as bare base64 that the edge labels `data:image/jpeg` — OpenAI sniffs bytes. Phase-1 passthrough will send proper `data:` URLs with correct mime (edge accepts them as-is, extract.ts:209).

LESSON: verify a retarget target's tile-spelling stability IN THE DUMPS before pinning (the El Tendedero retarget was made without checking and failed 3/3).
- I8 re-probe result: first-ever polloteria items PASS (r1 all dims except 1 options FP); r2/r3 items 50-51 vs cap 47. ## RULINGS 2026-07-14 (user) — duplication is trust-critical: verification pass pulled INTO scope; band stays +5
- **Duplication/phantom ruling (supersedes the relaxed reading of the early-release bar):** single-digit price misreads or a few misread chars in a name = acceptable; DUPLICATED or INVENTED items = release-blocking ("user asks the waiter for a plate that doesn't exist" = trust loss). Consequence: polloteria items_over_tolerance STAYS 5 (NOT widened); the 2026-07-12 post-release deferral of the name-verification pass is REVOKED for the tile path — the pass moves into horizontal Phase 1 scope (user approval 2026-07-14).
- **Verification pass design (approved):** after a 4-tile group's merge+hygiene in `runGroupedExtraction`: ONE extra GPT-4o call — inputs = the group's 4 tile images + the merged items as {index, name, price, grams}; strict json_schema {verdicts:[{index, printed}]}; judge ONLY what is printed (item name findable; printed price/weight not clearly different). printed=false → item dropped deterministically. FAIL-OPEN: verifier call error or missing index ⇒ keep the item (never lose a scan to the verifier). Tile-path only; injectable for unit tests. Honest cost note: the verify call re-sends 4 high-detail tiles ⇒ roughly DOUBLES a dense page's image cost (accepted-domain per the quality-first cost rule; optimize later).
- **Nico ruling:** nikkori items_with_options += {name_contains "Nico", unchecked: true} (single cosmetic toggle on a single dish tolerated; eval-054 class; real fix = this same verification machinery if it ever extends to options).
- Next: settled fixes ($0) → verification build + polloteria probe ×3 (~$0.55) → confirmation ×3 (~$0.45) → aspect-scoped detector trigger probe (~$0.90).

## Eval 063 — verifier v1 probe: over-count FIXED, but the verifier over-deletes real dishes (~$0.55)
- Date: 2026-07-14 | Verification pass v1 (single printed:boolean, drop on false) on polloteria 2x2 ×3: items PASS 3/3 (39/39/44) and every phantom + flaked twin correctly killed — but REAL dishes died with them: Dedos de Queso (printed!), Alitas card, Papabones!, Boneless Jr(200gr), and ALL Ensaladas (their titles are model-mangled → "not printed" verdicts; whole section vanished 2/3). Root cause: v1 conflates two questions — "is this dish printed?" and "does the price match?" — and deletes on either; a price/format flake on a REAL item = deletion, inverting the user's ruling (field flakes acceptable, missing dishes not).
- Also surfaced: parseItemGrams comma bug — printed "(1,200gr)" parses as 1.2 (comma read as decimal); Megachorla grams pin flaked 1.2/1200 across runs. Deterministic fix queued.
- Option FPs = fixture match-key gaps: model stably reads "Cabeza Pollotera" (oracle spelling "Cubeta" no longer matches the unchecked entry — need BOTH spellings as unchecked entries); "El Tenderazo" junk option (unstable-name family "El Tender*" needs its own unchecked entry).
- Verifier v2 design: two-field verdict {name_printed, price_matches}; drop iff name_printed=false (phantom), OR price_matches=false AND a same-name (one-indel) sibling was kept (twin disambiguation — keep the verified copy). Real item + price flake = KEPT. Prompt hardened: name_printed judges dish existence ignoring spelling flakes/size annotations; a name combining words from two different printed dishes is NOT printed.

## Eval 064 — verifier v2 probe: items/options/categories/grams GREEN 3/3; name-verification still over-rejects; Ensaladas = Phase-2 coupling (~$0.55)
- Date: 2026-07-14 | v2 (two-field verdict, twin-tie price drops, hardened prompt) + parseItemGrams comma fix + Cabeza/"El Tender" unchecked keys. Polloteria 2x2 ×3: items PASS 3/3, options PASS 3/3, categories+grams PASS 3/3. ONLY section_context fails 3/3: missing Ensaladas.
- Findings: (a) price_rejected = 0 in ALL runs — the price/twin-tie path is dead weight (merge rules already kill price twins upstream); (b) name-verification variance is high: r1 dropped only 3 (near-perfect), r2/r3 dropped 12 incl. clearly-printed dishes (Dedos de Queso, Alitas, Grande, Papabones!) — searching ~50 names across 4 overlapping images in one call is too hard a task; (c) the Ensaladas cards are the SIZE-VARIANT title items (Phase 2's subject): extraction mangles "M (150gr) / G (300gr)" into Ni/Si/Sisi/St so badly that any verifier balks → whole section dies. Phase-2 leakage into Phase 1, not a verifier bug.
- v3 design (approved-pending): per-tile, name-only, PRE-merge — each tile's own items verified against that single tile image (same image cost, trivially easier task, phantoms die at source); price machinery removed; merge rules keep owning twins. Ensaladas: temporary oracle tolerance proposed (sections→section_headers + drop pins, restore-at-Phase-2 TODO) pending user approval.

Polloteria 2x2 ×3: r1 items/options/cats/grams PASS, section_context FAIL (S&H heading never read — items under "Acompañadas de Papas" subheading; spurious truncated "Sandwiches"); r2 51/40 items FAIL + 1 option FP + spurious "Sandwiches"; r3 50/40 FAIL + 2 option FPs + spurious "Sandwiches".

- Dump decomposition of the overcount (r2/r3 vs corrected draft truth):
  (A) OPTION-ECHO ITEMS: one tile folds Paletas flavors into options (Agua opts=6 / Crema opts=6), another emits the flavors as standalone items (Uva/Piña/Yoghurt con Cajeta/Combinada, prices = parent's $20/$30) — both survive merge: +4.
  (B) TILE TWINS ESCAPING THE FOLD: same dish read by two tiles with conflicting price and section — PapaBoneless 600gr $192(null-sect) vs Papaboneless $189(Crispy Chicken); Waffle Cheese Sandwich 350gr $185(null) vs $158(S&H); Boneless Jr 200gr $132(Pollo Kids) vs $99(Crispy Chicken). twinFoldCandidate requires EQUAL section + EXACT name, so all escape. These are the trust-critical visible dupes.

  (D) TRUNCATED SECTION TITLE: a tile whose cut shows only "Sandwiches" emits it as section (full "Sandwiches & Hamburguesas" read by another tile) → spurious 3/3.
  (E) r1-only: S&H heading read by NO tile (items attributed to "Acompañadas de Papas" subheading) → missing required section + wrong mapping. Heading-read variance, no deterministic post-hoc fix (information never extracted). WATCH.

## Eval 066 — I9 deterministic fixes work, but item uniqueness remains red (1/3; ~$0.55)
- Date: 2026-07-14 | Landed: section-agnostic/near-name tile twin fold with equal non-null grams guard (`4fb8148`); `dropOptionEchoItems` (`1ae2aaf`); `remapTruncatedSectionTitles` (`30da619`); tile-chain wiring + integration test (`369e98b`). Local verification reported: `deno check extract.ts` PASS; analyze-menu suite 67/67 PASS; scorer module check PASS.
- Polloteria forced 2x2 x3: r1 ALL five dims PASS; r2 items FAIL at 47/40 while options/section_context/categories/grams PASS; r3 items FAIL at 46/40 while the other four dims PASS. Verifier v3 rejected 2/0/1 names, all mangled Ensaladas size-variant titles; it did not reject the residual non-dishes.
- The three I9 classes improved as designed: Paletas option-flavor standalone echoes are gone; eval-065's PapaBoneless/Waffle Cheese Sandwich/Boneless Jr cross-section twin pairs no longer survive as pairs; truncated `Sandwiches` no longer fails section_context. The remaining overcount is a different class, not evidence to widen the item band.
- PHOTO-ADJUDICATED residuals in the current r2/r3 dumps: duplicated Alitas size card; unprinted recombinations/double-field twins including `Boneless El Jr`, `Tender de Coliflor Vegetariano`, `Cheesy (300gr)` or `Boneless P (450gr)`, `Ensalada Verde (350gr) $70`, and `Nuggets de Coliflor`. The menu photo has only Boneless Jr, Boneless de Coliflor, Cheesy Bacon, Ensalada Verde 150gr/$52, and Nuggets; the extra names/fields combine neighboring cards. Paletas Agua/Crema are real tolerated content and are not this failure class.
- ROOT CAUSE: v3 verifies each candidate independently against one tile and asks only whether a real dish *corresponds* to its name. That can reject a pure invention, but cannot establish DISTINCTNESS across overlap tiles: both an accurate card and a wrong-price/grams/truncated twin can independently correspond to the same printed dish. The prompt already says two-dish word mashups are false; adding the same sentence again without source evidence would be guesswork. `When unsure, answer true` correctly protects real dishes but also makes this blind spot fail-open.
- INSTRUMENTATION GAP: `probe-tiles.ts` writes dumps only for scored failures and omits `result.raw_response`, even though that field already contains all four raw tile responses. Therefore current r1's `ALL PASS` output cannot be audited for hidden inventions (its on-disk r1 dump is stale at 20:14; current r2/r3 are 20:48), and r2/r3 cannot be traced back to their source tile/merge boundary. Next probe must always save final items + `raw_response`; this is harness-only and changes no model behavior.
- REPO HYGIENE: wrapped shell pastes caused `369e98b` to commit `supabase/.temp/{cli-latest,linked-project.json}` accidentally while omitting the user-approved Alitas fixture change. `scripts/fixtures/polloteria.expected.json` remains modified/uncommitted; `nohup.out` is untracked. Correct this before the next experiment; do not treat the Alitas pin as landed yet.
- NEXT: no fix selected. Eval 067 diagnostic trace APPROVED by the user 2026-07-14: repair the last commit at $0, make the probe persist every run's final output + existing raw tile responses, then run only until the first scored failure (one run ~$0.18; cap 3 runs/~$0.55). Use that trace to decide one behavior variable: per-tile whole-card verification, a narrowly targeted cross-tile ambiguity pass, or an explicit Phase-2 coupling ruling for the Alitas duplicate. A successful fix still requires a fresh x3 probe; the diagnostic run does not close Phase 1.

## Eval 067 — raw tile trace localizes every residual to overloaded tile 3 (~$0.18; stopped after first failure)
- Date: 2026-07-14 | Harness-only trace `d491a61` (repair/docs/oracle commit `126f4da`) saved final items + the existing four raw model responses and stopped on its first scored failure. Preflight: fixture self-check ALL PASS; `deno check` PASS; 68/68 tests; no `supabase/functions/analyze-menu` behavior diff after `369e98b`.
- Result: run 1 items FAIL at 49/40; options/section_context/categories/grams PASS. v3 verifier inputs mapped from raw response counts: tile 1 = 19 food, tile 2 = 17, tile 3 = 36, tile 4 = 10. Only tile 3 rejected anything: 1 mangled Ensalada, leaving 35 candidates. The other three tiles rejected 0. The old v3 design claim was "one image + a dozen names"; tile 3 violates that tractability assumption by 3x.
- Exact final-count decomposition (photo-adjudicated): 39 of the 40 expected food dishes are represented (one Ensalada missing) + 2 REAL tolerated Paletas cards + 4 PHASE-2 size-variant overrepresentations + 4 UNPRINTED phantoms = 49. Phase-2 class: `Grande` + separately emitted `Jumbo` = +1; one combined Alitas line from tile 1 plus three separately emitted Alitas sizes from tile 3 = 4 representations of one card, +3. Unprinted class, all sourced only from tile 3 and kept by v3: `Boneless Jr (150gr) $119` under Especialidades, `Crispy (300gr) $159`, `Ensalada Verde (350gr) $70`, `Nuggets de Coliflor (200gr) $99`.
- Merge evidence: I9b successfully collapses many other tile-3 field twins (`Papabones`, Tender, Boneless de Coliflor, Cheesy Bacon, Waffle/Papas/French Fries/Coleslaw/Kids variants) when their general guards have evidence. The four survivors are exactly the unsafe shapes: different-grams same-name (could be a real size pair), cross-card word recombination, or no compatible sibling. Broadening deterministic folds would delete legitimate Phase-2 variants elsewhere.
- ROOT CAUSE split: (1) Shape-A size cards are printed evidence but Phase 1's item scorer counts their pre-Phase-2 representations as distinct dishes; this needs a user phase/convention ruling, not a hidden merge rule. (2) Tile 3's 36-candidate verifier call is overloaded and fails open on all four unprinted recombinations; this is the next behavior hypothesis. A count-only gate is now PROVABLY insufficient: folding the four Phase-2 extras would yield 45/40 (numeric PASS) while all four unprinted phantoms remain and one real Ensalada is missing.
- USER APPROVED 2026-07-14: preserve phase isolation by treating the printed Grande/Jumbo and Alitas representations as Phase-2-owned until the mandatory Shape-A fold, while keeping every unprinted item release-blocking. Approved next one-variable probe: when a tile has more than 24 verifier candidates, split that list in menu order into batches of at most 12 against the same single tile image (only tile 3 expands from 1 verifier call to 3 in this trace; the observed-clean 10/17/19-candidate tiles stay unchanged). Estimated 4 extraction + 6 verifier calls ≈ $0.23/run; x3 ≈ $0.68 APPROVED. Do not add prompt text, relax the band, change geometry, or start the detector trigger. Require raw dumps + photo audit even if the numeric scorer says PASS.

## Eval 068 — batching helps, but the name-only contract still admits fake copies (~$0.23; stopped after first failure)
- Date: 2026-07-14 | Ruling/docs commit `b15d2b4`, batching implementation `7a880ed`, checkpoint-log commit `1ab93f5`. Preflight independently verified by the planner: formatting/check PASS; analyze-menu 70/70 PASS; probe-output 1/1 PASS; no prompt, geometry, merge, postprocess, or fixture change. The forced 2×2 probe launched once and stopped on its first scored failure as designed.
- Result: run 1 = items FAIL 46/40, options FAIL (one false-positive item), section_context FAIL (Boneless Barbecue→null); categories+grams PASS. Tile candidate loads were 19/17/34/10 food-or-tolerated items. Batching turned tile 3 into 12/12/10-name calls; it rejected only `Boneless El Jr(300gr)`, proving the overload fix can help but is not sufficient.
- PHOTO-ADJUDICATED final count: 39/40 real food cards represented (one Ensalada card missing) + 2 real tolerated Paletas cards + 1 Phase-2-owned duplicate Alitas representation + 4 visible unprinted copies = 46. The four visible fakes are `Boneless de Coliflor Vegetarianos (350gr) $150`, `Buffalo (350gr) $150`, `Ensalada Verde (350gr) $70`, and `Nuggets de Coliflor (200gr) $132`. Raw-tile audit found a fifth surviving fake, `Boneless Jr(200gr) $95` under Crispy Chicken: merge folded it into the real Pollo Kids Boneless Jr and its borrowed prose became the scored option false-positive. Therefore final-item count alone still hides fake source candidates.
- ROOT CAUSE: the remaining failures occur inside already-small batches (12 and 10), so reducing batch size cannot solve them. `VERIFY_PROMPT` explicitly says to ignore weight annotations and the API receives only `{index,name}`. Four survivors reuse a real base name while borrowing price/weight/section from neighboring cards; the fifth combines Nuggets with the separately printed Boneless de Coliflor. Independent existence verification has no accurate overlap copy beside the suspect, so `when unsure, true` keeps it. This is the distinctness blind spot documented in eval 066, now proven after removing the overload confounder.
- NEXT RECOMMENDATION — USER COST APPROVAL REQUIRED: no production change yet. Run a harness-only paired-card ambiguity replay against the archived Eval-068 candidates and reconstructed 2×2 tiles. Two tiny calls per replay compare each suspect with its accurate overlap copy; include distinct-real controls and the Phase-2 Alitas representation as keep-both controls. Verdict may drop A/B only when the images clearly prove an inferior duplicate or unprinted mashup; uncertainty keeps both. Run ×3, stop on first wrong verdict. Estimated ~$0.08/replay, hard max ~$0.24 total (6 two-image verifier calls, no extraction calls). If all five fakes are selected and every control is kept 3/3, then design the generic suspect-pair selector; if not, reject this architecture rather than weakening keep bias. Do not repeat v1/v2, add another extraction-prompt sentence, shrink batches again, or start detector work.
- USER APPROVED 2026-07-15: proceed with that harness-only paired-card replay under the ~$0.24 hard cap. Build and verify the replay harness first; no paid call until the planner reviews its tests/diff and relays the approval. Production extraction/verifier/merge behavior remains frozen.

## Eval 069 — paired ambiguity verifier REJECTED: 2/5 fakes caught, 3/3 controls kept (~$0.08; stopped on run-1 failure)
- Date: 2026-07-15 | Ruling/ledger commit `2079812`, harness `3a674bb`, exact-prompt correction `b8ab3c8`, launch docs `37683b4` + `f5e069c`. The first executor-sandbox launch failed before Deno/API because `/tmp` redirection was denied ($0); Santiago explicitly approved one normal-Terminal launch. It ran once, wrote `polloteria.paired-ambiguity-eval069-r1.actual.json`, used exactly 2 calls, failed, and stopped without runs 2–3 as designed.
- Result: correct fake drops = 2/5 (`Boneless de Coliflor Vegetarianos 350gr/$150`, `Boneless Jr 200gr/$95`); missed fake drops = 3/5 (`Buffalo 350gr/$150`, `Ensalada Verde 350gr/$70`, `Nuggets de Coliflor 200gr/$132`, all returned `neither`). All keep-both controls were correct: Phase-2 Alitas, distinct Boneless Barbecue/Buffalo sandwiches, and distinct Nuggets/Chicken-Little Kids cards. Therefore the keep bias is safe, but the architecture fails the fake-removal gate on its first replay.
- ROOT CAUSE: the three misses are internally coherent and visually plausible when reduced to a two-candidate choice. `Buffalo` looks like a distinct sandwich because it carries another printed sandwich's coherent prose; `Ensalada Verde 350gr/$70` looks like a legitimate size/price variant of the real 150gr/$52 side; `Nuggets de Coliflor` looks like a plausible separate vegetarian Kids card. The decisive evidence is spatial card ownership — their words/price/weight were borrowed from neighboring cards — not semantic similarity to one accurate counterpart. With no reliable card boundary, the required `when unsure, neither` rule correctly refuses deletion. Weakening that rule would recreate v1/v2's real-dish over-deletion.
- RULING FROM THE PREDECLARED GATE: reject the paired-candidate verifier architecture. Do not run replays 2–3, tune this prompt, build a generic pair selector, or weaken keep-on-uncertainty. Production remains unchanged and unprinted items remain Phase-1 release blockers.
- NEXT RECOMMENDATION — USER COST APPROVAL REQUIRED: move upstream and test density reduction rather than another semantic verifier. In a harness only, freeze the three observed-clean Eval-068 parent-tile outputs and replace only overloaded/fake-producing tile 3 with a nested 2×2 extraction using the existing geometry, verifier, merge, prompt, and fixture unchanged. This isolates one variable: less content per extraction image. Estimated 4 extraction + up to 4 verifier calls ≈ $0.24/run; x3 hard max ≈ $0.72, stopping on the first fake/missing-card failure. Require raw nested-tile traces and photo audit; success means zero unprinted source/final items while preserving real controls and scorer dimensions. Alternative: another spatial-verifier design (~$0.24 max) is cheaper but not recommended after the full-field/two-image pair failure; stopping horizontal support costs $0.

## Eval 070 — external research adopted ($0): spatial co-location enters Phase 1; probes re-sequenced
- Date: 2026-07-17 | Docs-only session, no API calls, no code change. Santiago commissioned the external deep-research report requested in the 2026-07-15 brief entry (archived: `docs/superpowers/horizontal-menus/2026-07-15-external-research-dense-menus.md`).
- Assessment: the report names our failure class (DoorDash "item↔attribute linkage" / relational VLM hallucination from tile-based image encoding), reports it OPEN (no published zero-recombination guarantee), and prescribes layout-first decomposition + spatial grounding as the production pattern. It independently re-derives eval-069's nested tile-3 probe as the cheapest decisive experiment, restates eval-067's count-band-masking proof, endorses keep-on-uncertainty, and confirms semantic verification cannot establish distinctness — the evals 063–069 rejections were correct process-of-elimination.
- New mechanism adopted: co-location verification — a candidate's name+price+grams must fall inside ONE region box from a dedicated layout-OCR pass (NOT VLM self-reported boxes, unreliable on dense small text). First architecture operating on the proven root-cause variable (spatial card ownership); all prior verifiers operated on semantics.
- USER RATIFIED 2026-07-17 (container ruling 8; sequencing only, live cost approved separately per probe): Eval 071 = harness-only co-location replay FIRST (one Mistral-OCR layout call ≈ cents, then $0 offline geometry iterations vs archived Eval-068/069 candidates; predeclared gate 5/5 fakes rejected + 3/3 controls kept → Phase-1 release gate; 4/5 → advisory; any control lost → reject). Mistral role = coordinates provider (supersedes the unconcluded 2026-06-23 extractor-swap framing).

## Eval 071 — co-location replay: 5/5 fakes killed on printed evidence, 8/9 keeps verified; the 1 "failure" is a true unprinted-field finding (~$0.004 total)
- Date: 2026-07-17 | Harness `ad3816d` + launch-line fix `39d49e7` + lockfile `8b32502`; executor parser fix (real API returns flat `{content, top_left_*, bottom_right_*, type}` blocks, not `text`/lines/words) `8b67bd8`, 9/9 tests. Run 1 spent the single OCR call ($0.004, `mistral-ocr-latest`, `include_blocks: true`) and cached the response (`polloteria.mistral-ocr-eval071.json`); parse mismatch made r1 verdicts vacuous. Run 2 (cache hit, $0): 162 line-level blocks with tight pixel boxes — dense-menu name lines carry name+grams+price in ONE printed block (e.g. `Ensalada Verde (150gr) $52`, `Boneless Jr(200gr) $132`).
- RESULT r2: fakes rejected 5/5; keeps held 8/9. Numeric gate line says FAIL, but the planner photo/dump audit reverses the reading:
  - All 5 fakes anchored to their real printed source blocks and were rejected on exact printed contradictions: coliflor 48 prints 300gr/$139 vs claims 350/$150; boneless-jr 114 prints $132 vs claim $95 ($95 printed NOWHERE); buffalo verified against 71 `Boneless Buffalo (300gr) $150` (grams contradict; other 12 anchors are prose/sauce lines like `· Ranch Buffalo`); ensalada-verde 109 prints 150gr/$52 vs claims 350/$70 — and the borrowed $70 sits at block 105 `Papas Sazonadas (300gr) $70` only 104px away, so ANY radius/adjacency design would have missed it: block-level separation is the load-bearing choice; nuggets-coliflor anchors expose the mashup (`Boneless de Coliflor` card 48 + `Nuggets…waffles` prose 121), no anchor verifies.
  - The single keep failure `alitas-phase2-b` is CORRECT print-truth: the printed card (block 35 `Alitas 6 PZ $129 / 12PZ $169 / 20PZ $269`) prints NO grams; `125gr/315gr/525gr` appear nowhere in the OCR — those grams are model-added transcription embellishment on a REAL card. The check refused to confirm an unprinted claim; my case fixture mis-derived expect=true from eval-069's keep-both pairing (which tested "don't delete the card", a different question).
- DESIGN CONSEQUENCE (proposal, needs user ruling): binary co-location conflates two outcomes. Needed 3-way verdict: VERIFIED (an anchor block prints every claimed field) / CONTRADICTED (no anchor verifies AND a PRICED anchor — block containing a $-price — prints a claimed field type with a different value → fake signal, reject) / UNVERIFIABLE (no verify, no priced-anchor contradiction → KEEP the card, flag/strip the unprinted field, never delete). Priced-anchor restriction is required because weak single-token name matches on prose blocks (e.g. `alitas` in the Cubeta description block 17, which contains `(300gr)`) would otherwise contradict real cards. Dry-checked against the r2 dump: all 5 fakes land CONTRADICTED, 8 keeps land VERIFIED, alitas-phase2-b lands UNVERIFIABLE — 14/14 under the refined semantics.
- PENDING USER RULINGS: (a) ORACLE-CHANGE: alitas-phase2-b expectation true→UNVERIFIABLE-keep; (b) adopt the 3-way verdict semantics for the gate (VERIFIED/UNVERIFIABLE=keep, CONTRADICTED=reject). Both $0 to implement and re-run (cached OCR). No production change proposed yet — a full-gate pass on the replay is the promotion trigger per ruling 8.

## Eval 071 CLOSE — 3-way co-location passes the replay gate 14/14 (total spend $0.004; all reruns $0)
- Date: 2026-07-17 | Rulings A+B approved by user → ruling 9 (`cad2a24`). v2 3-way verdicts (`7e65469`) scored 13/14: alitas-phase2-b was CONTRADICTED by block 17 — the Cubeta Pollotera combo DESCRIPTION, which mentions alitas, contains `(300gr)`, and ends with a price, defeating the priced-line proxy for "prose cannot contradict". v3 mention-guard (`63fb8fc`): an anchor must be a line ABOUT the dish — ≥50% of the block's own name-like tokens must belong to the candidate's name; prose mentions are excluded for BOTH verification and contradiction. 14 unit tests.
- RESULT r4: fakes contradicted 5/5; keeps not deleted 9/9; expectations met 14/14 — FULL PASS at rulings-8/9 release-gate tier ON THE REPLAY. Evidence integrity: every verdict anchors to planner-verified printed blocks (48/35/69/71/109/114/116/118); all five fakes rejected on printed contradictions only (boneless-jr's claimed $95 is printed nowhere; ensalada-verde's borrowed $70 lives 104px away on Papas Sazonadas and block-level separation caught it); alitas lands unverifiable-keep (card prints no grams — user visually confirmed).
- SCOPE CAVEATS (why this is not yet production promotion): one fixture (Polloteria), one OCR snapshot; co-location addresses PRECISION only — the missing-Ensalada recall gap and tile-3 overload remain Eval 072's subject; cross-menu false-contradiction risk (stylized fonts, accents, other layouts) untested — production wiring proposal comes after Eval 072 with Nikkori/Bistro/GuestHouse spot-probes in the design.
- Production sketch parked for that proposal: edge-side Mistral OCR call only on dense/tile scans (~+$0.004/scan, ~3% of a dense scan's GPT cost), post-merge check, CONTRADICTED→drop, UNVERIFIABLE→keep + strip unprinted grams (F4 convention), fail-open if the OCR API errors.
- NEXT per ruling 8 order: Eval 072 — nested tile-3 density probe, frozen Eval-068 parent tiles, tile 3 → nested 2×2 at ≥25% overlap (~$0.24/run, ×3 hard cap ≈ $0.72, stop on first failure) — awaiting live-cost approval.

## Eval 072 — nested tile-3 REJECTED standalone (r1 hard fail ~$0.22); $0 co-location cross-audits reshape the plan
- Date: 2026-07-17 | Harness `1f90f3b` (frozen parents 0/1/3 replay the eval-068 r1 archive through `frozenTileResult`; only parent tile 2 re-extracted live as nested 2×2, 25%-of-parent overlap; production `runGroupedExtraction` via injected extract; 4 new tests, full suite 21/21; planner diff-reviewed before launch). Run 1: nested sub food counts 11/5/28/3, tile-2 nested merge = 60 items; scored FAIL items 61/40 + section_context (spurious SALSAS/SAZONADORES) + categories + grams → stopped per design. Total spend ~$0.22 of the $0.66 cap.
- Failure decomposition (photo/baseline diff): (a) the zoom made the salsa/seasoning PANEL legible → 11 pseudo-items (BBQ, Spicy Garlic, Lemon Pepper…, cat=other, mostly priceless) — printed text, so no printed-evidence verifier can kill them: they are a section/category echo class, not phantoms; (b) cross-source name twins escape merge (frozen `El Tenderete` $546 + nested `El Tendedero` $468 both survive; `Papa Sazonada`/`Papas Sazonadas`); (c) the nested read MINTED 3 new grams-recombination phantoms (`Tender/Gyro/Fresh (500gr)` vs printed 350/300/300). GAIN evidence: nested zoom fixed the historic name misreads (real `El Tendedero`, `Megacharola Boneless`, `Cubeta Pollotera` read for the first time) and RECOVERED the missing Ensalada card (`Ensalada Boneless M (150gr) $158` read correctly). Density reduction improves legibility but is generatively unsafe standalone.
- $0 CROSS-AUDIT (planner python port of the eval-071 checker vs cached OCR): nested-r1 62 items → 42 verified / 7 contradicted / 13 unverifiable — all 7 contradicted are true phantoms incl. the 3 new mints. Eval-068 BASELINE 46 items → 28 verified / 8 contradicted / 10 unverifiable: the 8 = the 5 known fakes + `Tender (150gr)` (printed 350gr), `Gyro (350gr)` (printed 300gr), `Dedos de Queso $119` (OCR block prints $199).
- POLARITY REFINEMENT REQUIRED (from the baseline audit): blanket CONTRADICTED→drop would delete REAL cards whose only representation carries one misread field (Tender, Gyro — the card is real, the grams are wrong; and Dedos is a GPT-vs-OCR disagreement needing photo adjudication). Correct production polarity: contradicted WITH a verified sibling on the same anchor block → drop (fake twin); contradicted ALONE on its anchor → keep the card, repair/flag the conflicting field from the printed block; unverifiable → keep (+ twin-fold candidate when a verified near-name sibling shares the anchor, e.g. El Tenderete→El Tendedero).
- Sibling-aware simulation on the baseline: 5 fakes drop (each has a verified sibling) → 41/40 items (band pass), Tender/Gyro grams repairable from print, Dedos flagged. Remaining known gaps: the one missing Ensalada card (recall — nested proved it's readable; a cheaper lever now exists: OCR priced-line completeness check, DoorDash guardrail (b) made deterministic by having blocks), and the salsa-panel echo class (deterministic section/category rule candidate, only fires on cat=other/priceless echoes — not touched by this eval).
- VERDICT: nested-tile architecture REJECTED as a standalone fix (do not iterate its geometry). The winning direction is production 2×2 + post-merge OCR co-location stage with sibling-aware polarity. PENDING USER: (1) photo-adjudicate Dedos de Queso printed price ($119 vs $199); (2) approve the production co-location stage design spec (edge fn, dense/tile path only, fail-open, ~+$0.004/scan) as the next work item.
- RESOLVED 2026-07-17 (same day): (1) user adjudication — the card prints `Dedos De Queso (200gr) $199`; GPT-4o misread $119, the OCR was correct (first reader-disagreement data point, favors OCR; repair still deferred to v2). (2) Spec green-lit and written: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-17-production-colocation-stage-design.md` (ruling 10). Next: executor implementation task block (unit tests + $0 replays; nikkori OCR call ~$0.004 needs approval in-block; live ×3 ~$0.72 approval at launch time).

## Eval 075 — printed-name existence tier: perfect separation on Polloteria, and a mandatory menu-level readability gate ($0)
- Date: 2026-07-17 | Planner-run analysis (python, archived dumps + cached OCR; user pre-approved). Ground truth = the user-corrected draft `PolloteriaMenu.png.draft.json` (40 real cards). Draft revealed the invention set is larger than eval-074's 4: `Sour Cream Cheese Fries` (not a card; Bacon Cheese Fries corruption) and `Ensalada de Pollo a la Plancha` (minted from the Gyro card's description prose; STABLE across all runs, has ridden inside the count band since the beginning) are also unprinted.
- Discriminator: for unanchored items, best per-block similarity of significant name tokens (≥3 chars, non-numeric/unit; edit-distance allowance scaled by token length, ≤ len/3 capped at 3; score = matched / max(candidate,block token counts), long prose self-penalizes). Result on Polloteria (both eval-068 and eval-074 dumps): ALL real-but-misread names score 1.00 (TortiBoneless→TostiBoneless, Cabeza→Cubeta, El Tenderazo→El Tendedero, Megachorla→Megacharola, Ensalada Boneless mutations); ALL 6 known inventions score ≤0.67 — clean gap, threshold 0.75.
- CRITICAL COUNTER-EVIDENCE: on nikkori, 42–48 of ~50 REAL rolls are unanchored and score 0.00–0.07 (v1: priced-lines-only comparison fails because nikkori prints names and prices in separate blocks; v2: even against all blocks, the OCR simply does not render nikkori's roll names as matchable text). A global existence-drop rule would delete essentially the whole nikkori menu. Therefore the tier REQUIRES a menu-level readability gate: activate existence-based dropping ONLY when the OCR demonstrably reads this menu's names — anchored-fraction of non-drink items ≥50% (polloteria ≈0.77 → active; nikkori ≈0.12 → inactive, 0 drops ×3). Structural, self-calibrating, worldwide-safe: deletion authority is granted per menu only where the evidence source proves itself.
- Simulated combined pipeline (co-location v1 + existence tier): eval-068 46 → 4 sibling drops → 2 existence drops → **40 exact**; eval-074 47 → 2 sibling drops (live) → 6 existence drops → 39–41 band. Zero real-card losses anywhere; nikkori untouched.
- NEXT: user approval to amend the spec (existence tier v1.1: unanchored + best-line-sim <0.75 + menu readability ≥0.5 anchored → drop, logged; else keep), implement + $0 replays, then spend the remaining ~$0.48 on live ×2 of the full stack.

## Eval 078 — ×3 gate attempt: r1 numerically ALL PASS (first ever) but FAILS the ruling-6 audit; r2 scored-fail; description-minted family + tile-OCR blindness are the residuals (~$0.48 of $0.72; stopped at r2)

Scorer ruling: Papa Sazonada-class misreads are tolerable → expectation lookup gains a small edit tolerance (scorer-side only, next planner specs it; invention audit unaffected). SAME-DAY ADDENDUM — root cause + permanent fix: macOS reaps /private/tmp nightly (`/usr/libexec/tmp_cleaner` at 00:00; `dirhelper` at 03:35 with CLEAN_FILES_OLDER_THAN_DAYS=3) and wipes it on reboot; files touched within 3 days survived (docs/, scripts/) while stale ones died (src/, supabase/, the `.git` link), which is exactly the observed half-wipe.
