## Eval 079 — tile-OCR hypothesis CONFIRMED + HARNESS CUTTER BUG found (sips flush-bottom-left no-op) + per-tile OCR architecture REJECTED on measurement; full-photo 2048/q95 OCR wins (~$0.09, all planner-run offline)
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Planner-run diagnostic (script committed: `scripts/probe-tile-ocr-eval079.ts`; caches durable in `~/Downloads/MenusTesting/`: `polloteria.mistral-ocr-tiles-r{1,2,3}.json`, `polloteria.mistral-ocr-tile3-true.json`, `polloteria.mistral-ocr-2048q95.json`). No production/harness code changed; no GPT calls. Method: regenerate the probe tiles, OCR ×3 rounds, replay the archived eval-077r1/078r1/078r2 dumps through the PRODUCTION `applyColocation`/`judgeItem` (imported, not ported) against tile-union vs full-photo blocks — 3 independent live samples per Santiago's ×3 preference. Mistral OCR fully deterministic: identical block signatures ×3 on every input tested.
- **HYPOTHESIS CONFIRMED (eval-078's tile-cut theory), mechanism refined.** Live per-tile OCR mints edge-cut artifacts: (a) tile 1's right border cuts the Pollo Kids descriptions → merged fragment blocks `"**Nuggets (200gr)**\nNuggets de pollo"` (4 name-like tokens) that pass the mention-guard for BOTH inventions — `Ensalada de Pollo` anchors via de+pollo (2/3 tokens, coverage 4/8) → `anchored=true` → the v1.1 existence tier never judges it → kept in ALL 3 dumps; (b) tile 4's top border cuts the Nuggets card → OCR merges leftover name-line+description into one PRICED block (`$85` — misread of printed $89) → `Nuggets de Pollo` lands contradicted-alone → flagged+kept (exactly live r2). Against full-photo OCR the same prose is one whole block (coverage 4/9 <0.5, no anchor) → both inventions existence-drop in all 3 dumps. Zero real-card differences between sources otherwise (verified counts identical 28/30/27).
- **HARNESS BUG (root cause of a deeper distortion):** `sips --cropOffset Y X -c H W` silently returns the FULL image when X=0 AND Y+H == imageHeight (flush-bottom left-aligned crop; width irrelevant — fails at 1363 too; y±1 or h-1 works; flush-bottom+x=910 works). A 2×2 grid's tile 3 hits this combo on EVERY menu by construction (originX=0, 0.4h+0.6h=h). Proof: harness "tile 3" OCR = 162 blocks, page 2274×1572, block coords byte-identical to the full-photo cache (Gyro desc (612,788)-(1065,858) in both). Affected scripts (same sips pattern): `probe-tiles.ts`, `eval-027-live.ts` cutTiles (its "pixel-identical, tile-4 TIFF hash" verification checked the one tile that works), `extract-draft.ts`, `probe-paired-ambiguity.ts`, `probe-nested-tile-eval072.ts`. Production CLIENT is unaffected (expo-image-manipulator, no sips) — the harness has NOT been production-mirror on tile 3.
- **Re-contextualizes the ledger:** every polloteria probe since eval 061 sent tiles 1,2,4 + the FULL PHOTO as "tile 3" — i.e. the landscape-crushed full-page read (eval-060's diagnosed failure mode) rode inside every "tile" run. Explains: tile-3 candidate overloads (36 in eval 067 = the whole menu), tile-3-sourced phantoms/recombinations (crushed-read inventions), the never-resolved Ensaladas mangling + missing-Ensalada recall gap — the tile-3-EXCLUSIVE region (left 40%, bottom 40%) contains the Ensaladas cards and was NEVER properly imaged in the harness (true tile 3, cut via rotate-180 workaround, shows all 3 Ensaladas cards crisply legible: Boneless M $158/G $179, Aguacate Chicken M $178/G $199, Pollotera M $168/G $189). Also explains eval-072's nested-tile gains (its "nested tile 3" = first real crops of that region). Nikkori harness gates since runtime cutting (2026-07-10) also ran tile3=full-photo; portrait full-photo preserves text (shortest side = width) AND device runs (true client tiles) passed independently, so closed gate DECISIONS stand — but the Phase-4 combined gate must run on a fixed cutter.
- **Mitigation trials on the corrected union (true tile 3), all $0 replays ×3 dumps:** (a) fixed union, no mitigation: inventions STILL survive (tile-1/4 fragments) AND the existence tier false-drops 9–11 REAL cards per dump — tile 2 (top-right) deterministically OCRs as ONE mega-block (its exclusive region = the Sides column: Papas Sazonadas, French Fries, Dedos de Queso… lose all evidence) while global readability stays 0.64–0.68 → tier active → massacre. (b) + interior-edge block exclusion (coords rescaled via pages[].dimensions): inventions die but the Sides massacre persists. Per-tile OCR is structurally fragile: any tile can mega-block and doom its exclusive region; a global readability gate cannot protect one starved region. (c) pad probe: a 40px white border FIXES tile 2 (1→55 line blocks incl. `Papas Sazonadas (300gr) $70`); JPEG-q95 and 1.5× upscale do NOT — but production padding needs edge-side image codecs (new dep, CPU risk) or client geometry changes + pad-aware exclusion: complexity tower, unmeasurable worldwide risk. REJECT per-tile OCR as the evidence source.
- **WINNER — full-photo OCR of a 2048/q95 JPEG copy (the exact ticket-#3 fallback transform, ~746KB):** deterministic 162 blocks ×3 rounds; across all 3 dumps drops EXACTLY the audited inventions and nothing else — 077r1 43→41 (Ensalada de Pollo + Nuggets de Pollo), 078r1 40→39 (Ensalada de Pollo), 078r2 44→42 (both; Papa Sazonada kept bestSim 1.00, Papas Sazonadas + Grande verified); readability 0.82–0.88; flags = known wrong-field class only. Also 4× cheaper than per-tile ($0.004 vs $0.016/scan).
- **PENDING USER RULING (proposed as ruling 15 — spec amendment v1.3):** colocationStage's evidence source becomes ONE Mistral OCR call on a compressed copy of the ORIGINAL photo, carried in the extract-pages request as a new optional per-dense-page field (client reuses the existing 2048/q95 compressImage transform; ~+1MB b64/request; field absent → stage skipped fail-open). Per-tile OCR path deleted. Matcher/polarity/v1.1/v1.2 UNCHANGED (already validated against exactly this input shape, incl. the eval-068 46→40 and nikkori 0-drops×3 replay gates which have always used full-photo caches). Plus: harness cutter fix (all 5 scripts; dims assertion + rotate-180 fallback for the flush-bottom-left combo) — after which the ×3 gate finally measures TRUE production geometry, with a real chance the missing-Ensalada recall gap closes (the region is legible). Costs this eval: 21 OCR page calls ≈ $0.084. Next after ruling: executor blocks (1) cutter fix $0, (2) v1.3 protocol+stage TDD $0 (replays: old two gates byte-identical + 3 new dump gates 41/39/42 exact), (3) ruling-14 scorer tolerance TDD $0; then fresh live ×3 ≈ $0.73 on the fixed harness (stop-on-first-failure, ruling-6 photo audit per run).
- RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (same day): user APPROVED ruling 15 (spec amendment v1.3 written into the spec; ROADMAP updated) plus the harness cutter fix. Planner handed Santiago three sequential executor blocks (cutter fix → v1.3 protocol/stage with $0 replay gates [eval-068 46→40 same 6; nikkori eval027 r1-r3 0 drops; NEW 077r1 43→41, 078r1 40→39, 078r2 44→42 exact-named] → ruling-14 scorer lookup tolerance) and the prior-art research prompt (`docs/superpowers/horizontal-menus/2026-07-18-external-research-prompt-prior-art.md`). Live ×3 (~$0.73) pre-approved, to be restated at launch after all gates green.

## Eval 080 — prior-art deep-research review #2 ($0): nobody has eliminated the recombination class; our stack matches published SOTA minus the human loop; new levers parked with evidence triggers
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Docs-only planner review of the external report answering `2026-07-18-external-research-prompt-prior-art.md` (delivered same day; the uploaded prompt was partially garbled in transfer — the researcher reconstructed items 3–6 correctly; the archived prompt file is clean).
- HEADLINE: no company/product/API/paper eliminates or even MEASURES cross-card field recombination as a class; production systems bound it with layout-first segmentation + post-hoc provenance + confidence-gated HUMAN review (DoorDash 2025: LightGBM guardrail classifier routes low-trust transcriptions to humans; no absolute error rates published; "none [of the newer models] significantly outperforms the others"). Menu digitization is still sold as a human-in-the-loop service, not an API with an SLA. No vendor publishes verifiable extraction guarantees.
- CONFIRMATIONS of our ledger: (1) semantic-verifier rejection (evals 063–069) re-validated — the report's anti-embedding warning uses our exact class ("Ensalada de Pollo"≈"Ensalada Pollotera" are embedding neighbors; a semantic matcher would VALIDATE the fabrication); its prescription — "stricter, not smarter: every name token inside a SINGLE block, price/weight from that same block" — is literally our v3 mention-guard + verified semantics. (2) "Never crop before OCR; run OCR once on the full image and slice geometrically" = ruling 15/v1.3, adopted from our own eval-079 measurements one day before the report arrived; the report found NO prior documentation of tile-OCR mega-blocks/flush-crop collapse/white-margin recovery — eval-079's findings are original. (3) Count-based metrics hiding binding errors is confirmed by literature omission (ruling-6 photo audits ahead of published practice; nobody reports binding errors as a class — no benchmark exists). (4) olmOCR publishes GPT-4o's dense-page omit-and-rewrite behavior — our phantom class is model-architectural in our exact model (prompt-freeze discipline vindicated; bake-off stays relevant, expectations tempered by DoorDash's "none outperforms"). (5) VLM self-reported boxes remain broken in 2026 models (ParseBench: Gemini 3 Flash 43.2%; ViDoRe V3 wrong-table groundings) — permanently closes that door.
- STALE report claim corrected by $0 cache check: we are ALREADY on Mistral OCR 4 (`mistral-ocr-latest`; all caches postdate the 2026-06-23 release; blocks carry `type`, pages carry `confidence_scores`). Unused free signals found: block `type` = clean printed section-header list (polloteria titles = exactly the 12 headers/panels — future section_context/Phase-2 aid); `confidence_scores` present but null (opt-in param — verify in official docs if ever needed).
- NEW LEVERS PARKED (evidence-triggered, nothing changes Blocks 2–3 or the pending live ×3): (a) v2 field-repair design gains localized re-read — re-OCR only the anchor region WITH margin (eval-079 pad finding makes this reliable; GutenOCR-style region-as-parameter) to adjudicate GPT-vs-OCR disagreements (Dedos class); (b) CONTINGENCY if true-tile-3 geometry mints stack-evading phantom classes in the ×3: olmOCR document-anchoring — inject full-photo OCR text into the tile extraction prompt (published hallucination reduction; single-variable probe; P1 non-local-side-effect discipline applies); (c) post-release: our verdict distribution (verified fraction + readability) is a FREE DoorDash-style per-scan trust score → powers the deferred retake-prompt/confidence-flag features; (d) bake-off track additions: PaddleOCR-VL/PP-DocLayoutV3 layout-first (menu card ≠ layout class — needs a card-detector fine-tune; guarantee only as strong as segmentation), GutenOCR conditional detection (open weights, F1 0.877, zero menu-domain eval), copy-only constrained decoding over the OCR lattice (UNCLAIMED ground per the report — the only mechanism making name-minting impossible rather than detectable; requires self-hosted model + grammar engine; composes with layout-first). (e) Optional visibility play: we hold labeled cases of all three recombination sub-types — publishing the first binding-error benchmark is a product decision, no engineering urgency.
- Ironclad (May 2026) failure list for lexical grounding (multi-instance ambiguity = our same-name-twin problem; normalization/hyphenation/semantic-equivalence defeats) endorses sibling-aware polarity and warns against ADDING fuzz — matcher threshold changes remain sweep-gated (the 5→4 protocol).

## Eval 083 — r1 scorer-green REFUSED by planner audit: FIRST STACK FALSE DELETION found (stable ×2) — contradiction anchor chosen by block order, not ownership; plus an Agua/Aguacate fixture-key collision (~$0.48; stopped r2)
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Retag `be7dca4`. r1: scorer ALL PASS. r2: options FAIL (1 FP). Identical colocation lines both runs: drop Cheesey Bacon (blk 7), flag Nashville (blk 81), drop Waffle/C&W 300gr twins (blk 83 — correct class).
- **DEFECT 1 (release-blocking; r1 NOT green):** `Cheesey Bacon (300gr)` — a REAL printed S&H card ($155) — was deleted in BOTH runs. Chain: tiles read $155/$161/$155 → merge kept a $161 flake copy → own line contradicts (161≠155) AND blk 7 `Bacon Cheese Fries (300gr) $139` cross-anchors (cheesey~cheese + bacon = 2/2 candidate tokens; block coverage 2/3 ≥0.5) and also contradicts → judgeItem returns the FIRST contradicting priced anchor in BLOCK-INDEX order = blk 7 (7 < ~79) → blk 7 verifies its true owner (Bacon Cheese Fries) → sibling-drop fired on a non-duplicate. Violates the eval-063 cardinal ruling (field flakes acceptable; deleting real dishes never). The count band hid it — the ruling-6 audit protocol caught it (vindicated again). Root cause: polarity v1's "same-anchor verified sibling = corrupted duplicate" assumes the reported anchor is the item's OWN card line; block ordering broke that ownership assumption for two real dishes sharing name words.
- **FIX (Block 6, $0):** judgeItem ranks anchors by ABOUTNESS — matched-candidate-token fraction desc, then block-coverage desc, then index — and reports the best-ranked contradicting anchor. Cheesey Bacon's best anchor becomes its own line (1.0 coverage vs blk 7's 0.67) which verifies nobody → contradicted-alone → flag+keep (the designed behavior for the field-flake class, exactly like Nashville $159-vs-print-$155 this run). Classic fakes unaffected: each fake's best anchor IS the real card's line that verifies the sibling (Ensalada Verde/Buffalo/coliflor sweeps must confirm). Gates: all six replay gates byte-identical + new two-real-dishes unit regression.
- **DEFECT 2 (fixture, ORACLE approval needed):** unchecked pin `"Agua"` consumes `Aguacate Chicken M` whenever item order puts it first (r2: Aguacate idx 31 < Paletas Agua 39 → Paletas Agua unconsumed → flavor-options FP; r1 order was lucky 29<33). Fix: keys `"Agua"`→`"Paletas Heladas Agua"`, `"Crema"`→`"Paletas Heladas Crema"` (Cabeza/El-Tender disambiguation precedent).
- Otherwise both runs clean: zero unprinted names, Queso/Shape-A/punctuation tolerances all worked, S&H twins correctly dropped. Budget: ~$0.48 spent; attempt over. After Block 6 + fixture fix: fresh ×3 (standing approval).
- APPROVALS 2026-07-18 (ruling 19): (a) anchor-ownership ranking fix approved WITH the explicit condition that it stays fully general (structural token math only, no menu-specific values — rule 7 restated by the user: the OCR stage must work on any menu type, never be Polloteria-specific); (b) fixture keys must be full multi-word phrases, never lone confusable words ("Agua"/"Crema" could read as drink/ingredient/extra) — implemented as "Paletas Heladas Agua"/"Paletas Heladas Crema" (the emitted-name substring; the user's literal "Paletas Agua helada" word order wouldn't substring-match, intent preserved, flagged for veto).
- BLOCK 6 LANDED + CLEARED 2026-07-18: `19c354a` — judgeItem contradiction scan iterates anchors ranked by ownership (matched-fraction desc, block-coverage desc, index asc; structural math only per ruling 19); multi-word Paletas pin keys. Planner real-evidence validation vs the cached 2048q95 OCR: Cheesey Bacon $161 → contradicted on its OWN line blk 79 → KEPT+flagged even beside verified Bacon Cheese Fries; classic fake Ensalada Verde 350/$70 still drops (blk 109 sibling). All 7 replay gates byte-identical; suites 122/122. NEXT: eval 084 fresh ×3 (standing approval ~$0.73).

## Eval 086 — 1f step B1 detector-only probe: suffix FIRES on the dense menu (Polloteria ✓) but OVER-TRIGGERS — all 3 wide menus dense=true, Bistro is a false positive (~$0.45)
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Commit `73e99da` (1f edge core: `LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX` + `landscape` flag threaded runExtraction/extractWithRetry/runPagedExtraction; `photoDims()`→`isLandscape` w>h routing; production index.ts/client wiring deferred). Probe: `scripts/probe-detector.ts`, phase-1 ONLY, real client-mirror dims passed, `PROBE_RUNS=3` × {Polloteria, Bistro, GuestHouse}, detached PID 62391, 9 GPT-4o calls, all completed naturally.
- RESULT (temp=0 + seed; all 3/3 identical per menu — deterministic): **Polloteria DENSE-SIGNAL pages=[0] 3/3** (dims 2274×1572) ✅ — the eval-060 landscape blind spot is broken; the detector now fires on the dense wide menu. **Bistro DENSE-SIGNAL 3/3** (2384×1844) ❌ — must stay dense=false (ruling 21: bistro would pay ~5×/scan). **Guest House DENSE-SIGNAL 3/3** (2606×1580) — record-only, either verdict acceptable.
- GATE VERDICT: **FAIL** (Bistro non-regression). The suffix over-triggers — it flips EVERY landscape photo to dense, not just genuinely-dense ones. Root-cause hypothesis: the approved wording primes size→dense ("scaled down… packed text can look small… if you cannot clearly read every item → set dense=true"), which the model applies to any wide image, overriding the base EXTRACT_PROMPT's packing-based density criterion. This is exactly the FP risk ruling 21 pre-flagged.
- NEXT (ruling 21's ONE authorized-in-principle reword round, ~$0.15): reframe the suffix to base density on item PACKING/CROWDING, not photo width, with an explicit "spacious / few-item wide menu = dense=false" anchor. Plan: reword → directional ×1-each re-probe (~$0.15) to check it separates Bistro (→ not dense) from Polloteria (→ stays dense); if it separates cleanly → ×3 gate (~$0.45); if not → escalate to Santiago with the fallback ladder (detail:"high" on the single-photo landscape phase-1 call → deterministic force-tile with a density proxy). The wording change needs Santiago's sign-off (he approved the first sentence verbatim; it is guarded by a verbatim pin test).
- Cost: ~$0.45 (9 phase-1 calls). No tracked code changed by the probe; `probe-b1-1f.log` is untracked in the worktree.

## Eval 087 — 1f reworded suffix directional re-probe: the "packing-not-width" wording OVER-corrects — all 3 wide menus dense=false, Polloteria LOST; prompt cannot separate dense from non-dense wide menus (~$0.15)
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Commit `14b0304` (reworded `LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX`, verbatim-pinned, planner-verified EXACT). Probe: `probe-detector.ts`, `PROBE_RUNS=1` × {Polloteria, Bistro, GuestHouse} (3 phase-1 calls). First launch mangled the redirect filename (`> probe- b1-reword.log` split → 0-byte `probe-`, no usable output, ~$0–0.05 ambiguous — at most 1 in-flight Polloteria call); clean relaunch with a hyphen-free log name, PID 87335, all 3 completed.
- RESULT (×1 each; temp=0 + seed): **Polloteria normal dense=false, items=44 ❌** (must be dense); **Bistro normal dense=false, items=26 ✓**; **GuestHouse normal dense=false, items=49** (record-only).
- DIAGNOSIS — the two wordings BRACKET the model without separating the menus: original suffix (eval 086: "scaled down / text small / can't read → dense") = strong push → ALL 3 dense=true (Polloteria ✓, Bistro ✗); reworded suffix ("base density on packing, not width") = weak push → ALL 3 dense=false (Bistro ✓, Polloteria ✗). GPT-4o perceives Polloteria and Bistro as SIMILARLY dense on the 768-shortest-side-rescaled wide image, so the suffix is a global dense dial, not a per-menu discriminator (consistent with the roadmap's core finding that the rescale crushes the density signal). Polloteria's whole read = 44 items (a mangled undercount — the known blind spot) yet the model still self-reports "not dense": overconfident on the crushed image. Items/megapixel: Polloteria 12.3, Bistro 5.9, GuestHouse 11.9.
- CONCLUSION: ruling-21's ONE reword round is spent and prompt-based dense detection cannot reliably separate dense from non-dense wide menus. ESCALATED to Santiago (2026-07-18). Options presented: (1) RECOMMENDED — deterministic tile-all-horizontal (in `runPagedExtraction`, any landscape page → `needs_crops` with NO model call; delete `LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX`; reuses the eval-085 photoDims plumbing; robust, ~$0.20/scan extra only on the rare simple wide menu; the original suffix already ≈ this); (2) deterministic items-per-image-area proxy (threshold ~9 items/MP separates these 3, but magic-threshold generalization risk AND fails on the densest menus, whose whole read UNDER-counts → low items/MP → wrongly not-dense); (3) a third middle-strength reword (likely futile — menus appear inseparable by prompt; beyond ruling 21's one round).
- Cost: ~$0.15 (3 calls) + ≤$0.05 from the mangled attempt.

## Eval 088 — text-size hypothesis probe: REFUTED — no text-size floor separates dense from non-dense wide menus; Bistro sits BETWEEN the two dense menus (~$0.03)
- Date: 2026-07-18 | Probe `fbc5f1c` (scripts/probe-textsize-eval088.ts + 5 unit tests; executor-built TDD, planner-designed; Santiago approved ~$0.03). 7 new Mistral OCR captures (bistro, guest-house, brasero, brasero-two, casa-nostra, el-marcos, mochomos @2048/q95 — durable caches in `~/Downloads/MenusTesting/*.mistral-ocr-2048q95.json`) + 2 cache reuses (polloteria, nikkori; both matched the planner's pre-computed anchors exactly, validating the metric implementation at $0). Metric: median per-line OCR block height × (768 / image shortest side) = printed text height as GPT-4o actually sees it after its rescale.
- HYPOTHESIS (predeclared): a menu is dense ⇔ its rescaled per-line text height falls below a legibility floor. Predeclared gate: Bistro must clear Polloteria's 9.2px with ≥1.5× margin (≈14px+), else fall back to deterministic tile-all-horizontal.
- RESULT (9 menus, medians in px-after-rescale): DENSE = polloteria 9.2, nikkori 12.2. NOT-DENSE = **bistro 10.9 — BETWEEN the two dense menus**, guest-house 12.1 (record-only, ≈nikkori), verticals 13.9–18.0 (brasero 14.2, brasero-two 14.5, casa-nostra 14.4, el-marcos 13.9, mochomos 18.0). No floor exists at ANY statistic: p25 also interleaves (polloteria 8.1, bistro 10.2, guest-house 11.1, nikkori 12.2), and p75 is INVERTED (dense 17.4/16.9 vs not-dense-wide 14.5/13.6). Landscape gap polloteria→bistro = 1.18×, far under the predeclared margin. GATE: FAIL — hypothesis refuted.
- INTERPRETATION: the eval-086/087 model verdicts were honest, not dumb — Polloteria and Bistro genuinely occupy near-identical post-rescale text sizes. Bistro reads fine at 10.9px because it carries ~26 well-spaced items; Polloteria drowns at 9.2px with ~55 packed ones. "Dense" is a JOINT property of text size AND content volume AND packing. Three independent cheap single-signal detectors are now dead on the same menu pair: model self-report (evals 086–087, brackets without separating), items/MP (eval 087 analysis, fails on under-read), physical text size (this eval, interleaved). The failure is the detection problem's, not any particular detector's — cheap single-signal dense detection on wide menus is CLOSED as a class.
- Residual signal observed but recommended AGAINST: OCR blocks per megapixel (dense 55.9/68.3 vs not-dense 18.4–35.0; the boundary gap is 1.6× between el-marcos 35.0 and polloteria 55.9). Rejected by the planner as a third post-hoc fit to the same 9 data points — N=2 dense samples, content-volume proxy with ruling-7 fragility (a legitimately busy-but-readable menu would false-tile; an under-OCR'd dense menu could false-pass), symmetric failure directions. Presented to Santiago for completeness only.
- RECOMMENDATION (the predeclared fallback branch): deterministic tile-all-horizontal — any landscape page → `needs_crops` with NO model call; delete `LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX` (both wordings dead); keep the photo_dims plumbing (`73e99da`/`14b0304` infra minus the suffix). Cost truth: dense wide scans get CHEAPER/faster (~$0.29→~$0.24 — the discarded whole read is skipped); simple wide menus pay ~$0.24 vs ~$0.05, rare per Santiago's own observation that most wide menus are dense, and partially offset by the dense-side saving. The one real risk to probe BEFORE deploy: spacious wide menus have never ridden the tile path — bistro-through-tiles quality is unprobed.
- PENDING SANTIAGO RULING: adopt tile-all-horizontal (reverses the 2026-07-18 "keep discriminating" cost ruling — now evidence-backed by three independent dead ends) vs pursue blocks/MP (not recommended) vs other. On adoption: spec revision → executor block ($0: deterministic routing + tests + suffix deletion) → new B-ladder with fresh approvals (B1' bistro-through-tiles ~$0.24/run; B2 polloteria e2e detector-live ~$0.87; B3 guest-house ~$0.29) → production wiring (index.ts photo_dims validation + client) → Phase-1 exit check.
- RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (same day, ruling 24): Santiago ADOPTED tile-all-horizontal — discrimination is a cost nice-to-have, not release-critical; prioritize release speed. Landscape pages → `needs_crops` deterministically, no model call; `LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX` deleted; `photo_dims` plumbing retained; portrait path untouched. Wide-menu dense discrimination parked POST-RELEASE (master roadmap list; blocks/MP lead recorded there). Next: $0 implementation (executor) → $0 probe-detector check → B1' bistro-through-tiles (~$0.24/run) → B2 polloteria e2e ×3 (~$0.87) → B3 guest-house (~$0.29) → production wiring → Phase-1 exit check.
- 1f IMPLEMENTATION LANDED + CLEARED 2026-07-18: `24940a7` — runPagedExtraction short-circuits landscape pages (photo_dims w>h) to needs_crops with NO model call (single-landscape early return; mixed scans extract portrait pages only, needs_crops = landscape ∪ portrait-dense/failure, ascending); LANDSCAPE_PROMPT_SUFFIX + landscape params deleted (runExtraction/extractWithRetry revert to tile/page shape). Planner independent verification: diff surgical (2 authorized files only), full suite 130/0 fresh (executor's 2 tile-cut fails = sandbox-only tmpdir), suffix sweep 0 hits repo-wide, and probe-detector re-run WITHOUT --allow-net → all 3 wide menus needs_crops=[0] deterministically, zero API calls possible by construction. Production routing for ruling 24 is proven at $0. NEXT: B1' bistro-through-tiles (needs probe-tiles menu parameterization, $0, then ~$0.24/run live).

## Eval 089b — B1' resume ×2: scorer ALL PASS ×2 but r1 REFUSED by ruling-6 audit — `Petrica` corrupted twin survives beside verified OSTRICA; ×3 attempt NOT MET (~$0.49)
- Date: 2026-07-19 | Fixes `747b598` (ruling A fixture: categories ["food"] + tolerated ["other"], Pollo pin deleted; ruling B scorer: word-prefix section tolerance `sectionSatisfies` in missing/spurious/mapping checks, reverse direction still strict; probe-tiles PROBE_RUNS env). $0 gates all green: eval-089-r1 dump re-score ALL 5 PASS; bistro fixture self-check PASS; replays byte-identical (polloteria 46→40 same 6; nikkori 0×3; 43→41/40→39/44→42). Live resume DUMP_TAG=eval089b ×2 (~$0.49): scorer ALL PASS both runs.
- **RULING-6 AUDITS:** r2 CLEAN (24 items = exactly the gated set; prices all exact; Tóxica killed bestSim 0.00; Con Champiñones truncation killed by v3). **r1 FAIL** — finals carry 25 items: the real `OSTRICA $252 (Ostión ahumado…)` AND `Petrica $252 (Salmón ahumado…)`, sect=null — print has only OSTRICA (title block + desc verified in the eval-088 OCR cache). Visible corrupted duplicate = release-blocking (2026-07-14 ruling). Scorer blind by design: items 25/24 rides the +3 band; the name differs so the name+price+desc duplicate check can't see it (eval-067 lesson re-confirmed; the audit protocol caught it).
- MECHANISM (boundary leak of existence tier v1.1, planner-traced): `petrica`↔printed `ostrica` = 2 substitutions on a 7-char token. Colocation anchor matching (tokenMatch, 1-edit) → NO anchor → polarity can't contradict (and the twin carries the SAME printed price $252, so no field conflicts exist anyway). Existence tier looseTokenMatch allowance = min(3, len/3) = 2 for 7 chars → bestSim 1.00 → kept as "real-but-misread name" (the deliberate keep-bias that protects Cabeza↔Cubeta, 2 subs @6 chars, allowance 2). Merge twin-fold needs near-name one-indel → 2 subs escape. Contrast the same run's kills: Tóxica (3+ edits → 0.00), MEDITERRÁNEA/IBÉRICA (≥3 → 0.00). CLASS: **unanchored same-price corrupted-name twin exactly 2 edits from print** — generic tile-overlap artifact (not bistro- or tile-all-specific); global allowance tightening is REJECTED up front (would delete the real Cabeza class).
- PROPOSED FIX (needs Santiago ruling + $0 validation before any code): **sibling-aware existence twin rule** — an UNANCHORED item X (bestSim ≥0.75 via loose match to printed line L) is dropped as a corrupted duplicate when another FINAL item Y matches L strictly better (exact/1-edit anchor) AND carries the same price. Structural, menu-generic (ruling 7); narrow (real misread-without-sibling stays — Tenderazo class; anchored items untouched; different prices untouched). Residual risk profile: fires wrongly only if OCR misses a real card's line whose 2-edit same-price neighbor reads — bounded by readability gate, empirically checked by the sweep. VALIDATION PLAN ($0): extend replay-colocation to report would-drops; gates = Petrica dropped on eval089b-r1 AND zero would-drops across every archived clean run (eval-085 r1–r3, eval-089-r1, eval089b-r2, nikkori r1–r3, eval-077/078 dumps).
- STATUS: B1' ×3 attempt = re-scored eval089-r1 ✓ + eval089b-r1 ✗(audit) + r2 ✓ → NOT MET. Next: Santiago rules on the fix + $0 sweep → TDD → fresh B1' ×3 (~$0.73, new approval) — and B2 polloteria ×3 doubles as the regression exposure for the new rule.
- RESOLVED 2026-07-19 (ruling 25): Santiago ruled PARK IT, MOVE ON — the Petrica class (unanchored same-price ≤len/3-edit corrupted twin beside a better-matching sibling) is TOLERATED for release; ruling-6 audits RECORD it without blocking (all other unprinted classes still block); the sibling-aware twin rule + $0 sweep is parked POST-RELEASE (master roadmap). B1' bistro-through-tiles judged CLOSED under ruling 25 (eval-089-r1 re-scored clean + 089b-r1 parked-class-only + 089b-r2 clean; tile path beats bistro's whole-read baseline — 24/24 recall, all prices exact, both runs). Next: B2 polloteria reconfirmation ×3 (~$0.87, approved) → B3 guest-house → production wiring → Phase-1 exit.

## Eval 090 — B2 polloteria reconfirmation through the ruling-24 deterministic tile path: r1 ALL PASS + audit CLEAN; r2 items/audit CLEAN but options FAIL = the ruling-16 Queso upsell emitted VERBATIM ("Agrega queso a tus papas") which the exact-match ["Queso"] tolerance misses; NO regression from eval 085 (~$0.49)
- Date: 2026-07-19 | Start commit `c01139a`; harness `probe-tiles.ts` PROBE_RUNS=3 DUMP_TAG=eval090, stop-on-first-scored-failure; PID 94302; 16 OpenAI + 2 Mistral OCR calls, ~$0.49 of the $0.87 cap. r1: ALL 5 dims PASS. r2: items=P section_context=P categories=P grams=P, **options=F (5 FP items)**; stopped.

Colocation r2 behaved exactly as eval 085: dropped the two 300gr corrupted twins (Waffle Cheese Sandwich / Chicken & Waffles Sandwich, blk 83/85, verified siblings present) and kept+flagged the field-flake class (Cheesey Bacon blk 79, Nashville blk 81). r1 colocation inert. Minor: r2 read the Alitas title as "6/12/16/20 PZ" (extra 16PZ) — in-title token variance on the tolerated Phase-2 Alitas card, NOT a new item (name-spelling class, ungated). The scorer matches tolerated option names by EXACT normalized equality (`eval-extraction.ts:307`) → "agrega queso a tus papas" ≠ "queso" → 5 FP → options FAIL. Alternative: add the verbatim string to the list (brittle to further wording).

Planner recommends accepting B2 as MET (a 3rd live run is low-value — the extraction stack is unchanged since eval 085; the prior planner flagged B2 as arguably redundant) → move to B3 guest-house + production wiring. Literal 3rd run if desired: ~$0.24.

## H1.1 diagnostic + H1.2a existence-tier fix — eval-091 losses decomposed; merged-row split lands ($0, no live spend)
- Date: 2026-07-21 | worktree `feat/extraction-eval-harness`; $0 offline (cached OCR + tile-rect math + replay sweep; 0 OpenAI, 0 Mistral calls).
- **H1.1(a) Enhancements loss = TILE GEOMETRY + edge-skip rule (fixable).** The 12 Enhancements are a compact 2-column block at normalized x[0.40,0.67] y[0.75,0.87] (from `guest-house.mistral-ocr-2048q95.json` block coords, page 2048×1241). `gridCropRects(2606,1580)` → tiles t0 x[0,.60]y[0,.60], t1 x[.40,1]y[0,.60], t2 x[0,.60]y[.40,1], t3 x[.40,1]y[.40,1]. Only t2/t3 reach that y-band and the block STRADDLES the x=.40/.60 vertical seam: in t2 the right column exceeds .60 (clipped right); in t3 the left column sits exactly on the .40 boundary (clipped left). Every containing tile sees it cut off → `TILE_PROMPT_SUFFIX` ("skip partially visible / cut-off items") suppresses all 12. Block width .27 > overlap width .20 and shifted right of it → a modest horizontal-overlap increase would seat it cleanly inside t3. Whole-read (eval 087) captured all 12 → whole+tiled union is the known-good fallback. Do NOT touch the suffix (it prevents cut-off-fragment inventions). → recall fix = H1.2b, needs Santiago ruling + paid probe.

The `max()` denominator is the DELIBERATE description-mint guard (eval-078 class) → cannot switch to candidate-coverage.

The "MISO MUSHROOMS 15" segment → 2/2 = 1.0. Confined to `bestLineSim`: `judgeItem`/anchoring/polarity/readability/unmatched-advisory all byte-identical. `colocation.ts` +17/-2, `colocation_test.ts` +19 (4 new asserts: merged-priced ≥0.75; prose-form <0.75; "NAME 15"→1.0; short-name "Mint" riding a priced neighbor <0.75). `deno fmt`/`check` clean; `colocation_test` 21/21.

## Eval 094 — H2.1 rotation-detection separability probe (mechanism found; then SUPERSEDED as active work by Priority Zero — see eval 095)
- Date: 2026-07-22 | Script `scripts/probe-rotation-h2.ts` (committed `1ef41fe`) + `_test.ts` (4/4). 4 REAL sideways horizontal-menu captures in `~/Downloads/MenusTesting/MenuRotationTesting/` (Santiago-labeled: Bistro/Polloteria pointing-left → need +90; ElMarcos/GuestHouse pointing-right → need −90 [=270]; all are portrait-shaped photos of HORIZONTAL menus — ElMarcos here is a different horizontal menu, NOT the vertical `el-marcos` fixture). 12 Mistral OCR calls (~$0.048), 3 orientations each (r0 as-captured / rCorrect / rFlip), cached durably as `{menu}-{tag}.mistral-ocr.json`.
- **FINDING 1 — Mistral OCR is orientation-ROBUST for TEXT:** reads the correct menu words in every orientation (word recall 0.89–0.99 rotated-vs-upright; near-identical word counts). → "does it read well" CANNOT verify orientation — killed the original cheap-pre-check premise for $0.05.
- **FINDING 2 — OCR BLOCK GEOMETRY = a clean single-shot, direction-aware detector (12/12):** (a) AXIS via `wide-frac` (fraction of text blocks wider-than-tall): sideways ≈0.00, horizontal ≈1.00 — huge, layout-independent margin; (b) POLARITY via `sign(corr(readingOrder, blockCenterX))`: **+ = upright, − = upside-down**; sideways turn-direction via `sign(corr(readingOrder, blockCenterY))`. Visually confirmed on bistro + guest-house (both capture directions). NOTE: the executor's `MENUS.correct` degrees were later shown INVERTED by photo — `sips -r 90` produced UPSIDE-DOWN bistro; true-upright = the `rFlip` set. The sign→degrees mapping must be pinned empirically at build time (this bit us here).
- **FINDING 3 — no orientation field** in the Mistral response (detection must be computed, not read off).
- **FINDING 4 (Santiago's worry, demonstrated live):** naive fixed "rotate 90°" produced an upside-down menu → blind single rotation is UNSAFE. The detector must be direction-aware AND re-verified after rotating; bounded ≤2 tries, never 4.
- **FINDING 5 — rotated OCR is NOT price-equivalent:** bistro sideways dropped the ENTIRE PIZZAS BISTRO price column (10 of 25 price numbers; names intact). → rotation stays REQUIRED regardless (and GPT-4o Vision, the actual extractor, is far more orientation-sensitive than Mistral OCR).
- Verdict: H2 rotation mechanism IDENTIFIED (OCR/on-device block-geometry detector; on-device iOS Vision/Android ML Kit can deliver it at $0/scan — finding transfers). PARKED behind the new Priority Zero; resume at H2.2 build.

Rotation (H2, eval 094) parked behind it.

(Executor DEVIATED on the wording — paraphrased to "printed on an item's own card"; planner caught it at the Block A checkpoint and restored the approved sentence before the paid run — commit `3221119`. The pin test asserted the executor's own string so it passed green: the exact `73e99da`-class trap the brief warns about.)
- **RESULT — decisive regression vs the M1 `b1` baseline (b1 re-scored ALL-5 this session, intact + untouched by the b1e run):**
  - polloteria (release-critical): **ALL-5 → 2/5** — items 43/40 PASS + categories PASS; options 7 FP, section_context spurious MALTEADAS, grams FAIL (Cheesey Chicken Balls + Papas Sazonadas → null).
  - bistro: options **1 FP → 15 FP** (items/section/categories/grams still PASS) — WORSE, the fix target regressed.
  - guest-house: recall 48/48 held; options 14→5 FP (all Phase-2-deferred).
- **MECHANISM (measured, not theorized):** a field `description` is a GLOBAL lever on Mistral's whole option-extraction distribution, not a surgical fix. (1) It SUPPRESSED the self-echo weight-option on 23 items — b1 = 58/58 items carry ≥1 option (the grams-fold source the M1 cleanup depends on); b1e = only 35/58 → grams break on the 2 gated items whose weight-echo vanished. (2) The "sizes or extras" framing made Mistral emit the pizza SIZE "28 cm" as an option on all 15 bistro pizzas (= the 15 FP). (3) It DID remove the target misattachment — b1's `5 Formaggi → Pollo$20/Camarón$25` is GONE in b1e — so the PROHIBITION clause works; the collateral is entirely from the positive-framing "sizes or extras" clause.
- **VERDICT: REJECT the E-instruction-via-options-description.** Classic non-local prompt side effect (master-roadmap lesson). The M2 regression check did its job — shipping it would have broken the release-critical menu. Dumps cached `~/Downloads/MenusTesting/*.mistral-b1e-r*.{raw,dump,clean.dump}.json`. Cost this leg ~$0.045 (of the $0.15 approval).
- **DECISION PENDING (Santiago), then M3:** (a) RECOMMENDED — revert the E instruction ($0, restores b1 ALL-5) + tolerate bistro `Pollo`/`Camarón` as a printed priced add-on under the ruling-16/26 family ($0 fixture `tolerated_option_names`) → bistro 5/5, M2 closes with no extraction gamble; (b) one narrow reword keeping ONLY the prohibition clause, dropping "sizes or extras" (+~$0.045, another global-lever gamble on the shared schema); (c) accept bistro 4/5 and defer the single FP like GH's, proceed to M3 (parity regression vs GPT eval093). HEAD (`3221119`) still carries the rejected instruction — revert on (a).

## Eval 097 — M2 close-out: option-misattribution colocation guard (Mistral native blocks) — GATE MET offline; M2 done
- Date: 2026-07-28 | Priority Zero (ruling 29), M2. Santiago chose to FIX the misattribution (not tolerate) — options on a wrong dish (bistro `5 Formaggi` carrying `Pollo`/`Camarón` from a general "Agrega a tu pasta o ensalada" note) is the waiter-embarrassment class. Fixed at the CLEANUP layer after eval 096 rejected the extraction-instruction channel. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-28-mistral-option-misattribution-colocation-design.md`. Commit `f8a0efc` (also reverts the eval-096 E instruction; kept the TAG param). $0, NO API.
- **MECHANISM — `dropMisattachedOptions` (mistral-cleanup.ts):** uses `pages[0].blocks` geometry, which Mistral returns FREE in the same response (no separate OCR call — this is the ruling-15 colocation idea, now Mistral-native). Per item with options: anchor the item to the block that TIGHTLY matches its name (bidirectional token-set match — the block must be *mostly* the name), then for each option find its tightest block; DROP only if `anchorStrength≥0.6 AND optStrength≥0.6 AND normalized-Euclidean-dist>0.35`; else KEEP (fail-open). Chain: dropDrinkSections → dropOtherCategoryItems → **dropMisattachedOptions** → dropSelfEchoWeightOptions → normalizeSectionTitle.
- **WHY bidirectional + fail-open (measured, cached b1 raw):** a naive distance threshold is NOT safe — single-token option names (`Pollo`/`Fresa`/`Limón`/`Alitas`) and imperfect anchoring push several LEGIT options to 0.3–0.78, overlapping the bistro misattachment. Requiring the block to be MOSTLY the name (bidi≥0.6) + fail-open collapses the noise: eligible strong-match legit options ≤0.17 (Paletas flavors 0.06–0.12, Cubeta component 0.17, exact-card 0.00); bistro `Pollo`/`Camarón` 0.56–0.57. ~3× gap; 0.35 sits mid-gap.
- **GATE (re-score 9 cached b1 dumps, blocks wired from `.raw.json`, ×3):** polloteria **ALL-5 ×3** (unchanged — grams fold intact); bistro **ALL-5 ×3** (options FP 1→0); GH recall 48/48 ×3 (options/section/grams Phase-2-deferred, unchanged). **RULING-6 AUDIT:** colocation dropped EXACTLY bistro `5 Formaggi`'s 2 options, 0 elsewhere (polloteria 9 carriers/26 opts intact incl Shape-A `M/G`, `Alitas 6/12/20 PZ`, Paletas flavors; GH 14 carriers untouched). Zero collateral.
- **TESTS:** 4 new unit (bistro drop / near keep / weak-match fail-open keep / no-blocks no-op) + 10 existing = 14; probe tests back to 4 (E-instruction test removed). `deno check` clean; `deno fmt` clean (planner ran fmt + gate — executor sandbox blocked ~/Downloads writes + git; planner committed).
- **DETERMINISM/LIVE NOTE:** rests on eval-095's live b1 extraction (deterministic ×3) + the deterministic offline guard, so a fresh live ×3 is near-redundant (Santiago's call before M3; M3 exercises it live e2e and M4 re-proves all 9 anyway).
- **DEFERRED:** vertical-menu re-validation at M4 (only wide-menu B1 dumps exist today; fail-open keeps it conservative; the 0.35 threshold gets confirmed on wider data then); nearest-owner threshold-free hardening if M4 shows a false-drop; relocation (option B — re-attach add-ons to the right dishes) out of scope.
- **VERDICT: M2 gate MET offline.** NEXT = M3 edge wiring (swap extractor to Mistral; port the mistral-cleanup chain incl. this guard to the edge; re-evaluate removing the standalone colocation stage / tile-all path) → M4 re-prove all 9 menus.

## Eval 098 — M3.0: Mistral B1 on the 6 VERTICAL menus (pulled forward from M4) — recall EXCELLENT incl. dense nikkori; guard FALSE-DROP found; "Peso" weight-option class found
- Date: 2026-07-28 | Priority Zero (ruling 29), M3.0. Santiago approved pulling the deferred vertical validation AHEAD of the M3 edge port, because two M3 architecture decisions depend on it (does a single whole-image call hold recall on verticals incl. the only `dense:true` fixture → is the tiling path really moot; does `dropMisattachedOptions` false-drop on vertical layouts → is ported edge code at risk). **7 live Mistral annotation calls ×1 (brasero, brasero-two ×2 photos, casa-nostra, el-marcos, mochomos, nikkori), tag `b1`, ~$0.035.** Harness parameterized first (`b962111`: `MENUS`/`RUNS` env, per-page raw files, per-page cleanup with that page's OWN blocks then cross-page `mergeItemSources`) — verified a strict no-op: all 9 cached wide clean dumps regenerate BYTE-IDENTICAL.
- **HEADLINE — recall in ONE whole-image call, no tiling: `nikkori` (the densest menu in the whole suite, the fixture that forced the 2x2 tile architecture) returns food-scope 48/48 EXACTLY** (raw: 122 items = 42 food + 6 dessert + 74 drink; 42+6 = the fixture's `food_items: 48`). brasero 28/28, brasero-two 44/44 (2 photos), casa-nostra 23/23, mochomos 22/22 — all `items` PASS. → **the tile-all / dense-cutter path looks MOOT on verticals too**, subject to a ruling-6 photo audit (not yet done on these dumps).
- **FINDING 1 — the `"Peso"` weight-option class (systemic, dominates the vertical FAIL count).** Mistral emits the printed item weight as a null-price option literally named **`Peso`** carrying `grams` (`New York → {name:"Peso", price:null, grams:400}`). `dropSelfEchoWeightOptions` matches only name-echo OR a bare `(NNNgr)` paren, so it misses this shape → the weight becomes an option FALSE POSITIVE *and* item grams stay null. One bug, two failed dims. **Broadening the predicate to `price == null && grams != null` is a STRICT NO-OP on all 9 cached wide dumps (verified exhaustively — zero options in polloteria/bistro/guest-house r1–r3 are newly matched), so it cannot regress the M2 gate.** Simulated through the real cleanup code: brasero 2 FAIL→1 (grams PASS, options FP 25→0), casa-nostra 2→1 (options FP 10→1), brasero-two options FP 31→15, mochomos unchanged 1 (2 FP). NOT yet implemented — planner does not write the rule (executor TDD block, pending approval).
- **FINDING 2 — `dropMisattachedOptions` FALSE-DROPS a fixture-EXPECTED option on a vertical menu.** `brasero-two` `Taco Loiro → "Pollo" @150` is a REQUIRED `items_with_options` fixture pin, and the guard drops it (1 drop across all 6 verticals: brasero 0/30 options, casa-nostra 0/16, el-marcos 0/11, mochomos 0/2, nikkori 0/42, brasero-two 1/47). **Mechanism:** the option's TRUE printed source is the card's own description block `"…A elegir: (picaña $165, pollo $150)"` at dist **0.038** — but `bestBlock` requires bidi≥0.6 (block must *be* the name), which a long description block can never satisfy, so the option instead tight-matches (1.00) a DISTANT `"# POLLO"` heading (a different dish's card) at **0.422** → far → drop. This is precisely the risk the M2 spec deferred to M4; running it early caught it before it was ported into edge code.
  - The spec's deferred **"nearest-owner" hardening does NOT fix this**: `"# POLLO"` is 0.029 from another item's anchor (`Pechuga al Limón…`), so nearest-owner also reads it as owned elsewhere and still drops.
  - **Candidate fix (NOT validated enough to adopt):** before dropping, rescue the option if a block NEAR the anchor CONTAINS its tokens (containment, not bidi). Naive form (rescue radius = FAR_DIST 0.35) **breaks the bistro true positive** — a pizza description `"…portobello y pollo marinado."` at 0.306 would rescue bistro's misattached `Pollo`. A tighter rescue radius (~0.15) separates on this data (brasero-two 0.038/0.052 vs bistro 0.306) but is another constant fitted to few points.
- **FINDING 3 — `dropDrinkSections` collateral on mixed sections (2 menus).** The rule drops a whole section when ANY item in it is `drink`-labeled. nikkori `POSTRES` holds 4 drink-labeled items beside 6 real desserts → **all 6 desserts killed** (the cleaned `items` 42/48 FAIL + `categories` missing `dessert` + `section_context` missing `Postres` are ONE root cause, not three). el-marcos `JUGOS Y FRUTAS` holds 5 drinks + 2 real FOOD items (`Plato Surtido`, `Yogurth con Fruta…`) → both dropped, and `Plato Surtido` is a fixture-pinned expectation. The wide menus are unaffected (only polloteria `Bebidas` is mixed, and that drop is intentional/M1-designed). The rule earned its evidence on a menu where the mixed section WAS all-drinks; verticals disprove the generalization.
- **OPEN — el-marcos overcount needs a Santiago photo adjudication.** Raw 59 items (45 food / 12 drink / 2 other) → cleaned 43 vs the fixture's 28 food. Not diagnosed here and NOT auditable from model output (ruling: adjudicate from the photo). Note el-marcos has prior form on exactly this question (iteration 028b real-vs-junk diagnostic; re-adjudicated 34→28), so this may be an oracle-fit issue, a genuine over-read, or both.
- **Scored summary (cleaned, r1, current code):** brasero 3/5 · brasero-two 3/5 · casa-nostra 3/5 · mochomos 4/5 · nikkori 1/5 · el-marcos 0/5. With the FINDING-1 broadening simulated: brasero 4/5 · casa-nostra 4/5 · mochomos 4/5 · brasero-two 3/5 · nikkori 1/5 · el-marcos 0/5. **No dim is failing for a reason that traces to Mistral's READING** — every diagnosed failure is our cleanup layer meeting shapes it was never measured against (the M1/M2 rules were fitted on 3 wide menus).
- **VERDICT: the sequencing call paid for itself at $0.035.** Mistral's extraction quality holds on verticals (full recall incl. the dense fixture, one call, no tiling); the *cleanup layer* is what is not yet menu-general. Porting first would have shipped a guard that deletes a gate-required option into edge code. **NEXT = 3 cleanup-layer decisions (see brief PROGRESS LOG) before the M3 port; the port itself is unchanged in shape.** Cost this leg $0.035 live + $0 analysis.

## Eval 099 — M3.0b: $0 constant-sensitivity sweep of the 3 candidate cleanup fixes across ALL 9 menus — all three adopted-ready, wide plateaus
- Date: 2026-07-28 | Priority Zero (ruling 29), M3.0b. Santiago approved "$0 and any other validation that gives grounded evidence", with the standing constraint that solutions must generalize to **all languages and diverse designs**. Method: a scratchpad harness re-implements the 3 candidate rules with their constants EXPOSED, runs the full cleanup over all 9 menus / 12 photos (wide ×3 runs, verticals ×1), scores every dim, and sweeps each constant across its whole range — **the deliverable is the width of the stable plateau, not a single passing value** (a narrow plateau = a constant fitted to our 9 menus = a ruling-7 generalization risk; a wide one = a real separation).
- **Faithfulness self-check FIRST:** the re-implementation at baseline settings reproduces today's shipped behaviour EXACTLY — polloteria 5/5, bistro 5/5, guest-house 2/5, verticals 3/3/3/0/4/1, and the guard dropping precisely the 2 bistro true-positives + the 1 brasero-two false-positive. Sweep results are therefore measuring the candidates, not a divergent copy.
- **All three rules are language-agnostic BY CONSTRUCTION (no keyword lists):** #1 = "option has no price but carries a grams number"; #2 = page geometry + token containment; #3 = the model's own `category` labels. Only their numeric constants needed evidence.
- **#1 `Peso` weight-option fold — no constant, no trade-off.** `peso=off → on`: TOTAL 26→28/45 (brasero 3→4, casa-nostra 3→4), **zero regressions on any menu**. Confirms the earlier exhaustive check that it is a strict no-op on the wide dumps.
- **#2 guard rescue radius — plateau 0.05–0.30 (6× wide), breaks only at 0.35.** Rescue = "an option survives if a block within radius R of the dish's anchor CONTAINS its tokens". At R=0 the brasero-two false drop occurs; **at every R from 0.05 to 0.30 the bistro true-positives are still dropped 2/2 AND there are zero false drops**; at R=0.35 the bistro fix is LOST (a pizza description "…portobello y pollo marinado" at 0.306 rescues the misattached option). The planner's earlier pessimism ("fitted to 2 data points, needs ~0.15") was WRONG — the separation is wide. Recommend **R=0.15** (plateau centre, ~2× margin either side).
- **#3 drink-section threshold — plateau 0.75–0.90, bounded by real menus on both sides.** Drop a section only when ≥F of its items are drinks (F=0 is today's "any single drink kills the section"). Lower bound = el-marcos `Jugos y Frutas` at 71.4% drink (must be KEPT — it holds 2 real food plates); upper bound = polloteria `Bebidas` at 93.3% drink (must be DROPPED — the M1 Malteadas requirement); nikkori `POSTRES` at 40% sits far below. Measured: F=0.5–0.7 → 30/45; **F=0.75–0.90 → 31/45 (flat across 4 sampled values)**; F≥0.95 → polloteria BREAKS 5/5→3/5. Recommend **F=0.80** (plateau centre; the true gap 0.714↔0.933 has midpoint 0.82).
- **COMBINED FINAL (`peso=on, F=0.80, R=0.15`) — the M2 gate is preserved EXACTLY ×3:** polloteria ALL-5 ×3, bistro ALL-5 ×3, guest-house 2/5 ×3 (recall PASS; options/section/grams Phase-2-deferred, unchanged). Verticals: brasero 3→4, casa-nostra 3→4, nikkori **1→3**, el-marcos 0→1, brasero-two 3→3 (its required `Taco Loiro → Pollo` option is RESTORED — a ruling-6 correctness win its score doesn't show), mochomos 4→4. TOTAL 26→31/45. **Ruling-6 audit: across all 9 menus the guard now drops EXACTLY the 2 bistro misattached options and nothing else.**
- **Residual `options` failures are ONE more systemic class each, all deterministic + language-neutral (scoped to M4, NOT expanded here):** nikkori's remaining 42 option FPs are 100% *self-echo WITH price* (option name == item name AND option price == item price — e.g. `Salmón Roll → "Salmón Roll"@169`), which the null-price fold cannot touch by design; el-marcos emits `price: 0` where the oracle expects null (a normalization class); brasero-two/casa-nostra carry portion words (`Porción individual`) as null-price options. None is a new failure MODE.
- **VERDICT: all three adopt-ready on measured evidence, each sitting mid-plateau rather than on a fitted edge.** NEXT = executor TDD block implementing the three in `scripts/mistral-cleanup.ts` (gate: the numbers above reproduce), then the M3.1 edge port. el-marcos's 43-vs-28 overcount remains a Santiago PHOTO adjudication and is independent of all three.

## Eval 099b — el-marcos overcount ROOT-CAUSED ($0): not invention — the VARIANT-FOLD convention was a GPT-4o PROMPT behaviour Mistral does not reproduce
- Date: 2026-07-28 | Priority Zero, M3.0b follow-up. The planner was about to ask Santiago for a photo adjudication of el-marcos (cleaned 43 vs oracle 28 food). **No photo is needed — the fixture itself is the proof.**
- **Evidence:** el-marcos's `items_with_options` oracle expects the FOLD: `Huevos Revueltos` = ONE item carrying `jamón` as an option; `Chilaquiles` = ONE item + `Regionales`/`Divorciados`; `Enchiladas` = ONE item + `Verdes`/`Rojas`/`Suizas`; `Machaca` + `huevo`/`verdura`; `Hot Cakes` + `jamón`; `Pan Tostado` + `Blanco`/`Integral`. Mistral instead emits **every printed variant line as its own item** (HUEVOS section = 18 items incl. `Huevos Revueltos con Jamón` $90, `…con Chorizo` $90, `…con Tocino` $90). Those lines ARE printed separately — that is exactly why the fold convention exists — so this is a CONVENTION mismatch, not a hallucination. Both scorer symptoms agree: items 43 vs 28 (over) AND `options` "missed targets: 10" (the folds absent).
- **Mechanism:** the fold is instructed IN THE GPT-4o PROMPT (`extract.ts` EXTRACT_PROMPT: *"When the same base dish is printed several times with different fillings, proteins, or preparations, return ONE item named after the base dish and put each printed variant in options"*). The Mistral annotation call carries NO such instruction, and **eval 096 proved the schema-`description` channel is unusable** (global lever: regressed polloteria ALL-5→2/5). So the fold must become a DETERMINISTIC cleanup rule, like every other Mistral artifact in this migration.
- **Scope — this is a GENERAL migration item, not an el-marcos quirk:** any menu printing base-dish variants is affected; el-marcos is merely where the density makes it dominate the score. It is the same convention family as Shape-A size variants (ruling 1, Phase-2-owned) and is closely related to the residual `options` classes from eval 099 (nikkori's self-echo-with-price, `price:0` normalization).
- **Consequence for planning:** el-marcos CANNOT reach its gate under Mistral until a deterministic variant fold exists; it is not fixable by the 3 eval-099 fixes (which is why el-marcos only moved 0/5→1/5 there). Candidate rule shape (NOT designed yet): group same-section items whose names share a base prefix and differ by a trailing modifier, fold the modifiers into options on the base-priced item — must be language-neutral (no keyword list) and must not fold genuinely distinct dishes; needs its own spec + plateau validation across all 9 menus, and a Santiago convention ruling on where the fold's boundary sits.

## Eval 099c — M3.0c: the 3 validated cleanup fixes LANDED (`94d75a3`, $0) — gate reproduces eval 099 exactly; 26→31/45
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, M3.0c. Santiago approved adopting all three eval-099 fixes. Executor implemented TDD-first from the planner's block; planner verified, ran fmt + the gate, and committed (executor sandbox blocks fmt/~Downloads/git, the established pattern).
- **Executor HARD-STOPPED correctly on a pre-existing test failure** — `dropDrinkSections drops a drink section including non-drink items` went red. **Planner adjudication: that test encoded the OLD rule at an unrealistic ratio** (synthetic `Bebidas` = 1 drink + 1 dessert = 50% drink, vs polloteria's real 14/15 = 93%). Its INTENT (a dropped section takes its non-drink items with it; other sections survive) is still valid, so the fixture was changed to 5 drinks + Malteadas (83%) rather than the assertion being weakened. The authoritative proof that no requirement was lost is the real gate below — polloteria's actual Bebidas + Malteadas are still dropped. 19/19 tests, `deno check`/`fmt` clean.
- **Implementation matches spec** (planner diff-audit): weight predicate `price==null && (echo || WEIGHT_PAREN || grams!=null)`; `DRINK_SECTION_FRAC = 0.8` with per-section drink/total tally (null-section items only dropped if themselves drinks); `RESCUE_DIST = 0.15` containment check (plain token containment, NOT bidiMatch) evaluated only after the FAR_DIST drop test. One benign deviation from the planner's sweep code: an option whose name normalizes to ZERO tokens is rescued (kept) rather than dropped — fail-open, the safe direction, and the gate proves no such case exists in the 9 menus (numbers match the sweep exactly).
- **GATE — reproduces eval 099's predicted numbers EXACTLY:** wide ×3 → polloteria **ALL-5 ×3**, bistro **ALL-5 ×3**, guest-house 2/5 ×3 (recall PASS, options/section/grams Phase-2-deferred, unchanged). Verticals → brasero 3→**4**, casa-nostra 3→**4**, nikkori 1→**3**, el-marcos 0→**1**, mochomos 4, brasero-two 3. **TOTAL 26→31/45.**
- **RULING-6 AUDIT (6 checks, all pass):** bistro `5 Formaggi` options `[]` (both misattachments gone); brasero-two `Taco Loiro` options `[Picaña@165, Pollo@150]` (the false drop RESTORED); nikkori 48 items with all **6 desserts kept**; el-marcos `Plato Surtido` + `Yogurth…` kept; polloteria Malteadas absent + no `Bebidas` section + combo grams intact (`Megacharola Boneless` 1800, `PapaBoneless` 600); brasero weights folded onto items (`New York` 400) with zero `Peso` options surviving.
- **VERDICT: M3.0 CLOSED.** The cleanup layer is now validated on all 9 menus, with every constant sitting mid-plateau. **NEXT = M3.1 edge port** (spec then executor): Mistral annotation call replaces the GPT-vision call in `extract.ts`/`runPagedExtraction`; port this cleanup chain (incl. the guard, blocks from the SAME response, NO `parseItemGrams`); tiling/colocation become unreachable rather than deleted (prove-then-remove); $0 gate = replay the cached raws through the EDGE code and reproduce the numbers above. Then M3.2 live e2e (~$0.015), then M4. Known M4 work items (all deterministic + language-neutral, none a new failure mode): the variant fold (eval 099b), nikkori's self-echo-WITH-price options, el-marcos `price:0`→null normalization.

## Eval 100 — M3.1: Mistral extractor + cleanup PORTED INTO THE EDGE (`86bde25`, $0) — edge gate reproduces eval 099c exactly
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero (ruling 29), M3.1. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-29-m3-1-mistral-edge-port-design.md`. Executor implemented TDD-first from the planner block; planner verified, fixed the gate script, ran fmt + gate + audit, committed. NO redeploy (deployed fn untouched), NO API calls.
- **THE SWAP:** `runPagedExtraction` now does, per photo in parallel: Mistral `document_annotation` call → `mistralCleanup(toExtractedItems(items), page)` with **THAT page's own blocks** → `mergeItemSources` across pages. `mistral-extract.ts` reads `pages[0]` blocks from the SAME response (no second API call — the ruling-15 colocation evidence is now free+native); schema copied VERBATIM from the probe (**no field `description`** — eval 096); retry once on timeout then throw, no GPT fallback. GPT-4o enrichment untouched.
- **SINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH:** the rule functions MOVED to `supabase/functions/analyze-menu/mistral-cleanup.ts` byte-for-byte (planner verified by diffing the extracted rule block against `HEAD:scripts/mistral-cleanup.ts` → IDENTICAL); `scripts/mistral-cleanup.ts` now re-exports them and keeps only its CLI. The eval-099c gate therefore cannot drift from what production runs. Constants intact (0.8 / 0.6 / 0.35 / 0.15). Confirmed no `parseItemGrams` / `postprocessItems` on the Mistral path.
- **TRIGGERS DISCONNECTED, MACHINERY KEPT:** deleted the landscape short-circuit (ruling 24), the `photoDims` param, the `DENSE_FAILURE` dense-signal branch and the `needs_crops` assembly — all dead once Mistral reads whole images (evals 095/097 wide + 098 nikkori dense 48/48). `runGroupedExtraction`, `verifyTileItems*`, `colocationStage`, `EXTRACT_PROMPT`/`EXTRACT_SCHEMA` and the `extract-pages` stage are left INTACT but unreachable ("prove moot before removing" — deletion is a post-M4 step). **The `PagedExtraction` union and `needs_crops` handling stay, so `index.ts` routing and the ENTIRE CLIENT are untouched — zero release coupling in M3.1.** Metadata Mistral doesn't provide is synthesized (`usable:true`, `dense:false`); verified safe — the client stores `image_layout` as null and never reads `image_quality`.
- **EDGE GATE (`scripts/replay-edge-mistral.ts` — cached b1 raws injected through the EDGE `runPagedExtraction`, $0): reproduces eval 099c EXACTLY** — polloteria **5/5 ×3**, bistro **5/5 ×3**, guest-house 2/5 ×3, brasero 4, brasero-two 3, casa-nostra 4, el-marcos 1, mochomos 4, nikkori 3 = **31/45**. No `needs_crops` ever returned (the script throws if one appears).
- **RULING-6 AUDIT VIA THE EDGE PATH (6/6):** bistro `5 Formaggi` options `[]`; brasero-two `Taco Loiro` = `[Picaña@165, Pollo@150]`; nikkori 48 items / 6 desserts; el-marcos keeps `Plato Surtido` + `Yogurth…`; polloteria no Malteadas, no `Bebidas`, `Megacharola Boneless`=1800, `PapaBoneless`=600; brasero `New York`=400 with zero `Peso` options.
- **PLANNER FIXES to the executor's work (disclosed):** the executor's `deno check` of `index.ts` failed only on a sandbox DNS block for the std-lib import (planner re-ran with network → clean). The gate script needed two corrections before it could be trusted: it iterated ALL menus at one `RUNS` value (verticals have only r1 cached) → added a `MENUS` filter; and it keyed cached responses by **call order** via a mutating counter → re-keyed **by photo**, so the gate can't silently depend on the extractor's invocation order. `extract_test.ts`'s `runPagedExtraction` block was rewritten (the "portrait byte-identical" invariant is gone BY CONSTRUCTION under ruling 29 — M4's vertical gates replace it); all other tests in the file untouched. 124/124 pass.
- **VERDICT: M3.1 CLOSED.** NEXT = **M3.2 live e2e** (~$0.015, needs Santiago's cost approval): the 3 wide menus ×1 through the real edge path with live Mistral calls, confirming the wiring works against the API and not just cached JSON. Then **M4** all-9 ×3 + its work items (variant fold eval 099b, nikkori self-echo-with-price options, el-marcos `price:0`→null). Deployment of the edge fn remains a separate explicit step (back up first).

## Eval 101 — M3.2 live e2e: WIRING PROVEN, but Mistral's annotation SHAPE has DRIFTED — the cached-dump gate no longer reflects the live model ($0.020)
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, M3.2. Ran the 3 wide menus ×1 through the REAL edge path (`eval-027-live.ts` → edge `runPagedExtraction` → live Mistral), production-mirror input. Prerequisite fix first (`5357c2a`): the gate runner's dense-detector assertion was OBSOLETE and would have spuriously FAILED polloteria + nikkori (both `dense:true`) — replaced with a tile-path-DISCONNECTED assertion.
- **WHAT M3.2 SET OUT TO PROVE — PROVEN:** `routing: single Mistral read` on all 3 menus (no `needs_crops`, tile path stays disconnected), live API accepted the strict schema, `pages[].blocks` flowed into the guard, per-page cleanup + merge ran end-to-end. **The M3.1 wiring is correct.**
- **WHAT IT ALSO REVEALED — the live output SHAPE differs from the 07-22 cached dumps:**
  - polloteria: `items` PASS 42/40, `section_context` PASS, `categories` PASS; **`options` 31 FP, `grams` FAIL** (`Cheesey Chicken Balls`→null).
  - bistro: `items` 26/24 PASS but the "Agrega a tu pasta o ensalada" note now arrives as **two standalone ITEMS** (`Pollo`, `Camarón`) instead of misattached OPTIONS on `5 Formaggi`. `dropMisattachedOptions` removes misattached *options* and structurally CANNOT touch misattached *items* — a new manifestation of the same root failure, not a regression of the fix.
  - guest-house: 47/48 items; options 18 FP (was 14), spurious `GUEST HOUSE` section.
- **MECHANISM (measured, not theorized):** `options` semantics changed from *printed weight/variant* to *ingredient list*. Cached 07-22 `TostiBoneless → [{"Boneless (300gr)", grams 300}]`; live today `TostiBoneless → [Queso cheddar, Cebollín]`. The grams failure is downstream of this by construction — the M1 fold has no weight-option left to fold.
- **ROOT CAUSE — we pinned a MOVING ALIAS, and pinning the version does NOT fix it:** `GET /v1/models` shows `mistral-ocr-latest` → **`mistral-ocr-4-0`**, with `mistral-ocr-2512` → `mistral-ocr-3-0` also available; the cached raws record `model: "mistral-ocr-latest"`. **But a $0.005 confirmation call on the PINNED `mistral-ocr-2512` (v3) did NOT reproduce the cached shape either** — it returned the weight echo AND ingredient options (63 raw items). So the OCR model version is not the only moving part: **the LLM that fills `document_annotation` is itself unpinnable from our side.** Pinning `mistral-ocr-*` is therefore necessary-but-insufficient for reproducibility.
- **WHAT SURVIVES THE DRIFT (the important half):** **item RECALL is robust** across both versions and both dates — polloteria 42/40, bistro 26/24, guest-house 47/48 all PASS `items`, and raw v3 today returns 63. Reading every printed dish — the hard part, and the reason for ruling 29 — is stable. `section_context`/`categories` also held on polloteria. What drifts is `options` semantics and, downstream, fold-derived `grams`.
- **CONSEQUENCE:** evals 099/099c/100 remain valid as proof that our CODE is correct (they are exact, reproducible replays), but they are NO LONGER evidence about the CURRENT live model. Every cleanup constant in M1/M2/M3.0 was fitted to the 07-22 shape. **M4 must not be gated against the cached dumps.**
- **ESCALATED to Santiago with 3 options (planner recommends a+c):** (a) make the cleanup SHAPE-ROBUST rather than shape-fitted — drop ingredient-options generically (null price, no grams, tokens appearing in the item's own description = a component, not a choice) and take grams from the printed name/description via `parseItemGrams` as a FALLBACK when no weight option exists (note: as a fallback this does NOT reintroduce the M1 bug, which was parseItemGrams *overriding* a correct summed fold); (b) re-baseline on today's shape and accept periodic re-fitting (fragile — it will break again on the next silent upgrade); (c) **reconsider the architecture: use Mistral for OCR TEXT + BLOCKS only (deterministic, and what we already trust for the colocation guard) and do the structuring with a PINNED model we control** — restoring end-to-end reproducibility and converging with the external research's layout-first hybrid (eval 070 / ruling 20). Cost this leg $0.015 (M3.2) + $0.005 (version probe) = **$0.020**.

## Eval 102 — Option (c) feasibility: Mistral's OCR layer is FROZEN while its ANNOTATION layer drifted 2.7× — the drift is isolated to the LLM stage ($0.005)
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, post-M3.2 escalation. Santiago asked to see what option (c) — "Mistral for OCR text+blocks only, structuring by a PINNED model we control" — would look like, to decide whether to discard it. This decomposes the eval-101 drift by LAYER, on the SAME alias (`mistral-ocr-latest`), 07-22 cached vs today.
- **RESULT — the two layers behave completely differently:**

  | layer | 07-22 → today (same alias) |
  |---|---|
  | OCR `markdown` | char similarity **0.9999**, token Jaccard **0.997**, exactly ONE token differs (`cebollin`) |
  | OCR `blocks` | **162 → 162**; content/geometry hashes differ only by that same token-level noise |
  | dish-name recall from OCR text | **IDENTICAL** (same 2 first-word absences, both draft spelling variants) |
  | `document_annotation` items | 58 → 59 |
  | `document_annotation` options | **90 → 245 (2.7×)** |

- **CONCLUSION: the drift is isolated to the ANNOTATION (LLM) stage.** The OCR transcription+geometry stage is effectively frozen week-over-week. Option (c) is therefore **NOT discardable — it is the structurally sound option**: it keeps the layer that is stable and deterministic (and that we ALREADY trust — `dropMisattachedOptions` runs on exactly these blocks) and moves the volatile semantic stage onto a model we can pin. It also converges with the external research's layout-first hybrid (eval 070, ruling 20).
- **PLANNER CORRECTION (recorded so the next planner does not inherit the error):** an earlier statement this session — "the OCR text layer also drifts at character level" — was WRONG. That comparison was `mistral-ocr-2512` (v3) vs the 07-22 `latest`, i.e. a MODEL-VERSION difference, not date drift. On the same alias the text is 99.99% stable. This strengthens (c) rather than weakening it.
- **HARD CONSTRAINT FOUND for any pinning strategy:** `mistral-ocr-2512` (= `mistral-ocr-3-0`) returns **`blocks: []`** — ZERO layout geometry — while `mistral-ocr-latest` (= `mistral-ocr-4-0`) returns 162. So **pinning the OCR model to v3 would silently make the colocation guard inert** (fail-open ⇒ keeps every misattached option). Any "pin the version" plan must verify blocks survive; today only `latest`/v4 provides them, and `latest` is by definition unpinnable. Aliases seen today: `mistral-ocr-latest`=`mistral-ocr-4-0`=`mistral-ocr-4`; `mistral-ocr-2512`=`mistral-ocr-3-0`=`mistral-ocr-3`.
- **(c) SHAPE, as it would be built (NOT designed in detail, NOT approved):** Stage-1a = Mistral OCR call returning `markdown` + `pages[].blocks` (no `document_annotation_format`) — deterministic, cheap, already cached for all 9 menus; Stage-1b = a PINNED model (e.g. a dated Claude/GPT snapshot) converts that text+geometry into our menu schema; the existing deterministic cleanup + colocation guard stay as-is (they already consume blocks). Open questions to settle before any spend: does a text+geometry prompt reach the same recall Mistral's native annotation achieved (the eval-095 win we must not lose)? cost per scan vs today's ~$0.005? and Stage-1b becomes a NEW prompt surface, so it inherits the master-roadmap "prompt edits have non-local side effects" discipline.
- **Cost this leg $0.005** (one `latest` call; the v3 call was eval 101's). Session total live spend: **$0.060** ($0.035 M3.0 verticals + $0.015 M3.2 + $0.010 the two version probes).

## Eval 103 — Option (c) PROBED LIVE: pinned text-structuring from Mistral OCR text reaches full recall in ONE call; bistro ALL-5, nikkori 48/48 4/5 ($0.145)
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero (ruling 29), decision gate a/b/c. Santiago ruled **probe (c) first**. Script `scripts/probe-c-textstructure.ts` (+`_test.ts`) — commits `b4bc2ac` (request), `7336dbd` (archival), `e66b960` (source amendment). **Single-variable design: the INCUMBENT extractor with pixels replaced by text** — `EXTRACT_PROMPT` + `EXTRACT_SCHEMA` imported VERBATIM from `extract.ts` (never copied), temp 0, seed 17 (= `EXTRACT_SEED`), `strict` structured output, one text part, zero `image_url`. The only new prompt surface is a 3-fact `TEXT_PROMPT_SUFFIX` (input is a transcription / work only from it / the image-metadata fields are unobservable). Planner independently re-derived the body for all 4 menus: prompt prefix byte-identical, suffix immediately after, markdown last.
- **KEY INSIGHT THAT MADE (c) CHEAP TO TEST — the conventions were never lost, only unplugged.** `EXTRACT_PROMPT` already instructs every open M4 work item: the variant fold (lines 43-45 — eval 099b), "ingredients joined by y/and are parts of ONE dish, never options" (= eval 101's ingredient-option drift), "never omit printed weights" (= grams). `postprocess.ts:68 foldVariantCards` implements the fold deterministically. Both passed 3/3 on all 6 verticals (F1-F4) and eval-093 H1.3. **The el-marcos overcount and the option-semantics drift are MIGRATION-INDUCED, not new convention questions** — which is why the variant-fold ruling was deferred rather than spent.
- **$0 PRE-FLIGHT (recall ceiling) — retired the biggest (c) risk and caught a trap.** Every apparent truth-vs-text absence is a spelling/accent/unit variant (`Cheesy`/`Cheesey`, `Salas's (450g)`/`Salsa's (450gr)`, `Maíz`/`Maiz`, `Mangudo`/`Manguilo`, `Kurimi`/`Kurimu`). **Decisively: Mistral's own annotation returns the SAME spellings its markdown has** (`Manguilo`, `Kurimu Roll`) ⇒ the annotation LLM extracts nothing beyond the text layer, so a text-only Stage-1b starts from exactly the evidence the 48/48 annotation had. **TRAP CAUGHT:** the planner's first spec pointed Stage-1a at `nikkori.mistral-ocr.json`, a stale 07-18 capture MISSING printed `Rainbow` + `Maiz Roll` (8386 chars vs the b1 response's 8192 — the bigger file is the worse read). Probing on it would have starved nikkori and read as "(c) loses recall". Source moved to `*.mistral-b1-r1.raw.json` (same-response text-vs-annotation; byte-identical text for the other 3 menus).
- **ARM 1 (canary, `gpt-4o-2024-11-20`, $0.018): FAILED on ENUMERATION, not comprehension.** polloteria returned **12 items** — the first 3 of 20 printed headings (Entradas 6, Pa'Compartir 3, Ensaladas 3) — with `finish_reason=stop` at only 914 output tokens, i.e. NOT truncation but GPT-4o's long-list laziness, which `EXTRACT_PROMPT`'s "do not stop after a representative sample" does not win from text. Every returned item was correct, including `Dedos De Queso $199` — the price GPT-4o **vision** misreads as $119 (ruling 10).
- **ARM 2 (section-chunked gpt-4o, ~$0.08) NOT RUN — made MOOT by arm 1's model swap.** Approved but deliberately unspent.
- **ARM 1b (`gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, same request, ONLY the model id changed): the enumeration failure DISAPPEARS.** polloteria 12→**95** items, nikkori **121** (Mistral's own annotation on nikkori: 122), all `finish=stop`, fingerprint `fp_c935aef436` identical across all 4 calls. **The densest fixture in the suite — the one that forced the entire 2×2 tile architecture — structures completely from ONE text call.**
- **SCORING NOTE (the planner's first scoring pass understated (c) and was corrected):** raw/`postprocessItems` dumps score badly because the model enumerates every printed salsa/aderezo/sazonador/paleta line. The fair comparison applies the MODEL-AGNOSTIC subset of the existing chain — `dropOtherCategoryItems` (ruling 12) + `dropDrinkSections` + `normalizeSectionTitle` — which act on the model's own labels/titles and are shared by both architectures. Mistral-artifact rules (`dropSelfEchoWeightOptions`, `dropMisattachedOptions`) deliberately EXCLUDED.
- **SCORECARD, (c)+gpt-4.1 + shared filters (vs Mistral-native — cached eval 099c / LIVE eval 101, and per eval 101 only the LIVE column is valid evidence about the current model):**

  | menu | (c) gpt-4.1 | Mistral-native |
  |---|---|---|
  | bistro | **ALL 5 DIMS PASS** (24/24, options 0 FP) | 5/5 cached; **live: the "Agrega a tu pasta" add-on arrives as 2 standalone ITEMS the option-guard structurally cannot touch** |
  | nikkori | **4/5** — items **48/48 EXACT**, options **0 FP**, grams PASS, categories PASS | 3/5 (its 42 self-echo-WITH-price option FPs — an open M4 work item — simply do not occur under (c)) |
  | el-marcos | 2/5 (categories + grams PASS) | 1/5 |
  | polloteria | 1/5 (categories PASS) | 5/5 cached; **live 3/5** (options 31 FP, grams FAIL) |

- **RULING-6 AUDIT (all 4 menus, food-scope names vs the OCR text): polloteria 52/52 PRINTED, nikkori 48/48 PRINTED, bistro 24/24 PRINTED — ZERO unprinted items.** el-marcos: 5 names are **welded composites** (`CHILAQUILES (70gr.) Regionales` = base card + its variant text) — every constituent part is printed and there are no phantom dishes, but it is a name no diner sees, so it is recorded as a name-level artifact of the anti-fold, not a clean pass.
- **polloteria's failure is ONE root cause, diagnosed not guessed:** the Paletas Heladas flavor blocks print as priced HEADINGS (`# AGUA $20`, `# CREMA $30`) with 6 flavor lines each; (c) emits 12 items where the oracle counts 2 (M1 fixture ruling C). That single class drives BOTH the items overcount (52 → 42 = exactly the count Mistral scores at `items PASS 42/40`) AND the 2 spurious sections. `nullPriceNoteSections` is the existing machinery for the shape. Its remaining grams miss is the KNOWN M1 combo-sum bug (`Megacharola` 1200 vs 1800 — `parseItemGrams` takes the first description weight instead of summing).
- **WHAT (c) BUYS, measured:** full recall with NO tiling and NO chunking · zero inventions on 3/4 menus · `options` 0-4 FP where live Mistral gives 18-31 · grams recovered by the existing GPT-era `parseItemGrams` (`TostiBoneless`→450, `El Tendedero`→750) where the live Mistral path returns null · the colocation guard becomes UNNECESSARY (bistro 5/5 without it) · **and a PINNED, re-runnable extractor**, which is the entire point: deterministic constants fitted to a pinned model do not rot on a silent vendor upgrade.
- **WHAT (c) COSTS / STILL OWES:** ~$0.014-0.048 per scan of structuring + ~$0.001 OCR (vs Mistral-native ~$0.005; vs the GPT-vision path it replaces ~$0.05 whole / ~$0.24 tiled — so 3-10× today but 5-16× cheaper than the incumbent). Two calls per scan. Determinism UNPROVEN (identical fingerprint across 4 calls is not evidence — a pinned snapshot is not determinism, lesson 1, so ×3 is mandatory). Remaining work, all deterministic + convention-level and all fitted to a PINNED target: (1) priced-heading flavor blocks → one item + variants (fixes polloteria items AND sections); (2) variant fold from welded composite names (el-marcos); (3) combo grams SUM (the known M1 bug); (4) ×3 determinism gate.
- **Cost this leg $0.145** ($0.018 gpt-4o canary + $0.127 gpt-4.1 ×4 menus), under the $0.30 cap, with arm 2's ~$0.08 deliberately unspent. **Session total live spend: $0.205.** Dumps cached `~/Downloads/MenusTesting/{polloteria,nikkori,el-marcos,bistro}.eval103c{,-m41}-r1.{raw,dump,nopost.dump}.json`.
- **NEXT = Santiago's a/b/c ruling with evidence in hand** (planner recommends (c)): (c) is now measured, not theorized — it fixes the two classes that opened this gate (ingredient-options, add-on-as-items), beats Mistral-native on nikkori/el-marcos/bistro, and is behind only on polloteria via a single diagnosed printed-price-heading class. NOTE the fair next comparison would be Mistral-native's 4 menus re-run LIVE (~$0.02), since its cached column is stale.
