- **RULING-6 AUDIT: ZERO inventions across ALL 8 measured menus.** guest-house 48/48, brasero 28/28, casa-nostra 23/23 all printed. mochomos's single flagged name was a FALSE ALARM in the planner's matcher, and the finding is a CREDIT to the model: the OCR text wraps the heading across two lines (`# TACOS LECHUGA DE FILETE $295` / `DE PUERCO`) and (c) correctly rejoined it as `TACOS LECHUGA DE FILETE DE PUERCO`. Under the GPT-VISION path inventions were the dominant failure class for weeks (the whole verifier/colocation ladder, evals 061-093, exists to kill them) — they are simply absent here, structurally, because the model only ever sees transcribed text.
- **guest-house's failures are NOT (c) defects — they are the fixture and the OCR:** all 3 `options` "FPs" are the Shape-A size variants the ROADMAP names as the Phase-2 eval set (`SEAFOOD PLATEAU* → FOR TWO @150 / FOR THREE OR MORE @280`, `HALF MAINE LOBSTER TAIL → chilled/grilled`, `MEXICAN WHITE SHRIMP → EA @7`) — **(c) emitted them in EXACTLY ruling 1's Shape-A form** (base item + priced variants as options), the oracle simply has not promoted those pins yet (ruling 17). Its `grams` miss is an OCR MISREAD of the printed `(3g)`/`(5g)` as `(3c)`/`(5c)` — identical for either architecture, not fixable at the structuring layer. Residual: `FOIE GRAS→side` is a single-item category flake (exactly the ruling-28 residual risk already recorded as watch-across-gates), and 2 item names leaked into `section_title` (`SEAFOOD PLATEAU*`, `PRIME TOMAHAWK* GF, DF`).
- **C2 DESIGN CONSTRAINT DISCOVERED — this is what C1 was for.** C2's planned fix #2 was "split WELDED COMPOSITE names" (el-marcos `CHILAQUILES (70gr.) Regionales` ⇒ base + option). mochomos proves the same model ALSO correctly REJOINS headings that wrap across OCR lines (`TACOS LECHUGA DE FILETE` + `DE PUERCO`). **A naive splitting rule would break that legitimate rejoining.** Fitting fix #2 on eval 103's 4 menus would have shipped it blind — the exact M1/M2→M3.0 failure, avoided for $0.067.
- **VERDICT: NO new systemic failure class. C2's work list is confirmed and bounded:** (1) priced-heading flavor blocks (polloteria `# AGUA $20` + 6 flavors ⇒ one item + variants — the ONLY systemic overcount class, and it fixes items AND sections together); (2) welded-composite variant fold **subject to the rejoining constraint above**; (3) combo grams SUM (known M1 bug); (4) NEW+small: item names leaking into `section_title` (guest-house ×2); (5) NOT code — guest-house Shape-A options are a Phase-2 FIXTURE promotion (ruling 17), the `(3g)`→`(3c)` misread is OCR-level, `FOIE GRAS` category is a known flake.

## Eval 104b — C1 CLOSED at 9/9: brasero-two multi-page ($0.024) — zero inventions suite-wide, but a FOLD-WITH-DATA-LOSS class found
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, C1 close. Executor added multi-page support (`97fd334`): `ocrSourcePaths` derives one cached path per photo from `MENU_PHOTOS` via the existing `rawPath()` convention; **each page gets its OWN Stage-1b call, `postprocessItems` runs PER PAGE, then `mergeItemSources`** — the same order production's `runPagedExtraction` uses (concatenating the pages into one call would have measured an architecture we do not ship). Single-page menus unchanged by construction; polloteria's dry-run still one page at 7508 chars. Planner-verified 171 passed / 1 failed (the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` assertion; the executor's extra failure was its sandbox tmpdir denial again), `buildRequest`'s pinned-shape assertion intact byte-for-byte.
- **brasero-two: pages=2, per-page items [18,25], merged 44, `finish=stop` both pages. Scored 2/5** (items **44/44 PASS**, categories PASS; options/section_context/grams FAIL). Mistral-native cached: 3/5.
- **FINAL C1 SCORECARD — all 9 menus, (c) = 29/45 dims** (Mistral-native cached eval 099c = 31/45, but per eval 101 that column is STALE — live polloteria is 3/5 and live bistro's add-on class is unfixable):

  | menu | (c) | Mistral cached |
  |---|---|---|
  | bistro | **5/5** | 5/5 (live: broken) |
  | brasero | **5/5** | 4/5 |
  | casa-nostra | **5/5** | 4/5 |
  | nikkori | 4/5 | 3/5 |
  | mochomos | 4/5 | 4/5 |
  | el-marcos | 2/5 | 1/5 |
  | brasero-two | 2/5 | 3/5 |
  | guest-house | 1/5 | 2/5 |
  | polloteria | 1/5 | 5/5 cached / **3/5 live** |

  (c) wins or ties on 6 of 9; **3 menus at ALL-5 vs Mistral's 2.**
- **RULING-6 AUDIT: ZERO unprinted items across ALL 9 MENUS.** The invention class that dominated the GPT-VISION era — and for which the entire verifier + colocation ladder (evals 061-093) was built — is structurally absent, because the model only ever sees transcribed text.
- **🔴 CLASS A (NEW, WORST FOUND — FOLD WITH DATA LOSS).** The OCR prints `# CHURRASQUERÍA` / `SENCILLA (300gr) $495` / `DOBLE (600gr) $950`. Mistral emits TWO items (`Churrasquería sencilla` $495, `Churrasquería doble` $950). **(c) emits ONE item `CHURRASQUERÍA` $495 with `grams=null` — `DOBLE` $950 and BOTH printed weights are GONE.** The model applied `EXTRACT_PROMPT`'s variant-fold rule but DROPPED the variant instead of preserving it, where ruling 1 (Shape A) requires `CHURRASQUERÍA` $495/300g + option `DOBLE (600gr)` @950/600g. **This is a data-loss class and outranks every overcount class on severity** — an overcount is visible and foldable; a dropped priced variant is silent. It is the direct cause of brasero-two's `grams` failure (`Sencilla`/`Doble` "item not found").
- **🔴 CLASS B (NEW, 3 occurrences / 2 menus — `#`-MARKED DISH CARDS BECOME SECTIONS).** The OCR itself marks dish cards as markdown headings (`# TACO LOIRO (sirloin)`), and that markup OVERRIDES `EXTRACT_PROMPT`'s semantic rule ("a heading … must also group menu items beneath it"). Consequences on brasero-two: `TACO LOIRO (sirloin)` becomes a spurious section, and its printed choices `picaña @165`/`pollo @150` become STANDALONE ITEMS instead of options on the Taco Loiro card — the same pin eval 098's colocation guard once false-dropped. Also guest-house ×2 (`SEAFOOD PLATEAU*`, `PRIME TOMAHAWK* GF, DF`). The model is being FAITHFUL TO ITS INPUT here, so the fix belongs at the deterministic layer (a heading carrying a price and grouping nothing is a dish — `promoteSections`/`dropHeaderEchoes` are the existing machinery), NOT in the prompt: overriding the `#` prior by instruction is a global lever and eval 096 is the standing warning about those.
- **🟡 CLASS C (NEW, small) — letter-spaced section titles:** brasero-two emits `P O S T R E S` (so `Postres` is missing and `BROWNIE` maps to the spaced form). Deterministic and language-neutral to normalize (collapse runs of single-character tokens); belongs beside `normalizeSectionTitle`.
- **TWO PLANNER MATCHER ERRORS, disclosed:** (1) an audit script flagged mochomos's `TACOS LECHUGA DE FILETE DE PUERCO` as not-in-text — it is a heading the OCR WRAPS across two lines and (c) correctly REJOINED it (a credit to the model, and the source of C2's rejoining constraint); (2) a case-sensitive grep produced the claim "`Sencilla`/`Doble` are absent from the OCR text", which was WRONG — they are printed in lowercase, the text is complete, so Class A is a structuring defect and NOT an OCR ceiling. **Corollary: the eval-102/103 finding still stands — Mistral's annotation sees nothing its own markdown layer lacks.**
- **C2 WORK LIST, now measured across all 9 and ordered by severity:** (A) fold-with-data-loss ⇒ enforce Shape A (ruling 1): a folded variant MUST survive as a priced option with its grams, never be dropped; (B) `#`-marked dish cards ⇒ a priced heading that groups nothing is an item, not a section (fixes 3 spurious sections AND recovers the Taco Loiro options); (C) letter-spaced titles; (D) priced-heading FLAVOR blocks (polloteria `# AGUA $20` + 6 flavors ⇒ one item + variants — fixes items AND sections together); (E) welded-composite fold (el-marcos) **subject to the wrapped-heading rejoining constraint**; (F) combo grams SUM (known M1 bug). NOT code: guest-house's Shape-A options are a Phase-2 FIXTURE promotion (ruling 17), its `(3g)`→`(3c)` is an OCR misread, `FOIE GRAS→side` is the known ruling-28 flake.
- **Cost this leg $0.024** (2 calls; in 3330 / out 2216). **Session total live spend: $0.296.** **C1 is CLOSED at 9/9.**
- **VERDICT: C1 paid for itself three times over** — Classes A, B and C are all invisible from eval 103's 4 menus, and Class A is a silent data-loss defect that would have reached a ×3 live gate. Fitting C2's rules on 4 menus would have repeated the M1/M2→M3.0 failure exactly. **NEXT = C2, designed against this list with plateau discipline; ×3 determinism remains a C4 gate item (the identical fingerprint across all 11 calls is NOT evidence — lesson 1).**

## Eval 104c — CORRECTION to 104b: there is NO data-loss class. (c) natively produces ruling-1 Shape A; part of its score gap is FIXTURE DEBT, not model error ($0)
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, C1 post-mortem. **Two claims in eval 104b were WRONG and are retracted here rather than silently edited.**
- **RETRACTION 1 — "CLASS A: fold with data loss" DOES NOT EXIST.** The planner's inspection query selected on item name/section and printed only `name | price | grams | section` — **it never printed `options`**, so `CHURRASQUERÍA $495 grams=null` looked like a dropped `DOBLE $950`. The actual output is `CHURRASQUERÍA $495 → options [SENCILLA (300gr)@495, DOBLE (600gr)@950, …]` — **precisely ruling 1's Shape A** (base card + priced variants carrying grams in the option name). Nothing was lost. **The severity-ordered C2 list in 104b is therefore wrong at the top and is superseded by this entry.**
- **RETRACTION 2 — the "sizes are NOT options prompt conflict" hypothesis was also refuted** (before spending, at $0): polloteria under (c) keeps `Ensalada Boneless $158 → M (150gr)@158 / G (300gr)@179`, `Crispy Chicken $162 → Grandes (300gr)@162 / Jumbo (600gr)@235`, `Alitas $129 → 6 PZ@129 / 12PZ@169 / 20PZ@269`. The `EXTRACT_PROMPT` sentence "serving formats and sizes … are NOT options" does NOT suppress printed food size variants.
- **NEW INSTRUMENT (planner scratchpad, $0) — PRINTED-PRICE COVERAGE:** every price printed in the OCR text should be claimed by the output as an item price or an option price. This catches SILENT loss that item-count bands hide (the eval-067 lesson). **Result across all 9 (c) dumps: 152/156 distinct printed prices claimed = 97.4%, and every one of the 4 unclaimed is a KNOWN-ACCEPTABLE class:** polloteria `$10` = the ruling-16/26 queso upsell (explicitly tolerated as option OR description), nikkori `450` = `Botella 450 mxn` (a drink serving-FORMAT price the prompt correctly excludes, and drinks are out of gate scope), brasero-two `120`/`125` (unresolved, minor). **⚠️ INSTRUMENT BLIND SPOT, disclosed: the matcher only sees `$NNN` and `NNN mxn`. guest-house prints BARE numbers (`HALF MAINE LOBSTER TAIL 35`) so its `printed=0 / missed=0` is a FALSE CLEAN, not evidence.** Any productionised version must handle bare trailing prices before being trusted as a gate.
- **THE REAL FINDING THAT REPLACES CLASS A — the FIXTURES encode the pre-Shape-A shape while (c) produces Shape A NATIVELY.** brasero-two's `grams` "failure" is `Sencilla`/`Doble` → "item not found": the oracle expects TWO ITEMS (which is what GPT-vision and Mistral both emitted, `Churrasquería sencilla` / `Churrasquería doble`), while (c) returns ONE item + two priced options. Same story as guest-house's 3 option "FPs" (`SEAFOOD PLATEAU* → FOR TWO/FOR THREE`, `LOBSTER TAIL → chilled/grilled`, `SHRIMP → EA @7`). **Ruling 1 says (c) is RIGHT and the fixtures are behind; ruling 17 deferred promoting those pins to Phase-2 close.** So a real part of (c)'s 29/45 is FIXTURE DEBT, not model error — and promoting the Shape-A pins is a Santiago ORACLE decision that would raise the score with ZERO code change. **This also means (c) may have quietly closed Phase 2's size-variant convention gap** (ruling 1 / the ROADMAP "MANDATORY RESTORE" list), which is a scope reduction worth Santiago's attention.
- **C2 WORK LIST, corrected (no data-loss class; all remaining items are bounded and deterministic):** (B) `#`-marked dish cards become sections — REAL, 3 occurrences / 2 menus (`TACO LOIRO (sirloin)` with `picaña @165`/`pollo @150` demoted to standalone ITEMS; guest-house `SEAFOOD PLATEAU*`, `PRIME TOMAHAWK* GF, DF`); (C) letter-spaced section titles (`P O S T R E S`); (D) polloteria priced-heading FLAVOR blocks (`# AGUA $20` + 6 flavors ⇒ one item + variants — fixes items AND sections together, the only systemic overcount class); (E) el-marcos welded-composite fold, subject to the wrapped-heading REJOINING constraint; (F) combo grams SUM (known M1 bug, `Megacharola` 1200 vs 1800). **NOT code — Santiago fixture decisions:** promote the Shape-A pins (brasero-two `Sencilla`/`Doble`, guest-house ×3) per rulings 1/17; guest-house `(3g)`→`(3c)` is an OCR misread; `FOIE GRAS→side` is the known ruling-28 flake.
- **PROCESS LESSON (three matcher errors in one session — all planner-side, none the model's):** a case-insensitivity bug, a wrapped-heading false alarm, and this options-blind query. **When auditing a dump, print the WHOLE item object, not a hand-picked field list** — a projection that omits a field cannot distinguish "absent" from "not selected". All three errors pointed the same direction: they made the model look worse than it is.

## Eval 105 — GPT-vision archive comparison ($0): the worked target shape for Paletas, and TWO corrections to the C2 spec
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, C2 design. **Santiago's question: "could we get useful information from how the GPT model successfully got the Paletas and the other errors we're getting — was it capable of solving it?"** Answer: yes, decisively, at $0. GPT-4o **vision** passed polloteria ALL-5 ×3 at eval 093, so its archived dumps (`polloteria.tiles-2x2-eval093{,r2,r3}-r1.actual.json`) are a **worked example of the target shape**, not a guess.
- **FINDING 1 — the Paletas target shape, reproduced identically in r1 and r2:** `Paletas Heladas Agua | $20 | sec=Paletas Heladas | opts=[Uva, Piña, Melón, Nuez, Tamarindo, Fresa, Limón]` and `Paletas Heladas Crema | $30 | sec=Paletas Heladas | opts=[Yoghurt con Cajeta, …]`. So GPT **synthesized the item name from PARENT + child heading**, took the price from the child heading, made the flavors NULL-PRICE OPTIONS, and set `section_title` to the **PARENT** — which technically violates `EXTRACT_PROMPT`'s "each item belongs to its nearest subheading, never the parent", yet is exactly what passed the gate ×3. GPT-vision never had this failure because it saw the cards VISUALLY; (c) inherits it because the OCR markdown renders `# AGUA $20` as a heading. **⇒ Class D and Class B2 are ONE class: `#` markup turning priced dish cards into sections.** The parent heading needed to reproduce the shape IS in the Stage-1a OCR text (`# Paletas Heladas`).
- **FINDING 2 (CORRECTION to the C2 spec) — proposed option (a) for §5.1 was WRONG and is withdrawn.** The polloteria fixture's two Paletas pins are **`unchecked: true`** — per lesson 5 that is a TOLERANCE, not a requirement. So re-keying them to `AGUA`/`CREMA` would fix NOTHING: polloteria's `items` 51/40 is caused by the **12 extra flavor items**, not by a pin missing. Only the 12→2 collapse fixes the count, and it removes the 2 spurious sections as a side effect. The mechanism question is therefore settled in favour of reproducing GPT's shape from the OCR text.
- **FINDING 3 (re-characterises C2-4) — GPT never solved the combo grams either.** Its eval-093 passing dump reads `Megacharra Boneless … g=1200`, **identical to (c)'s 1200**, against a fixture that now says 1800. The 1800 value entered the fixture during **M1**, when Santiago re-adjudicated the combo as boneless 1200 + fries 600 — i.e. AFTER eval 093 had already passed against 1200. **So the grams-sum rule is new work required of ANY extractor, not a (c) regression**, and eval 093's "ALL-5" was against the older truth. Every other combo matches exactly across both extractors (`TostiBoneless` 450, `Cubeta Pollotera` 300, `El Tendedero` 750, `PapaBoneless` 600).
- **FINDING 4 — the Ensaladas fixture is fitted to GPT's NAME-WELDED Shape A.** GPT emitted `Aguacate Chicken M (150gr) | $178 | g=150 | opts=[G (300gr)@199]` (size welded into the item NAME); (c) emits the cleaner `Aguacate Chicken $178 → [M (150gr)@178, G (300gr)@199]`. Both are ruling-1 Shape A in spirit; the pins are `unchecked` so both are tolerated. Recorded because it is the same fixture-debt pattern as §5.2 (brasero-two `Sencilla`/`Doble`, guest-house ×3).
- **VERDICT: the archive is a first-class evidence source and was under-used.** One $0 query replaced a guess about the target shape with the shape itself, killed a proposed fixture change that would have achieved nothing, and reclassified a "(c) defect" as a universal unmet expectation. **Standing recommendation for future planners: before designing a fix for a class, check whether a PASSING archived dump already shows the target shape.** Spec `2026-07-29-c2-text-structuring-cleanup-design.md` updated (B+D unified under C2-3, §5.1 option (a) withdrawn, C2-4 re-characterised). $0 this leg; session live spend unchanged at **$0.296**.

## Eval 106 — generalization audit ($0): ruling-7 compliance is CLEAN, but vocabulary coupling is real and 2 of 5 keyword lists are already DEAD
- Date: 2026-07-29 | Priority Zero, ruling 30. **Santiago's question: are the evaluations tightly coupled to these 9 menus, and will a new/different menu break after all tests pass?** Plus his proposal: translate the vocabulary lists to the user's language and run the prompt translated too.
- **RULING-7 COMPLIANCE — CLEAN.** Grepping production code (`supabase/functions/`, excluding tests) for all 9 menu names + their signature dishes returns hits ONLY inside COMMENTS documenting why a rule exists (`// el-marcos "Jugos y Frutas" is 71% drink…`). Zero menu names, dish names or hardcoded counts in LOGIC. The evidence trail names the menu; the code does not. Numeric constants are plateau-validated with their evidence cited inline (`DRINK_SECTION_FRAC 0.8` between real bounds 71%/93%; `SPACED_RUN_MIN 3` plateau = its entire range; `RESCUE_DIST 0.15` mid-plateau of a 6×-wide window).
- **THE REAL LIMIT — VOCABULARY COUPLING, in production logic (es/en only):** `SERVING_FORMAT` (copa/botella/vaso/jarra/glass/bottle/chico/mediano/grande/small/large/litro/ml/mxn), `CHOICE_CONNECTOR` (`a elegir`/`choice of`/`c/`/con|de|en|with), `INLINE_DISJUNCTION` (o|u|or), `UNENUMERATED_CHOICE` (`a elegir`/`de su elección`/`of your choice`/`to choose`), `PER_UNIT_NOTE` (por|per|c/). A French/Italian/Japanese menu keeps items+prices+sections (shape-based) but loses these.
- **MEASURED FIRING RATES on the (c) path (450 items / 70 options across all 9): `SERVING_FORMAT` 0 · `UNENUMERATED_CHOICE` 0 · `INLINE_DISJUNCTION` 13 · `CHOICE_CONNECTOR` 5 · `PER_UNIT_NOTE` 1.** **Two of the five lists are COMPLETELY INERT** — the pinned model already obeys "serving formats and sizes are NOT options" unaided. **⇒ The answer to vocabulary generalization is mostly SUBTRACTION, not translation:** delete the inert lists once C2 closes (dead code that looks protective is worse than none).
- **REFRAMING THAT MATTERS: every one of these lists is a BACKSTOP for model non-compliance, not primary logic.** The primary path is the model's own reading, which is language-agnostic. So an untested language degrades GRACEFULLY (items/prices/sections survive; only the nets are absent) rather than falling off a cliff.
- **PLANNER ASSESSMENT of Santiago's proposal:** (1) **the KEY IS WRONG — it must be the MENU's language, not the USER's.** A Spanish-speaking user photographing a Japanese menu needs Japanese patterns; the lists match printed menu text, not the UI. (2) **Do NOT translate the prompt.** Each translation is a NEW prompt surface requiring its own 9-menu ×3 gate (the gate matrix multiplies by language count), against a prompt that is months-tuned and where eval 096 proved ONE sentence can regress 5/5→2/5; reward is near zero because English instructions over foreign-language input is a standard model strength, already evidenced by the same prompt handling 7 Spanish menus and 2 English ones equally. Reconsider only for a genuinely low-resource language, as a one-menu experiment. (3) If a gap survives subtraction: translate the CONCEPT TABLE (not the prompt), key off DETECTED MENU language, es/en fallback, and **validate each language against at least one real menu in that language** — an unvalidated word list is the vocabulary form of the ruling-7 "constant fitted to zero data" risk. (4) Prefer a MODEL-PROVIDED signal over any word list: `dropDrinkSections` already does this (uses the model's own `drink` labels) and is language-neutral BY CONSTRUCTION — the proven pattern for retiring the remaining three.
- **THE METHODOLOGICAL GAP WITH NO SAFEGUARD — NO HELD-OUT MENU.** All 9 menus are simultaneously the design set AND the test set. The plateau method protects numeric constants but NOT structural predicates, and **three current rules were each motivated by a SINGLE occurrence** (`P O S T R E S`, `SEAFOOD PLATEAU*`, `per oz`). Suite diversity gaps: only 2 languages; no prix-fixe, chalkboard/handwritten, CJK, right-to-left, or wine-list-as-primary. Layout diversity IS decent (dense/spacious/wide/vertical/multi-page).
- **RECOMMENDED (needs Santiago's time, not money — ~$0.03/menu):** add 2-3 **HOLDOUT menus that no rule is ever fitted against**, scored only at the C4 gate, ideally one non-es/en language and one unusual layout. Plus a $0 discipline change: every new rule records how many DISTINCT menus motivated it, so one-menu rules are visibly provisional.

## Eval 107 — C2 steps 1-3 LANDED ($0): 29/45 → 34/45; C2-3 took THREE attempts and both failures were PLANNER design errors
- Date: 2026-07-29/30 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, step C2. All validation $0 against the 9 cached (c) dumps via the new repo-resident harness `scripts/score-c-dumps.ts` (replaced the planner's ad-hoc scratchpad scorer — the M3.1 one-source-of-truth lesson). Commits `ec33c08`, `3fedf33`, `f0afcee`, `594fa95`.
- **C2 entry point:** `textStructureCleanup(items, markdown?)` in `mistral-cleanup.ts` = the (c) chain in one place: `normalizeSectionTitle` map → `dropOtherCategoryItems` → `dropDrinkSections` → `dropSelfNamedSectionTitles` → `foldSmallestOptionGrams` → `foldPerUnitNoteSections` → `foldPricedHeadingCards`. **`markdown` is OPTIONAL and omitting it is byte-identical (pinned by test)**, so every existing caller and the M3.1 gate keep working.
- **C2-1 letter-spaced titles — ADOPTED, widest possible plateau.** `normalizeSectionTitle` collapses a run of ≥`SPACED_RUN_MIN`(=3) single-character tokens. Sweeping every section title in all 9 dumps: `P O S T R E S` runs at **7**, every legitimate title (`Y`, `&`) runs at **1** — plateau is the ENTIRE 2..7 range. Fires on exactly one title suite-wide. (`GH MAC | N | CHEESE` never reaches the rule: it is an item name, not a section title.)
- **C2-2 self-named section — ADOPTED after a planner-caught correction.** v1 nulled any `section_title` equal to the item's own name and fired on polloteria's `Crispy Chicken`, a REAL printed section holding 5 items. Fix = require the section to have exactly ONE member (itself). Measured separation 1 (guest-house `SEAFOOD PLATEAU*`) vs 5. No damage had occurred (the section survived via its other 4 members) but a single-dish section elsewhere would have lost a real section claim. **`dropHeaderEchoes` in `postprocess.ts` was deliberately NOT loosened** — its `price === null` predicate is shared with the incumbent GPT path and is what keeps it safe.
- **C2 step 2 (Shape A + grams fold + per-unit) — 29/45 → 32/45.** guest-house **1→3** (`options` PASS with 0 missed/0 FP — the Shape-A pins went from `[]` to REQUIRED, i.e. strictly stricter; `section_context` PASS — C2-2 removed `SEAFOOD PLATEAU*` and the per-unit rule turned the junk section `PRIME TOMAHAWK* GF, DF`/`per oz` into an item `PRIME TOMAHAWK* GF, DF` @6.5, sec=null, NO computed total per ruling 31). brasero-two **2→3** (`grams` PASS via the smallest-option fold, `CHURRASQUERÍA`→300). `PER_UNIT_NOTE` was made `export` in `postprocess.ts` — a 1-line, behaviour-preserving change (`postprocess_test.ts` 20/20 green proves it), authorized rather than duplicating the pattern. **The executor correctly HARD-STOPPED first on the contradictory instruction "import it / don't touch that file"** — a genuine planner error in the block.
- **⚠️ THE M3.1 MISTRAL GATE MOVED 31/45 → 30/45, and it is FIXTURE-DRIVEN, NOT a code regression.** Proof: wide is unchanged at 36; the only vertical delta is brasero-two 3/5→2/5 losing exactly `section_context` — the one dim ruling 32's Shape-A truth edit touched, because Mistral-native emits `Churrasquería` as a SECTION holding two items while Shape A makes it a DISH in `Especialidades`. Mistral-native cannot satisfy a Shape-A oracle by construction, and ruling 30 retires it. **30/45 is the correct new baseline for that gate — do not read it as a defect.**
- **C2-3 priced-heading dish cards — 32/45 → 34/45, and it took THREE attempts. BOTH failures were PLANNER design errors, caught by the abort condition and the firing list, NOT by planner reasoning. See master-roadmap lessons 11-13.**
  - **v1 (32→20/45, ABORTED):** predicate "a section with ≥2 priced, description-less items" — which describes the MAJORITY of all menu sections. Folded 9 REAL sections across 6 menus: brasero `ACOMPAÑAMIENTOS` (7 dishes), guest-house `ENHANCEMENTS` (12, the ruling-28 items), nikkori `POSTRES` (the 6 desserts eval 099c fought to save), el-marcos `REVUELTOS`/`DE LA PLAYA`/`JUGOS Y FRUTAS`, brasero-two `CERDO`/`RES`/`GUARNICIONES`. Only 3 of 12 collapses were intended.
  - **Discriminators MEASURED and REJECTED along the way:** *uniform child price* fails both ways — el-marcos `DE LA PLAYA` is 4 real dishes at ONE price (false positive) while `TACO LOIRO` has two different prices (false negative); *child count* and *description-less* are properties of normal sections.
  - **v2 (31/45, ABORTED):** correct signal (heading carries its own price) matched with `.includes()` → section `VEGETARIANO` matched the ITEM heading `HONGO VEGETARIANO $285`, borrowed its price and folded 8 real mochomos dishes. **The planner's own probe had the identical substring bug and therefore reported "fires on exactly 2 headings, zero collateral" for this rule.** On mochomos nearly every DISH is a priced `#` heading, so substring matching could never survive there.
  - **v3 (34/45, ADOPTED):** heading text, minus `#`s, minus its price, must **EQUAL** the section title (normalized). Re-measured with the exact matcher: fires on exactly `AGUA` ← `# AGUA $20`, `CREMA` ← `# CREMA $30`, and `MALTEADAS` ← `# MALTEADAS $89` — and MALTEADAS is unreachable because `dropDrinkSections` removes it upstream. **Chain order is therefore LOAD-BEARING and is now pinned by a test.**
  - **RESULT: polloteria 1/5 → 3/5** — `items` **42/40 PASS** (52 − 12 flavour items + 2 cards = 42, the exact count Mistral passes at), `section_context` PASS (no spurious `AGUA`/`CREMA`), `categories` PASS. **Ruling-6 audit CLEAN:** `AGUA $20` and `CREMA $30` each retain all six flavours as OPTIONS — nothing printed lost, nothing invented.
- **STATE: 34/45.** bistro 5 · brasero 5 · casa-nostra 5 · nikkori 4 · mochomos 4 · polloteria 3 · guest-house 3 · brasero-two 3 · el-marcos 2. Suite 186 passed / 1 failed (the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` source-position assertion, unrelated — nothing imports the probe).
- **Remaining to 45, with causes:** el-marcos welded composites (C2-5) **3** · Paletas card naming (ruling 33) **1** · combo grams SUM **1** (GPT-vision fails it too, eval 105) · brasero-two `TACO LOIRO` **2** (diagnosed NOT a section case: its children come from the card's own prose `"…A elegir: (picaña $165, pollo $150)"` and arrive lowercase while every real card on that menu is UPPERCASE — an `extractInlineChoices` compliance case) · undiagnosed option FPs on polloteria/mochomos **~2** · OCR misreads (nikkori `MATERIALES`, guest-house `(3g)`→`(3c)`) + the `FOIE GRAS→side` flake **3** (tolerance decisions for Santiago, exactly as the GPT era handled its stable misreads).

## Eval 108 — ruling 33 + the polloteria option-FP fixture debt ($0): 34/45 → 35/45, and a wrong planner prediction corrected
- Date: 2026-07-30 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, step C2. All validation $0 against the 9 cached (c) dumps (`scripts/score-c-dumps.ts`). Commits `d006df5` (ruling 33), `660337d` (fixture). Planner acted as executor for this leg at Santiago's direction.
- **RULING 33 LANDED AND IS CORRECT — BUT ITS PREDICTED "+1 dim" WAS A PLANNER ERROR.** `foldPricedHeadingCards` now names a folded card `<nearest preceding UNPRICED heading> <own heading>`, walking back over priced headings. Verified on the real Stage-1a markdown: polloteria prints `[14] # Paletas Heladas`, `[15] # AGUA $20`, `[16] # CREMA $30` — so AGUA takes index 14 directly, and CREMA skips the priced 15 to reach 14. Level-walking is unusable (every polloteria heading is `#`), exactly as the ruling predicted.
- **What it actually bought: polloteria option FPs 6 → 4**, and both `name_contains: "Paletas Heladas"` pins went `NO MATCH` → matched, with no fixture change. **The score did NOT move (34/45).** Ruling 33 was scoped as "worth 1 dim (polloteria `options`)"; that assumed the two Paletas cards were what blocked the dim. They were two of SIX FPs. **The executor did nothing wrong — the prediction was the planner's.** Verified by stashing the implementation to get a true before/after rather than trusting the recorded baseline.
- **Ruling-6 audit CLEAN (whole-object, lesson 14):** the rule fires on exactly 2 cards on 1 menu across all 9 dumps; zero collateral on the other 8; all 12 flavours survive as priced options. Names land as `Paletas Heladas AGUA`/`Paletas Heladas CREMA` — uppercase, where GPT-vision's eval-093 shape was `Paletas Heladas Agua`. The scorer normalizes so it costs nothing, and uppercase card names are already normal suite-wide (`CHURRASQUERÍA`). **Benign implementation deviation recorded:** the card name is now sourced from the OCR heading text rather than the model's `section_title`. The two are token-equal by construction (`headingPrice` demands exact normalized-token equality), so casing is the only observable difference.
- **THE REAL FINDING — polloteria's 4 remaining option FPs were 100% FIXTURE DEBT. Not one was a model defect.** Adjudicated from the printed text (lesson 4), each option is printed on its own card:
  - `Gyro (300gr)` and `Tender Cordon Bleu (350gr)` → `[papas fritas, ensalada verde]`, printed **"Elige entre papas fritas o ensalada verde."** No pin existed.
  - `Chicken & Waffles Sandwich (350gr)` → `[tocino@15]`, printed **"+$15 tocino."** — precisely the ruling-16/26 printed-upsell family.
  - `Crispy Chicken` → `[Grandes (300gr)@162, Jumbo (600gr)@235]`, printed **`Grandes(300gr) $162 / Jumbo(600gr) $235`** — native ruling-1 Shape A, against a fixture still holding the pre-Shape-A pin `name_contains: "Grande"`.
  This corrects the brief, which recorded these as "undiagnosed option FPs ~2". It is 4, on polloteria alone, and all 4 are now diagnosed.
- **SANTIAGO RULED "full promotion".** Landed: `tocino` → `tolerated_option_names` (ruling 26); new pins `Crispy Chicken`, `Gyro`, `Tender Cordon Bleu`. **polloteria options FP 4 → 0, dim PASS, 3/5 → 4/5, suite 34/45 → 35/45**, no other menu moved.
- **⚠️ THE PINS ARE `unchecked` BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY DEFAULT — and this is the entry's most reusable finding.** Required pins were written and scored FIRST. They also give polloteria `options` PASS, **but they break the corrected-draft self-check: 3 missed targets.** `PolloteriaMenu.png.draft.json` records NO options on Gyro/Cordon Bleu and still names the Crispy Chicken card `Grandes (300gr) $162 / Jumbo (600gr) $235` (the draft is pre-Shape-A raw). A required pin must hold against BOTH artifacts, and `Crispy Chicken` does not exist in the draft at all — **so required pins are impossible here until the DRAFT is promoted to Shape A.** That is ruling 1's original warning ("pinning Shape A now would break fixture↔draft self-consistency") still live in the draft, even though ruling 32 closed it for the fixtures. **Open Santiago oracle call: promote `PolloteriaMenu.png.draft.json` to Shape A, which would then permit required pins.**
- **The dead `Grande` pin is KEPT, not replaced** — it is what consumes the draft's raw-named card; deleting it would turn that card into a draft FP. **`Cabeza` and `El Tender` audited and left alone:** they are GPT-vision-era MISREAD tolerances, NOT Shape-A debt (`El Tender` does not even substring-match `El Tendedero` — "el tende**d**ero" breaks the needle at the 9th char). Both are inert no-ops today; lesson 5 says do not remove a tolerance whose target a future run may re-emit.
- **Regression checks, all $0:** corrected-draft self-check still **ALL 5 DIMS PASS**; **M3.1 Mistral edge gate still 30/45** (`RUNS=1`; wide subset ×3 = 36, unchanged — unchecked pins and tolerances can only remove FPs, never create misses); scorer `--self-check` passes; suite **189 passed / 1 failed** (the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` source-position assertion — it imports `adaptiveExtraction`/`tile-cut` and zero cleanup code).

## Eval 110 — our own POSTPROCESS was destroying correct extractions ($0): 35/45 → 37/45, and the $0 harness could not see postprocess at all
- Date: 2026-07-31/08-01 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, step C2. Santiago collapsed the planner/executor split; the planner implemented directly. Commits `c0d38d6` (CP1), `2e96185` (CP2), `cf1b146` (CP3). **This eval did NOT do the planned C2-5 el-marcos work** — diagnosing el-marcos first surfaced three defects in our own code that outranked it.
- **🔑 THE HARNESS DEFECT — the most reusable finding, and it invalidated the first gate run.** `scripts/score-c-dumps.ts` scored the cached `*.dump.json`, which was **already postprocessed at probe time**, and only re-ran `mistral-cleanup`'s `textStructureCleanup`. So the $0 gate was **structurally blind to every change in `postprocess.ts`**: CP1's fixes scored a flat 35/45 while actually being worth +2. Every C2 rule so far lived in `mistral-cleanup.ts` (which IS re-run), so the gap had never shown. The harness now rebuilds from the archived RAW responses through `postprocessItems` per page → `mergeItemSources` — the order `probe-c-textstructure.ts` uses live and production uses. **Proven a strict no-op at unchanged code by stash-and-remeasure: 35/45, per-menu identical** (lesson 17). `FROM_DUMP=1` restores the old behaviour for confirming an archived number.
- **CP1 — `promoteSections` was exploding real dish CARDS (+2 dims).** The model emits brasero-two `TACO LOIRO (sirloin)` with **exactly the fixture's target shape** (null price, `options [picaña@165, pollo@150]`, sec `ESPECIALIDADES BRASERO`); `promoteSections` deleted the item and promoted its options to standalone dishes under a spurious section. Same shape on guest-house `PRIME TOMAHAWK*`. **This refutes the eval-107/108 diagnosis** that brasero-two's 2 dims were "an `extractInlineChoices` compliance case — children arrive lowercase": that read the POST-processed dump and blamed the model for our own damage. Discriminator adopted: **a folded section is a bare name + children; a dish card carries its own description.** New `foldPerUnitPrice` lifts a printed rate onto the item before `filterServingFormatOptions` drops the per-unit option and its price with it — without it, sparing `PRIME TOMAHAWK*` LOST its printed 6.50 (a scorer-invisible regression: no pin asserts that price). It now matches the corrected draft exactly (price 6.5, sec `BUTCHER'S BEST`). **brasero-two 3/5 → 5/5.** Firing list: exactly **2 items across all 9 menus**, 7 byte-identical.
- **CP2 — `foldVariantCards` was silently DELETING a real printed dish (0 dims, and worth doing anyway).** el-marcos prints two separate egg cards, `REVUELTOS` and `FRITOS`, each listing `Dos huevos naturales 78`. The fold keyed on `name#category` and **ignored `section_title`**, so both FRITOS lines folded into their REVUELTOS namesakes and the card vanished. **The model read it correctly**; the scorer reported `Fritos→(item not found)` as though the model had missed it. Section added to the fold key. **The score does NOT move and el-marcos's detail gets nominally WORSE** (items 35→37/28, "2 duplicates", spurious `FRITOS`) — the restored lines still need the card-collapse rule. **Recorded deliberately: a rule can LOWER the total while restoring real dishes, the inverse of lesson 11.** Firing list: exactly **2 items, 1 menu**, 8 byte-identical.
- **CP3 — the `EA` per-unit note + two ORACLE edits; the drafts guard is GREEN.** Santiago approved each after photo adjudication. (a) `GuestHouseMenu.png.draft.json` gains the printed choices it held only as description text: `SEAFOOD PLATEAU* → [FOR TWO@150, FOR THREE OR MORE@280]`, `HALF MAINE LOBSTER TAIL → [chilled, grilled]`. (b) The `HALF MAINE LOBSTER TAIL` pin → `unchecked` (ruling 34 — "chilled or grilled" is WORDING); `SEAFOOD PLATEAU*` stays REQUIRED (two printed portions + two prices is STRUCTURE). (c) **The `MEXICAN WHITE SHRIMP` pin is DELETED — the FIXTURE was wrong, not the draft.** `7 EA` is a printed rate, the same family as `6.50 PER OZ`, and **ruling 31 says a per-unit rate is the item's price and never an option**; the draft already said `price 7 / options []`. Eval 107's pin contradicted ruling 31 on the same menu. `PER_UNIT_NOTE` now matches a bare `ea`/`ea.` (anchored; `Ealing`/`Easy`/`Tea`/`Sea Bass` pinned as non-matches). **Measured: neither oracle edit alone fixes the guard** (draft-only 1 missed target, fixture-only 1) — both were needed. **`drafts_test.ts` 6 passed / 0 failed, red since eval 107.** Firing list: exactly **1 option, 1 menu**, 8 byte-identical.
- **PLANNER ERROR CAUGHT IN-FLIGHT, disclosed (lesson 12 again).** The first simulation prefixed item names with their `section_title` using a SPACE separator to emulate a section-scoped fold key; section titles contain spaces, so the strip corrupted names (`BUTCHER'S BEST PRIME TOMAHAWK*` → `BEST PRIME TOMAHAWK*`) and produced a fake "guest-house 3/5 → 2/5 regression" for rule A. Fixed with a `\x01` separator and re-measured. **An approximate probe validates an approximate rule — including the planner's own probe, for the third time in this project.**
- **Regressions, all $0:** harness suite (`deno test -A scripts supabase`) **208 passed / 1 failed** — the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` dimension assertion, which imports only `tile-cut`/`adaptiveExtraction` and nothing this eval touched; scorer `--self-check` passes; `postprocess.ts --self-check` passes; **retired M3.1 Mistral-native gate 29, UNCHANGED** (ruling 30 retires that architecture — record only, never a gate).
- **STATE: 37/45.** bistro 5 · brasero 5 · casa-nostra 5 · **brasero-two 5** · nikkori 4 · mochomos 4 · polloteria 4 · guest-house 3 · el-marcos 2. **Four menus now ALL-5.** **Remaining to 45:** el-marcos **3** (see below) · combo grams SUM **1** (GPT-vision fails it too, eval 105) · mochomos residual option FPs **~1** · OCR misreads (nikkori `MATERIALES`, guest-house `(3g)`→`(3c)`) + the `FOIE GRAS→side` ruling-28 flake **3** (Santiago tolerance calls).
- **⚠️ THE "C2-5 el-marcos = +3" PREDICTION IS WITHDRAWN AS UNMEASURED (lesson 16).** el-marcos is NOT a "welded composite name" problem. Its real shape is ONE general pattern — **a card title followed by ≥2 priced variant lines** — occurring six times: `REVUELTOS` (3 lines), `FRITOS` (2), `HOT CAKES (3 piezas)` (2), `WAFFLES` (2), `CHILAQUILES (70gr.)` (3 preparations), `PLATO SURTIDO (350gr.)` (2). That is the SAME family as C2-3's `foldPricedHeadingCards`, except the card title here is often UNPRICED. It needs its own design pass, a predeclared abort condition and a full firing list before any number enters a ruling. $0 this leg; session live spend unchanged at **$0.296**.

## Eval 110b — the harness-blindness class is now STRUCTURALLY prevented, not just fixed ($0)
- Date: 2026-08-01 | Follow-up to eval 110 at Santiago's direction: *"avoid again that score-c-dumps.ts was scoring the cached .dump.json — here and in future LLM sessions."* Eval 110 fixed the symptom but left the footgun: the `.dump.json` read survived behind a `FROM_DUMP=1` opt-out.
- **AUDIT FIRST — only ONE harness had the defect.** `replay-edge-mistral.ts` is correct BY CONSTRUCTION (it feeds cached RAW into `runPagedExtraction`, the real edge entry point) — that is the pattern to copy. The `.actual.json` readers in `probe-nested-tile-eval072`/`probe-paired-ambiguity`/`probe-tile-ocr-eval079` are archive ANALYSIS, not gates; that use is legitimate and was left alone.
- **THE FOOTGUN IS DELETED, not flagged.** `score-c-dumps.ts` no longer reads `.dump.json` at all and `FROM_DUMP` is gone — there is no stale path left to select. `image_quality` was the only other thing the dump supplied; it is **inert here** (not in `DIMS`) and `archivePayloads` hardcoded `{usable:true, issues:[]}` into every archived dump anyway, so passing that constant is provably identical to the old behaviour. `itemsFromRaw` gained one optional `transform` parameter whose ONLY purpose is the guard below.
- **🔑 THE GUARD — a SENSITIVITY test, and it was verified to FAIL.** `score-c-dumps_test.ts` now has two tests: (1) inject a stub `transform` that returns `[]` and assert the harness output is empty — if the pipeline ever reads a pre-processed artifact again the stub is ignored and items come back non-empty; (2) assert real-vs-identity transform DIFFER on brasero-two, catching the inverse mistake (wiring straight to raw and skipping postprocess entirely). **Both were proven RED by deliberately reintroducing the exact eval-110 bug into `itemsFromRaw`, then restored** — a guard never seen to fail is not a guard. Failure messages name the class and cite eval 110; assertion (2) compares a boolean so a failure prints the reason instead of two full item dumps.
- **WRITTEN RULES for the path no test can cover** — a FUTURE harness (C3/C4) making the same mistake: brief operating rule **8** (with the good/bad patterns named) and **master-roadmap lesson 20** (primary folder (`feat/selectable-options`), `8644c85`). Lesson 20 records the diagnostic tell: **a change you can prove correct — by unit test and by hand-inspecting the dump — that moves the score by exactly zero.** It also notes this is the mirror image of lesson 9: there the stale artifact was from a different RUN, here from a different VERSION OF THE CODE.
- **Regressions:** gate **37/45**, per-menu identical to eval 110; harness suite **210 passed / 1 failed** (the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` dimension assertion); `drafts_test` 6/6. $0; session live spend unchanged at **$0.296**.

## Eval 110c — the eval-110 class is now guarded for harnesses THAT DO NOT EXIST YET ($0)
- Date: 2026-08-01 | Santiago: *"lets proceed to the safer fix for new harnesses."* Eval 110b hardened `score-c-dumps.ts` itself, but protection for a FUTURE gate (C3 edge port, C4 live gate) was only brief rule 8 + master-roadmap lesson 20 — words, and eval 109 already established that a TEST beats a written rule.
- **NEW: `scripts/gate-artifacts_test.ts` — an architecture fitness test.** Predicate, chosen by measuring rather than guessing: a file is suspect iff it **both** calls `scoreMenu` **and** mentions a derived artifact (`.dump.json` / `.actual.json` / `clean.dump`). Measured across `scripts/` + `supabase/functions/`: 15 files touch a derived artifact and 9 score, but the **intersection is only 6** — small enough for an annotated allowlist, and precise enough that write-after-score probes are not noise. Each allowlist entry carries a REASON (4 only WRITE dumps after scoring a LIVE run; `probe-nested-tile-eval072` reads a frozen eval-068 archive as a historical baseline; `score-c-dumps.ts` mentions the suffixes only in the comment explaining why it must not read them).
- **VERIFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, both halves.** (a) Dropped in a plausible `fake-c3-gate.ts` that scores a `.dump.json` → the test names it and prints the rebuild-from-raw instruction; removed → green. (b) Added a bogus allowlist key → the stale-entry assertion flags it; removed → green. A guard never seen to fail is not a guard.
- **Why this predicate and not a source grep for `.dump.json`:** 15 files mention the suffixes, most of them WRITERS. An allowlist of 15 is noise that invites blind appending; requiring `scoreMenu` too narrows it to the actual hazard — *scoring* stale data. Deliberately NOT attempted: read-vs-write detection by regex (path constants like `probe-nested-tile`'s `ARCHIVE` are declared far from their read, so any window heuristic misses them — the same approximate-matcher trap as lessons 12/110).
- **Residual limit, disclosed:** the test cannot force a new harness to carry its own SENSITIVITY guard (the injected-stub test in `score-c-dumps_test.ts`); it only forces an explicit, reasoned act when a gate touches a derived artifact. The allowlist reason text and lesson 20 carry that instruction.
- **Regressions:** gate **37/45** per-menu identical; suite **211 passed / 1 failed** (the pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` dimension assertion); `drafts_test` 6/6. $0; session live spend unchanged at **$0.296**.

## Eval 111 — multi-version card fold ($0): 37/45 → 39/45, el-marcos 2/5 → 4/5, and the REFUSALS are the deliverable
- Date: 2026-08-01 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, step C2. Split collapsed; planner implemented. Commit `bc30815`. Santiago approved after a plain-language brief, with one explicit condition: *"lets be sure this is not a solution that works for el-marcos only but breaks everything else."*
- **THE CLASS.** el-marcos prints cards listing ONE dish with several versions (`WAFFLES` / `Con plátano… 70` / `Con Frutos rojos 78`). The model returns two dishes, in TWO different shapes: **welded** (`WAFFLES Con Frutos rojos`) and **section-ified** (card title becomes `section_title`, e.g. `REVUELTOS`/`FRITOS`). Both are one printed dish with priced choices (ruling 1). Six occurrences: REVUELTOS, FRITOS, CHILAQUILES (70gr.), HOT CAKES (3 piezas), WAFFLES, PLATO SURTIDO (350gr.).
- **⚠️ THE ROADMAP'S "+3 welded composites" WAS WRONG ON BOTH COUNTS (lesson 16).** It is worth **+2**, not +3 (items + section_context; `options` does not flip), and it is NOT a "welded composite name" problem — that framing describes only 4 of the 6, and misses that the other 2 arrive as fake sections needing a different predicate.
- **🔑 THE HARD PART IS REFUSING, NOT FOLDING — and the firing list is the deliverable (lessons 11-13).** Menus constantly list distinct dishes sharing a first word. **Measured: an unguarded prefix rule fires 51 times across the 9 fixtures and would merge real dishes on 7 of them** (casa-nostra `Gnocchi alla sorrentina`+`Gnocchi toscano`, brasero `PASTA AL PESTO`+`PASTA ALFREDO`, mochomos' entire `TACOS`/`TOSTADAS`/`CARPACCIO` families, brasero-two `TACO` ×11, bistro `ENSALADA` ×4).
- **TWO GUARDS, BOTH LAYOUT NOT VOCABULARY** (so the rule carries no language assumption — better than the es/en word lists eval 106 flagged): (1) a real section is a `#` heading in the OCR, a card title is plain text; (2) a version is printed on a line of its OWN, while a distinct dish prints its FULL name on every line, so its suffix never opens a line. **Guarded: 6 firings, all el-marcos, all photo-checked. The other 8 menus are BYTE-IDENTICAL.**
- **THE FIVE REFUSALS ARE PINNED AS TESTS — that, not the score, is the answer to Santiago's condition.** casa-nostra Gnocchi · mochomos `TOSTADAS DE ATÚN` vs `TOSTADAS DE ATÚN AL AJONJOLÍ` · polloteria `Paletas Heladas AGUA`/`CREMA` (versions that are their own `#` headings — ruling 33 survives) · el-marcos `MACHACA` across two sections · a real `#` section. **Two of those would otherwise have re-merged the FRITOS card eval 110 had just restored.** A future loosening turns them red.
- **Chain order is load-bearing and test-pinned:** the section-shaped fold runs BEFORE the welded fold, which is what removes REVUELTOS/FRITOS' duplicate child names before the prefix rule could see them as one card.
- **A SCORER-INVISIBLE DEFECT CAUGHT BY THE WHOLE-OBJECT AUDIT (lesson 14):** the first implementation set the folded card's `description` to `""`, silently losing CHILAQUILES' printed prose (*"Tradicionales. Con pollo, crema y queso gratinado…"*). No dim moved — description is ungated — but it is what the diner reads. Fixed to keep the base version's description.
- **TWO PLANNER PROBE BUGS, both caught by re-measuring (lesson 12, now the fourth time in this project):** (a) v1 compared OCR headings to section titles WITHOUT `normalizeSectionTitle`, so `# PolloKids` ≠ "Pollo Kids" and `P O S T R E S` ≠ "POSTRES" — it reported polloteria `Pollo Kids` and brasero-two `POSTRES` as fold candidates when both are real sections; (b) v1's grouping excluded the base card whose name IS the prefix, hiding `CHILAQUILES (70gr.)` and `PLATO SURTIDO (350gr.)`. A third, smaller one: the suffix matcher first demanded a SPACE after the suffix, which missed `Regionales. Con pollo…`; fixed by matching on `norm` TOKENS instead of raw string boundaries — the same matcher family the rest of the module uses.
- **RULING-7 CLEAN:** no menu or dish name appears in logic, only in comments documenting why each rule exists.
- **el-marcos residual — `options` 1 missed / 1 FP, BOTH PRE-EXISTING and untouched by this fold** (diagnosed, both are Santiago oracle calls, neither is a code defect): (i) `Machaca de Marlín c/huevo o verdura` yields only `huevo` — the choice sits in the item's NAME while `extractInlineChoices` reads descriptions (the Mexicanos `MACHACA` gets both, because its choice is in the description); (ii) the `PA' LOS BUKIS` kids combo correctly extracts the printed choice `tocino`, but no pin covers it. Santiago separately ruled that `frutos rojos` vs `Con Frutos rojos` wording does not matter — and the measurement showed that pin was never the problem.
- **Regressions:** gate **39/45**; suite **219 passed / 1 failed** (pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts` dimension assertion); `drafts_test` 6/6; `gate-artifacts_test` and `score-c-dumps_test` green. $0; session live spend unchanged at **$0.296**.
- **STATE: 39/45.** bistro 5 · brasero 5 · casa-nostra 5 · brasero-two 5 · nikkori 4 · mochomos 4 · polloteria 4 · **el-marcos 4** · guest-house 3. **Remaining to 45:** combo grams SUM **1** (GPT-vision fails it too) · mochomos residual option FPs **~1** · el-marcos options **1** (the two oracle calls above) · OCR misreads + the `FOIE GRAS→side` flake **3** (Santiago tolerance calls). **Realistic code ceiling now ≈41/45; the rest are oracle/tolerance decisions.** Next: **C3** edge port, then **C4** the first 9-menu ×3 LIVE gate. **STILL OWED: ×3 determinism, and a HELD-OUT menu** — eval 111 makes that more pressing, not less: its guards are proven to leave 8 menus untouched, but every positive example comes from ONE menu.

## Eval 112 — a name that spells out a choice is not self-decomposition ($0): el-marcos ALL-5, 39/45 → 40/45
- Date: 2026-08-01 | Priority Zero, ruling 30, step C2. Commit `038464d`. Santiago: *"I would prefer that 'verdura' in 'Machaca de Marlín c/huevo o verdura' was to be read. I'm okay if 'tocino' is read but its price is not shown."*
- **THE MODEL HAD READ `verdura` ALL ALONG — our postprocess dropped it.** Raw output: `options: [huevo, verdura]`. Bisecting the chain put it on `filterServingFormatOptions` → `echoesOwnItemName`, which drops an option whose words appear in the item's OWN name. **This is the THIRD time in one session that a recorded "model defect" was our own code** (after `promoteSections` exploding brasero-two's `TACO LOIRO` and `foldVariantCards` deleting el-marcos's `FRITOS`) — now master-roadmap **lesson 21**.
- **THE FIX REUSES AN EXISTING RULING RATHER THAN INVENTING VOCABULARY.** `echoesOwnItemName` is correct for self-decomposition (`Omelette de Camarón **y** Marlin` → fake options `[Camarón, Marlin]`, eval 052) and wrong when the name spells out a printed CHOICE (`…c/huevo **o** verdura`). **Master-roadmap lesson 4 already draws that line** — y/and joins ingredients, o/or marks a choice — and `INLINE_DISJUNCTION` already holds the words. So the exemption is: an option adjacent to a disjunction word in the item name is a choice, not an echo.
- **FIRING LIST across all 9 menus:** `echoesOwnItemName` drops **21** options; the exemption keeps **16**; **every one of the 16 is a choice the menu really prints** (`Con jamón, chorizo o tocino`; `queso cottage o yogurth`; the Pa' los Bukis kids card's six). The two classes it must NOT touch are untouched and pinned as tests: the eval-052 `Omelette … y Marlin` self-decomposition, and guest-house's `PARMESAN-CRUSTED FILET* 7 OZ / 10 OZ` sizes — neither sits beside an o/or.

## Eval 113 — C3 edge port DESIGNED + baseline independently re-measured ($0, no code)

Matches eval 112 exactly. This is the number C3 must reproduce.

Measured: per-page vs post-merge cleanup is a STRICT NO-OP on the 9 fixtures, both orders score 40/45, and the guard described below does NOT catch it. Kept verbatim as the record of a wrong prediction.] THE HIGHEST-RISK DETAIL IS THE CHAIN ORDER.** The 40/45 was produced by `score-c-dumps.ts`, which runs `postprocessItems` PER PAGE → `mergeItemSources` → `textStructureCleanup` **ONCE** over the `"\n"`-joined markdown of ALL pages. The current edge `runPagedExtraction` instead cleans PER PAGE — correct for the OLD path, whose `mistralCleanup` keyed on that photo's own `blocks`, and wrong for this one. A port that keeps the old order will move dims for a reason unrelated to quality. The spec pins the harness order and adds a guard test asserting the edge's items are JSON-identical to the harness's on brasero-two + polloteria, so the order cannot silently drift again.

C3 therefore just stops fetching blocks; the functions stay in place (prove-then-remove). Consequence: `MENU_ANNOTATION_SCHEMA`/`reshapeMistral`/`mistralPage`/`extractMistral*` and the retired M3.1 gate `scripts/replay-edge-mistral.ts` are deleted together — verified by grep that nothing else imports them (`probe-bakeoff-mistral-b1.ts` carries its own private schema and fetch).

It removes the silent-swap exposure that cost evals 101/102. His question ("does Mistral even give us access to different models?") exposed it. Recorded because the pattern — offering two options that both cost something while a $0 one exists — is the same shape as the probe bugs in lesson 23.
- **GATE DESIGN OBEYS LESSON 20 BY CONSTRUCTION:** `scripts/replay-edge-c3.ts` reads ONLY `*.raw.json` for BOTH stages (cached `b1` OCR + cached `eval103c-m41` structuring), feeds them through the real edge `runPagedExtraction`, and must reproduce exactly 40/45. Two sensitivity guards, and the executor must be SEEN to make each one RED before it counts: (1) replay output ≠ raw parsed items (the chain really runs); (2) replay output === the harness's `cleanForScore(itemsFromRaw(...))` output (the edge matches what was measured).
