## Eval 115 — C4 ×3 LIVE GATE: **FAILED 0/3**, live is **31-33/45 not 40/45**, and the extractor is **NOT deterministic** (~$0.30 est.)

- **The first-ever 9-menu ×3 LIVE gate under the (c) architecture. It did not pass, and the result is worth more than a pass would have been.** `EVAL_RUNS=3 scripts/eval-027-live.ts`, all 9 fixtures, 5 GATE_DIMS + routing assert, 30 OCR + 30 structuring calls. Log `/tmp/c4-gate.log`; items archived as `*.eval027-r{1,2,3}.actual.json`.
- **PER-RUN TOTALS: 33 / 31 / 33 out of 45. The cached-replay baseline is 40/45.** The gate verdict is `0/3 consecutive passing runs`. **The binary FAIL was expected and predeclared** (the gate demands 5/5 on every menu while we sit at 40/45 with five known-failing dims) — what was NOT expected is the 7-9 dim gap between live and cached.

| menu | r1 | r2 | r3 | cached | |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| bistro | 5 | 5 | 5 | 5 | stable |
| brasero | 5 | 5 | 5 | 5 | stable |
| mochomos | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | stable |
| nikkori | 4 | 4 | 4 | 4 | stable |
| casa-nostra | 4 | 4 | 4 | **5** | stable, but below cached |
| brasero-two | 2 | 2 | 2 | **5** | stable, but 3 dims below cached |
| el-marcos | 4 | **5** | 4 | 5 | **UNSTABLE** |
| polloteria | 3 | 2 | 2 | **4** | **UNSTABLE** |
| guest-house | 2 | **0** | 3 | 3 | **UNSTABLE** |

- **⚠️ THE ×3 DETERMINISM DEBT IS NOW PAID, AND THE ANSWER IS NO.** Five dims flip across three identical runs: `el-marcos.options`, `guest-house.items`, `guest-house.section_context`, `guest-house.categories`, `polloteria.items`. **guest-house swings 0/5 → 3/5.** Its `items` dim reads **48/48, then 36/48, then 48/48** — the same menu, the same pinned snapshot, the same request. **A PINNED MODEL IS NOT A DETERMINISTIC ONE (master-roadmap lesson 1, now demonstrated rather than warned about).** Ruling 30 said exactly this — "pinning kills substitution, NOT variance" — and this is the first time we have measured the variance it leaves behind. The 12-item drop is the long-list laziness class eval 103 saw kill `gpt-4o-2024-11-20`; `gpt-4.1` does it less often, not never.
- **⚠️ THE 40/45 WAS MEASURED ON AN INPUT PRODUCTION NO LONGER SENDS — a concrete, checkable cause, NOT yet confirmed.** The cached `b1` OCR text every offline score rests on was produced from a **2048px q95 re-encode** (`probe-bakeoff-mistral-b1.ts:157`), while the live gate sends the **passthrough ORIGINAL** (`productionPhotoData`, the ticket-#3 fidelity fix). Different image ⇒ different OCR text ⇒ the C2 folds, which key on exact heading rendering, do not fire the same way. **Evidence: brasero-two.** Offline the `CHURRASQUERÍA` card folds to Shape A and scores 5/5. Live, all three runs return the PRE-Shape-A form — `CHURRASQUERÍA` as a SECTION holding `SENCILLA (300gr) 495` and `DOBLE (600gr) 950` as separate items — which is exactly C1's "defect A", the thing `foldPricedHeadingCards` exists to fix. The fold did not fire. **Stated as a hypothesis with a named decisive test (lesson 16), not as a finding: I cannot yet distinguish "the OCR text differs" from "the model grouped differently", because THE LIVE GATE DOES NOT ARCHIVE RAW RESPONSES.**
- **🔑 PROCESS DEFECT FOUND, AND IT MUST BE FIXED BEFORE C4 IS RE-RUN: `eval-027-live.ts` archives only `.actual.json` — the SCORED ITEMS — and throws the raw model responses away.** So a failure this expensive cannot be diagnosed without paying for it again. That is the same family as master-roadmap lessons 20/21: the raw response is the only artifact that can tell you whether the model or our own code caused a defect, and here it does not exist. `runPagedExtraction` now returns both stages in `raw_response`; the gate must write it. **Do not re-run C4 until it does.**
- **NOT a C3 port defect, on the evidence available:** the replay gate drives the SAME edge code and reproduces 40/45 exactly, per-menu. The code did not change between the two measurements — only the input text did. This is why eval 101 ruled that C4 must be live and never against cached dumps; that ruling has now paid for itself twice.
- **Ruling-6 audit, run 1:** no unprinted/invented items surfaced in the failing dims — every failure is a MISSED target, a false-positive OPTION, or a mis-mapped section, not a fabricated dish. `brasero-two` loses `Churrasquería` to the unfolded shape; `guest-house` r2 loses 12 real dishes to the truncation flake; `nikkori` gains a spurious `BOLOS` section title (r1) on top of the known `MATERIALES` misread.
- **Cost ~$0.30 estimated, not invoice-verified** (30 structuring + 30 OCR calls; C1's measured $0.091 per 9-menu structuring pass ×3). Running session spend ~**$0.33**; running total ~**$0.63**.
- **STALE DOC CORRECTED:** the brief said the OpenAI key is Tier-1 at **30k TPM / 90k TPD**, which would have made this 30-call campaign impossible. Measured from the live rate-limit headers before spending: **450,000 TPM / 5,000 RPM, no daily cap in the headers.** No throttling risk; the warning was months out of date.
- **NEXT — Santiago's call, options in the writeup.** The cheap decisive probe is ~$0.01: OCR `brasero-two` BOTH ways (2048/q95 like the cache, and passthrough like production) and diff the two markdowns against the cached one. That single diff separates "our offline baseline is measuring the wrong input" from "the model varies", and it must happen before any rule is touched. **Do NOT tune a fold to make brasero-two pass live until the input question is settled** — that would be fitting rules to a moving target.

## Eval 116 — the eval-115 input hypothesis is **REFUTED** (~$0.004): the OCR text is identical; the STRUCTURING model is the whole story

- **⚠️ THIS OVERTURNS THE CENTRAL HYPOTHESIS I RECORDED IN EVAL 115 ONE HOUR EARLIER.** That entry named "the 40/45 was measured on an input production no longer sends" as the likely cause of the live-vs-cached gap, and flagged it as a hypothesis with a decisive test. **The test says no.** Probe: `scripts/probe-ocr-input-fidelity.ts` (4 Mistral OCR calls on brasero-two).
- **MEASURED — all three OCR texts are the same size and carry the same heading:**

| markdown source | chars | `churrasqu` heading | fold fires? |
|---|---|---|---|
| cached July `b1` (2048/q95 re-encode) | 3967 | `# CHURRASQUERÍA` | **FIRES** |
| fresh OCR of the 2048/q95 re-encode | 3967 | `# CHURRASQUERÍA` | **FIRES** |
| fresh OCR of the passthrough ORIGINAL (what production sends) | 3967 | `# CHURRASQUERÍA` | **FIRES** |

  ⇒ **the input image does NOT change the OCR text, and the OCR model has NOT drifted since July.** Ruling 35's pin is doing its job. The compression-fidelity difference between the archive and production, which looked like an obvious culprit, is a non-issue at the OCR layer.
- **THE BISECT THAT LOCATES IT INSTEAD — same markdown, different items:**
  - cached items + cached markdown ⇒ **fold FIRES**
  - live items + cached markdown ⇒ **fold does NOT fire**
  - cached items + live markdown ⇒ **fold FIRES**
  ⇒ **the ITEM LIST is the variable, not the text.** brasero-two's live runs return 47 items where the archived response gave 43, and the CHURRASQUERÍA children are byte-identical in both — so a difference ELSEWHERE in the list is what stops `foldUnpricedCardSections` from firing. (Note `foldPricedHeadingCards` was never the relevant rule: `# CHURRASQUERÍA` carries no price. Eval 115 named the wrong fold as well as the wrong cause.)
- **🔑 THE REAL FINDING, AND IT SUBSUMES BOTH EVAL-115 HEADLINES INTO ONE CAUSE: `gpt-4.1` returns a different — also valid — item list run to run, and our deterministic folds are BRITTLE to that variation.** Every C2 rule was designed and tuned against exactly ONE archived response per menu. The rules are not wrong; they are fitted to one sample of a distribution we had never sampled twice. That single cause explains BOTH the 7-9 dim live-vs-cached gap AND the run-to-run instability (guest-house 48→36→48). **It also means the offline 40/45 is not a measurement of the extractor — it is a measurement of the extractor on one frozen draw.**
- **CONSEQUENCE FOR METHOD, not just for a fix:** the whole C2 loop optimised against a single cached response per menu, which is a design set of size one per fixture. This is the eval-106 held-out-menu gap in a second dimension — we lack held-out MENUS *and* held-out DRAWS. Any rule whose firing depends on incidental structure of one response will keep doing this.
- **PLANNER ERRORS DISCLOSED (both mine, both this session):** (1) the eval-115 input hypothesis, stated with a named mechanism and refuted within the hour — master-roadmap lesson 24 was written by me earlier TODAY about exactly this failure mode and I repeated it; the saving grace is that it was labelled a hypothesis with a decisive test attached, and the test was cheap and ran first. (2) The probe archived both pages to ONE filename (no page index), so page 1 overwrote page 0; a follow-up read then analysed half the menu and reported a bogus "markdown differs, 3967 vs 1810 chars". **The in-memory comparison in the table above was unaffected** (it never round-tripped through the archive), but the bug is fixed and called out in the code — lesson 23's "if a probe surprises you, suspect the probe" caught it within one command.
- **NOT CHANGED, deliberately:** no rule was touched. Eval 115's standing instruction holds — do not tune a fold to make brasero-two pass live until the variation question has a policy. Cost ~$0.004; running total ~**$0.64**.

## Eval 117 — 3 DRAWS × 9 MENUS (~$0.27): **the 40/45 was a lucky sample.** True offline range **33-37/45**, and the live-vs-offline mystery mostly DISSOLVES

- **27 archived structuring responses, 0 failures** (`TAG=eval117`, `RUN=1..3`, pinned `gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, Stage-1a served from the cached OCR text eval 116 proved input-invariant). New harness `scripts/score-c-draws.ts` reports a RANGE, which dims FLIP, and item-count spread. Capture gained `RUN=N` (`probe-c-textstructure.ts`) and `itemsFromRaw` gained a `run` parameter, defaulting to 1 so every existing caller and test is untouched (15 tests green).
- **PER-DRAW TOTALS 34 / 36 / 36. WORST 33/45, BEST 37/45.** ⇒ **THE 40/45 THAT THE ENTIRE C2 STEP WAS STEERED BY IS NOT REPRODUCIBLE — it is better than the BEST of three fresh draws.** The `eval103c-m41` archive was a favourable sample on at least two menus (brasero-two 5/5 where fresh draws give 5/5,5/5 but also 2/5; guest-house 3/5 where all three fresh draws give 2/5). Every "+N dims" recorded across evals 107-114 was measured against that one lucky draw.
- **🔑 THE LIVE-VS-OFFLINE MYSTERY LARGELY DISSOLVES.** Eval 115 reported a 7-9 dim gap between live (33/31/33) and offline (40). Against a properly sampled offline baseline (34/36/36) the gap is **~2 dims, not 9** — and the remaining sliver is the expected difference between cached and fresh OCR on the menus eval 116 did not check. **There was never a live-vs-offline defect; there was a sampling error, and it was ours.**
- **ONLY 2 OF 9 MENUS ARE BRITTLE — that is the good news, and it makes the work tractable:**
  - **polloteria `items` 42 / 52 / 52 (±10).** The 42-item draw PASSES; both 52-item draws FAIL. The +10 is the known Paletas class (a priced-heading card whose 12 printed flavours become standalone dishes instead of options). So the fold that was supposed to fix it fires on ONE draw in three.
  - **brasero-two 47 / 43 / 43 (±4).** The 47-item draw scores 2/5 and takes `options`, `section_context` AND `grams` down with it; the two 43-item draws score 5/5. **The live C4 runs all drew the 47 shape** — which is exactly why live looked catastrophically worse there.
  - The other seven menus are **identical across all three draws**, item-for-item. The model is not uniformly unstable; it is unstable on two specific card layouts.
- **⚠️ THE guest-house SHORT READ DID NOT REPRODUCE.** Live it read 48 → **36** → 48 dishes; across three offline draws it reads 48, 48, 48. So the silent-data-loss class is **rarer than 1-in-3 and remains UNDIAGNOSED**. It is not visible in this corpus, and it cannot be chased without the live raw responses the gate still throws away. **That archiving fix is now the blocker for the only release-blocking defect we have** (Santiago ruled the class release-blocking at eval 115).
- **METHOD CHANGE, effective now:** `scripts/score-c-draws.ts` replaces single-draw scoring as the measurement of record. **Never quote a single-draw number as the extractor's quality again** — report the range (master-roadmap lesson 25). `score-c-dumps.ts` stays as the single-draw tool the guards and the C3 replay gate are pinned to.
- Cost ~$0.27 (27 structuring calls, Stage-1a free from cache). Running total ~**$0.91**.
- **NEXT, in this order (planner recommendation):** (1) $0 — make `eval-027-live.ts` archive raw responses; without it the release-blocking short read is undiagnosable. (2) $0 — re-derive the polloteria and brasero-two folds against ALL THREE draws instead of one, using the firing-list discipline (lessons 11-13, 22); these two menus are worth up to 4 dims and are the only brittle ones. (3) Only then re-run C4, and gate on the RANGE, not on a single pass.

## Eval 118 — diagnosing the two brittle menus ($0): one is a RULE keyed on the wrong thing, the other is NOT FIXABLE by a layout rule

- **polloteria — the fold is keyed on something the model varies, and that is repairable.** All three draws return **95 pre-cleanup items**; cleanup then yields **42 / 52 / 52**. So the MODEL is stable here and OUR RULE is what differs. Cause: `foldPricedHeadingCards` groups by the model's `section_title`. Draw 1 labels the ice-cream flavours `section_title:"AGUA"`/`"CREMA"`/`"MALTEADAS"` and the fold fires (ruling 33 → `Paletas Heladas Agua` + 6 flavour options). **Draws 2 and 3 label the identical items `section_title: null`**, the fold skips null sections entirely, and 18 flavours survive as standalone dishes ⇒ 52 items, `items` FAILS. **The printed markdown is identical in all three draws (`# AGUA $20` followed by plain flavour lines) — it is only the model's grouping choice that moved.** ⇒ **PROPOSED FIX: key the fold on the MARKDOWN's heading structure (which printed lines fall under which heading) instead of on the model's `section_title`.** That is master-roadmap lesson 25(c) applied literally — prefer a rule keyed on what the model cannot vary. Needs the full firing-list discipline across 9 menus × 3 draws before adoption (lessons 11-13, 22); NOT yet built.
- **brasero-two — PLANNER RECOMMENDATION: DO NOT BUILD A RULE FOR THIS.** The card prints as:

  ```
  # CHURRASQUERÍA
  SENCILLA (300gr) $495          <- variant of the card
  DOBLE (600gr) $950             <- variant of the card
  EN TACO: …  /  EN TOSTADA: …   <- serving styles
  PÍDELO CON QUESO: … por $10.   <- upsell
  TACO JR SIRLOIN (50gr) $90     <- a DIFFERENT dish
  QUESADILLA (30gr) $60          <- a DIFFERENT dish
  ```

  The truth (which draws 2/3 satisfy at 5/5) folds the first five into a Shape-A card and keeps the last two as separate dishes. **All seven print identically — plain lines under one heading, most in `NAME (WEIGHT) $PRICE` form. There is NO layout discriminator between them.** The only thing separating `SENCILLA` from `QUESADILLA` is that one is a size word and the other is a different food — **vocabulary, which ruling 7 forbids and lesson 22 explicitly deprioritises against layout.** Note also that `# HAMBURGUESA BRASERO` on the same menu is a `#` heading that IS a dish, so this menu's OCR marks dish cards as headings routinely and the existing "a real section is a `#` heading" guard (which correctly protects the other 8 menus) cannot be relaxed here without collateral.
- **⚠️ CORRECTION TO EVAL 116's WORDING — the third self-correction today.** That entry said the fold "FIRES" on all three markdowns. **No fold ever fired for CHURRASQUERÍA.** The July archive already contained `CHURRASQUERÍA|495|opts=5` *before* `textStructureCleanup`, and pre/post counts are both 43 — the MODEL produced Shape A natively. My probe's `foldFired()` inspected the OUTPUT shape, which was already present in the INPUT, so it reported a fold that never happened. **Eval 116's CONCLUSION is unaffected** — it rested on the bisect (live items + cached markdown ⇒ wrong shape; cached items + live markdown ⇒ right shape), which is untouched. But the mechanism was described wrongly. Lesson 23 says "if a probe surprises you, suspect the probe"; **this result did not surprise me, which is why it went unchecked — a confirming result deserves the same audit as a surprising one.**
- **Observed frequency of the brasero-two bad shape: 4 of 7 draws** (July archive good; offline draws 1/2/3 = bad/good/good; live r1/r2/r3 = bad/bad/bad). Roughly a coin flip on ~3 dims, with no deterministic repair available.
- $0. Running total unchanged ~**$0.91**. Nothing was changed in production code.

## Eval 119 — key the card fold on the PRINTED PAGE, not the model's grouping ($0): polloteria stops flipping, worst case 33 → 34/45

- **THE CHANGE:** `foldPricedHeadingCards` now derives an item's group from the markdown when the model left `section_title` null — `headingOwners()` maps each printed non-heading line to the heading above it, and `groupOf()` is used for BOTH the grouping and the replacement. Direct application of master-roadmap lesson 25(c): key a rule on what the model cannot vary.
- **FIRING LIST — THE DELIVERABLE (lessons 11-13/22), measured by stash-and-remeasure, whole objects, all 9 menus × all 3 draws = 27 dumps:**
  - **25 of 27 BYTE-IDENTICAL.**
  - `polloteria#2` and `polloteria#3`: **52 → 44 items.** Removed: `Uva, Piña, Melón, Limón, Tamarindo` (+`Yoghurt con Cajeta, Combinada Fresa-Vainilla/Yogurt, Nuez, Sandwich de nieve, Cubiertas`). Added: **`Paletas Heladas AGUA`, `Paletas Heladas CREMA`** — exactly the two cards ruling 33 specifies, making draws 2/3 match what draw 1 already produced.
  - `polloteria#1` **identical** (that draw already carried section titles, so the fold already fired).
  - Zero collateral on any other menu or draw. Restore verified clean (before/after/after-again).
- **RESULT: polloteria 3/5 · 2/5 · 2/5 → 3/5 · 3/5 · 3/5. It is no longer brittle** — no dim now changes verdict across draws. **WORST-CASE TOTAL 33 → 34/45**; best case unchanged at 37. **brasero-two is now the only brittle menu.**
- **Both pinned single-draw gates unmoved at 40/45** (`replay-edge-c3.ts` and `score-c-dumps.ts`) — the change is a strict no-op on the July archive draw. Suite **230 passed / 1 failed** (pre-existing `tile-cut_test.ts`); the 2 new tests are the mechanism and **its refusal**.
- **THE REFUSAL IS TEST-PINNED:** a line printed under two different headings is left alone rather than guessed. polloteria prints `Fresa` three times — under `# AGUA $20`, `# CREMA $30` and `# MALTEADAS $89` — and guessing would attach a 20-peso paleta to the milkshake card. `headingOwners` marks such keys ambiguous and drops them.
- **PREDICTION vs OUTCOME (lesson 16):** predicted "draws 2/3 gain `items` on polloteria, 52→42, nothing else moves". **The dimension and the blast radius were right; the number was the wrong METRIC** — 52→44 counts ALL items including drinks, while the scorer bands food-scope only, which is what passes. Quote the metric the gate actually uses.
- **⚠️ TWO FALSE STARTS, BOTH "PROVABLY CORRECT, MOVED THE SCORE BY EXACTLY ZERO" — master-roadmap lesson 20's tell, twice in one sitting:** (1) `headingOwners` stored the raw heading `AGUA $20` while the fold compares against a clean title `AGUA`, so every comparison missed — the two-sides-normalised-differently bug of lessons 12/23; (2) the fix then changed the GROUPING but not the REPLACEMENT, which still read `section_title`, so the card was built and never substituted. **What ended the loop was refusing to patch a third time and instead testing the mechanism in isolation** on a five-line synthetic markdown, where "does not fold" was visible in one command. Patching against a whole-suite score is guessing; isolate the unit.
- $0. Running total unchanged ~**$0.91**. No fixture, draft or oracle touched.

## Eval 120 — hunting the guest-house short read (~$0.10): **0 of 13 offline draws reproduce it.** The trigger is very likely the TEXT, not the model's mood

- **Santiago approved the cheap order: verify the text first, then hunt.** Both done.
- **(1) TEXT CHECK (~$0.002) — and it QUALIFIES eval 116's claim.** guest-house OCR markdown: cached **2943** chars · fresh OCR of the 2048/q95 re-encode **2944** · fresh OCR of the passthrough original (what production sends) **2939**. **Close but NOT identical** — unlike brasero-two, where all three were exactly 3967. **⇒ eval 116's "the input image does not change the OCR text" was a generalisation from ONE menu and is only APPROXIMATELY true in general** (0.1% here). Offline hunting stays valid at that magnitude, but the claim is now scoped. Same error class as lesson 25: N=1 is not a measurement.
- **(2) THE HUNT: 10 further draws on cached text (`TAG=eval117 RUN=4..13`), all 9 menus' worth of discipline applied to one menu. RESULT: 48/48 items on every single one. Combined with evals 117's three draws, that is 0 short reads in 13.**
- **🔑 THE ASYMMETRY IS THE EVIDENCE: 0/13 on CACHED text vs 1/3 on LIVE text.** If the true rate on cached text were the live-observed 1-in-3, seeing zero in 13 draws has probability **(2/3)^13 ≈ 0.5%**. **⇒ the two paths genuinely differ, and the short read is very likely tied to the LIVE TEXT rather than to model sampling alone.** **Stated with its limit (lesson 16): the live side is ONE event in n=3.** The asymmetry is strong enough to redirect the search and far too thin to call a mechanism.
- **THE ONLY MATERIAL DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO PATHS IS THE MARKDOWN** — 2939 chars (live/passthrough) vs 2943 (cached). Everything else is shared code: the same `buildStructureRequest`, the same pinned `gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, `temperature 0`, `seed 17`, `max_tokens 16384`.
- **⇒ NEXT PROBE, decisive and cheap (~$0.10): re-run the same 10-draw hunt against the PASSTHROUGH markdown instead of the cached one.** That holds the model, the parameters and the code constant and varies ONLY the text — master-roadmap lesson 27's bisect, pointed at the one variable left. If short reads appear there and not on cached text, the trigger is the text and the fix is upstream of the structuring model. If they do not appear either, the defect is rarer than 1-in-3 and the live observation was unlucky, which is a different and much less alarming conclusion.
- **DO NOT build a retry-on-short-read yet** — Santiago ruled "diagnose first", and a retry built now would be a guess at a mechanism this probe is about to name or exclude.
- Cost ~$0.10. Running total ~**$1.01**.

## Eval 121 — the guest-house short read does NOT reproduce on the PASSTHROUGH markdown either: **0 of 10 draws** (~$0.18). The static-text hypothesis is REFUTED; 0 short reads in 23 offline draws total

- Date: 2026-08-01 | Santiago-approved (~$0.10 est; actual ~$0.18, see cost note). Planner ran it directly (split collapsed, standing pattern).
- **Method — lesson 27's bisect completed, second arm.** Same 10-draw hunt as eval 120, with ONLY the Stage-1a text changed: input = `guest-house.eval116-passthrough.p0.raw.json` (the archived fresh OCR of the passthrough ORIGINAL, 2939 chars — verified through the production `ocrMarkdown` before spending) instead of the cached b1 markdown (2943). Model `gpt-4.1-2025-04-14` (verified = eval 117's, from its raw archive), same `buildStructureRequest`, temp 0 / seed 17. New env override `OCR_SRC` in `probe-c-textstructure.ts` (comma-separated raw-OCR paths replacing the b1 cache; dry-run verified both arms: 2939 vs 2943, byte-identical request otherwise). `TAG=eval121pt RUN=1..10`; every draw archived (`guest-house.eval121pt-r{1..10}.{raw,nopost.dump,dump}.json`).
- **RESULT: 48/48 items on every draw.** `finish=stop` ×10 (never truncation), `fp_c935aef436` constant, out-tokens 1914–1980 (tight band). Zero short reads.
- **⇒ THE STATIC-TEXT HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED.** If the passthrough markdown carried the live-observed 1-in-3 short-read rate, 0-in-10 has p=(2/3)^10 ≈ 1.7%. Eval 120's asymmetry argument (0/13 cached vs 1/3 live → "trigger likely the live TEXT") pointed at the wrong variable: the 4-char text difference does not cause short reads.
- **What remains, both consistent with everything measured:** (a) **fresh-OCR run-to-run variance** — the live path OCRs the photo fresh each run, and the ONE live short read's markdown was never archived (the pre-eval-117 gate threw raw away), so whether THAT run got materially different text is unknowable retroactively; guest-house OCR variance on the SAME image is unmeasured (eval 120's text check was one OCR per image variant; brasero-two's ×3-identical result does not transfer — eval 116's scoping). (b) **rare structuring-model sampling** — under a pooled rate, 1 short read in 26 total draws ≈ 4%; 0-in-23 offline is then unremarkable (p≈0.4). No further offline draws can separate (a) from (b) — only a live recurrence can, and the gate now archives BOTH stages' raw on every run (eval 117 fix), so a recurrence self-diagnoses at $0.
- **⇒ RECOMMENDATION: stop hunting offline; re-run C4 gating on the RANGE.** The defect is demonstrably rarer than 1-in-3; whether "rare silent 12-dish loss" is releasable, and whether a completeness safety-net (e.g. retry-on-short-read) is wanted despite the unnamed mechanism, are Santiago decisions. Per the diagnose-first ruling, no retry rule was built.
- **Cost note (lesson: estimates inherit errors too):** the ~$0.10 estimate was inherited from eval 120's recorded cost for the same-shaped hunt; actual usage here = 20,050 in / 19,512 out tokens ⇒ ~$0.04 + ~$0.156 ≈ **$0.18** (output dominates; eval 120's ~$0.10 was likely undercounted the same way). Running total ~**$1.19**.

The scripts still read the working copies in MenusTesting; this exists so losing that scratch folder can never again cost money to rebuild. ## Eval 127b — grams graded on RELATIVE error, tolerance 50% ($0); all 23 Andaluz printed weights pinned

- Date: 2026-08-03 | **Santiago ruling 38, revising ruling 37.** Ruling 37 had tolerated the Andaluz weight misreads. He reversed it: *"It is important for me to get the weights that are already in the menu for nutritional accuracy in the estimates, so lets modify it so that it does consider it important to read it"*, and supplied the formula himself — `relative error = |estimated − true| / true`. He then set the dial to **50%**, not the 10% recommended.
- **Why relative and not absolute:** the weight scales the macro estimate proportionally, so one absolute band cannot serve both a 30 g garnish and a 1.8 kg platter. `GRAMS_RELATIVE_TOLERANCE` + `gramsRelativeError()` in `scripts/eval-extraction.ts`; every menu's grams detail now also reports accuracy (`22/23 within 50%`), so the band is auditable instead of invisible.
- **A weight the pipeline never read is ALWAYS a miss** (`Infinity` error), at any tolerance. "No reading" is not a near-miss of a printed number, and silently passing it would hide exactly the failure this pin exists to catch.
- **What 50% means concretely and what it costs:** a dish printed 200 g passes anywhere in **100–300 g**, i.e. up to a 2× macro error reaches the ranking. Recorded here so the number is never mistaken for a measurement of accuracy — it is a dial Santiago set, and the per-dish error percentages the report prints are the evidence for where it eventually belongs.
- **The 9 existing menus did not move**: `score-c-dumps` 45/45 · `score-c-draws` 44–45 · `replay-edge-c3` 45/45 · suite 235 passed / 1 pre-existing (`tile-cut_test.ts`). Unchanged because every existing weight pin is read exactly — widening the band had nothing to loosen.
- All 23 Andaluz printed weights pinned in `andaluz.expected.json` (generated from the corrected draft, **not selected** — selecting the pins after seeing the output is how a held-out test stops being one).
- $0. Running total ~**$2.60**.

## Eval 128 — 🎯 THE HELD-OUT MENU: Andaluz scores **2/5, identical on all three draws**. Completeness and sections PERFECT first try on a menu the pipeline has never seen; all four weight errors are Stage-1a OCR, not the model

- Date: 2026-08-03 | The intake recipe's step 4, run **before touching any rule** (`scripts/fixtures/README.md`). El Andaluz is a single-page, non-dense, 36-dish Spanish menu photographed by Santiago; the truth draft came from his hand-written MD, the grading sheet from the draft.
- **Method.** Stage-1a: one live passthrough OCR of the repo photo (`ocr-passthrough-cache.ts`, new `MENUS=` filter so onboarding one menu cannot re-pay for or overwrite the nine load-bearing caches) → `andaluz.mistral-pt-r1.raw.json`, 2983 chars. **Byte-identical to the independent probe read taken on 2026-08-02 — a 10th menu confirming OCR is deterministic per image (eval 123).** Stage-1b: `probe-c-textstructure.ts andaluz`, pinned `gpt-4.1-2025-04-14`, `TAG=eval128 RUN=1..3`, every draw archived raw and backed up to `scripts/fixtures/caches/`. New `OCR_TAG` env override selects the production-mirror `pt` caches; default stays `b1` so no existing gate moved (`score-c-dumps` re-verified 45/45 after the change).
- **RESULT — `score-c-draws` MENUS=andaluz: 2/5 · 2/5 · 2/5, 36/36/36 items, ZERO flipping dims.** Fully stable; no sampling variance on this menu at all.

  | dim | verdict | detail |
  |---|---|---|
  | items | ✅ | 36/36 distinct dishes, 0 duplicates, 0 section-headers-as-items |
  | section_context | ✅ | all 5 sections (Entradas/Ensaladas/Sushi/Del Mar/Carnes), none missing, none spurious |
  | options | ❌ | 3 items missing options, **0 false positives** |
  | categories | ❌ | `PAPAS FRITAS` → `side` (sheet expects `food`) |
  | grams | ❌ | **22/23 within 50%**; only `QUESO FUNDIDO` 90 vs printed 50 (80% off) |

- **RULING-6 AUDIT (raw dumps vs the photo/MD): ZERO invented items, ZERO missing items, ZERO false-positive options.** Every one of the 36 names, all 36 prices, and both real option groups (Boneless de Pollo, Alitas) are printed on the menu. `Tacos de Papada` correctly took `maíz`/`harina` as options and correctly did NOT take "más cebolla morada y aguacate".
- **⚠️ THE DECISIVE FINDING — every weight error originates in Stage-1a OCR, verified against the raw markdown. The structuring model copied faithfully in all four cases. No prompt, schema or postprocess change can fix any of them.**

  | dish | printed | OCR markdown said | extracted | rel. error | at 50% |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Espárragos con jamón serrano | 30 g | `(20 g)` | 20 | 33% | pass |
  | Camarones Empanizados | 230 g | `(200 g)` | 200 | 13% | pass |
  | Camarón Andaluz | 230 g | `(200 g)` | 200 | 13% | pass |
  | Queso Fundido · chistorra | 50 g | `(90 g)` | 90 | 80% | **FAIL** |

  Same class, ungraded: `6 pzas.` → `8 pints.`, `panko` → `panela`, plus ~8 accent/spelling slips in descriptions (`Sazonidas`, `elate`, `champía`, `banado`, `parrilleda`, `jagó`). None reached structure. **This is the first eval where the extractor's remaining error is measurably an OCR-fidelity problem rather than a structuring problem.**
- **The 3 option misses decompose into TWO real generalization gaps and nothing else:**
  1. **Section-level option modifier (2 of the 3).** The menu prints `empanizados o naturales` on its own line under the `# sushi` heading, applying to all three rolls. **Stage-1a captured the line**; Stage-1b attached it to nothing. Same family as eval 124's bistro `Agrega a tu pasta` shape — a printed block that modifies a whole section, not one card. First time it has appeared as a MISS rather than a false positive.
  2. **Folded heading card loses its base variant.** `QUESO FUNDIDO` prints two priced variants (`Con chistorra y champis (50 g) $235`, `Con chile verde + diezmillo (100 g) $290`). The model folded them into one card at $235 but kept the FIRST variant only in the `description`, making the second the sole selectable option. The corrected draft has both as options. **The user cannot select the $235 variant** — a real selectable-options defect, not a scoring artifact.
- **The categories miss is an ORACLE error, not a pipeline error.** `PAPAS FRITAS`/`PAPAS BRAVAS` → `side` matches the ruled convention exactly: polloteria's Santiago-corrected draft labels `Papas Sazonadas` and `French Fries` `side`, and 4 of the 9 fixtures legitimately contain `side`. Nothing drops `side` items (only `drink`/`other` are filtered), so the app is unaffected. **The grading sheet I wrote is wrong — it declares `categories: ["food"]` and pins `Papas Fritas → food`. NEEDS A SANTIAGO RULING before any edit (oracle files are never edited without one).**
- **Cost:** 1 OCR call (~$0.001) + 3 structuring calls (in 2050 / out 1915 each ⇒ $0.0194/draw) = **~$0.06**. Running total ~**$2.66**.
- **⛔ NEXT (nothing may be tuned before these are decided):** (1) Santiago rules on the `side` grading sheet. (2) Decide whether the two option gaps get fixed now — both are layout-general shapes, both must be re-validated across ALL 10 menus with a firing list (lessons 11-13, 22). (3) The Queso Fundido 80% weight miss is an OCR-fidelity question, and the first candidate for a Stage-1a improvement rather than a rule.

## Eval 129 — the two option gaps FIXED ($0): section-level choice lines and the swallowed base variant. 25 options recovered across 49 draws, 0 lost; Andaluz 2/5 → 4/5

- Date: 2026-08-03 | Santiago: *"Lets fix the two options now."* Both are LAYOUT discriminators (ruling 7); neither reads a word of any language's menu vocabulary.
- **Rule 1 — `foldSectionChoiceLines`.** A bare choice line printed directly under a section heading belongs to every dish in that section (`# sushi` / `empanizados o naturales`). Accepted only when the line sits IMMEDIATELY under a heading, carries NO printed price (a priced line is a dish, and demoting a dish to an option would be silent data loss), and is not a dish the model returned. What the line MEANS is then read by the **same `parseInlineChoices`** the item-level "con X o Y" rule already uses — one matcher on both sides (lessons 12/23), so this rule can never accept a line the item-level rule would have rejected.
- **Rule 2 — `promoteDescriptionVariant`.** A card whose NAME is printed WITHOUT a price and whose DESCRIPTION is printed as its own line carrying the CARD's own price is a variant card that lost its base version. Restores it as the first option at the card's price and grams. Prose descriptions satisfy neither half — on a normal dish the priced line IS the name line.
- **FIRING LIST — 49 archived draws (`scripts/firing-list.ts`, now a reusable corpus dump): 25 options ADDED, 0 REMOVED.**

  | where | count | evidence |
  |---|---|---|
  | andaluz sushi — 3 rolls × 2 choices × 3 draws | 18 | printed `empanizados o naturales` under `# sushi` |
  | andaluz `QUESO FUNDIDO` × 3 draws | 3 | printed `Con chistorra y champis (50 g) $235` |
  | **el-marcos `WAFFLES` + `HOT CAKES`, r2 and r3** | 4 | printed `WAFFLES / Con plátano, canela y miel balsámica 70 / Con Frutos rojos 78` |

  The el-marcos four were UNPLANNED and are the interesting result: the same defect, on a menu that has been in the suite since the beginning, on 2 of 3 draws (r1 already folded correctly through `foldWeldedPrefixCards`). So the rule also makes el-marcos more consistent across draws. Verified against the printed markdown before acceptance — el-marcos has no corrected draft, so the PRINT is the oracle.
- **Seven refusal tests pinned** (`mistral-cleanup_test.ts`): a priced line under a heading, a line the model returned as a dish, prose too long to be a choice list, a normal dish whose name line carries the price, a card with no priced option.
- Gates: `score-c-dumps` 45/45 · range 44–45 · `replay-edge-c3` 45/45 · suite 235 passed / 1 pre-existing (`tile-cut_test.ts`) — **all identical before and after, measured both ways.** Andaluz **2/5 → 4/5 ×3**.
- Also landed: `tolerated_grams` (ruling 39) and the `OCR_TAG` override. $0. Running total ~**$2.66**.

## Eval 130 — 🧭 A SIDE IS A SECTION, NOT A DISH ($0). Ruling 40 encoded as neighbourhood evidence; Andaluz 4/5 → **5/5 ×3**; the held-out menu is now PERFECT

- Date: 2026-08-03 | **This entry exists because the planner got it backwards first.** Eval 128 reported Andaluz's `PAPAS FRITAS`→`side` as an ORACLE error and cited polloteria's fries as precedent. Santiago rejected it: fries under `entradas` are an appetizer, and *"its too specific to say that papas fritas is always a side and could break on menus that have them as a plate in itself and not a side."* **The citation compared DISH VOCABULARY — precisely what ruling 7 forbids.** The grading sheet was right; the pipeline was wrong. Both oracle files were reverted to `food`.
- **What is actually written down** (`extract.ts`, the production extraction prompt): *"Use category `food` for appetizers, entrees, main dishes… Use `side`, `dessert` or `drink` only when that role is clear."* **`entradas` = appetizers = food.** The rule was never ambiguous; nobody had checked it against the corpus.
- **THE MEASUREMENT (`scripts/probe-side-sections.ts`, $0, 31 draws) — what makes the role "clear" is ALWAYS the printed heading, never the dish:**

  | menu | printed heading | items labelled `side` |
  |---|---|---|
  | polloteria | `Sides` | 4/4 — **100%** |
  | guest-house | `SIDES` | 8/8 — **100%** |
  | guest-house | `ENHANCEMENTS` | 12/12 — **100%** |
  | brasero | `ACOMPAÑAMIENTOS` | 7/7 — **100%** |
  | brasero-two | `GUARNICIONES` | 4/4 — **100%** |
  | **andaluz** | **`entradas`** | **2/17 — 12%** ⬅ the only counter-example in the corpus |

- **`demoteOutvotedSides`:** a `side` survives only when at least half its section is `side`; a dish outvoted by its own section is `food`. Neighbourhood evidence, the same shape as the drink-block guard — and it carries NO language assumption, which a list of heading words never could: it reads `Sides`, `ACOMPAÑAMIENTOS` and `GUARNICIONES` without knowing any of those words. **Bar is a MAJORITY, not the drink guard's 0.8, because the costs differ: dropping a drink section DELETES dishes, this only relabels a dish that stays.** Measured margin is 100% vs 12%, so every threshold in between separates the corpus identically. **REFUSAL: a `side` with no section has no neighbours and therefore no evidence — left alone rather than guessed (the Paletas-class refusal, eval 122).**
- **FIRING LIST — 49 draws, whole-corpus item dump diffed line by line: exactly 6 lines changed, ALL of them Andaluz's two potato dishes × 3 draws.** Zero items added, zero removed, zero option changes, and all 31 real side items in the corpus untouched. Four tests pin both halves (fires on `entradas`; refuses `Sides`, `GUARNICIONES`, and the section-less case).
- **RESULT: `score-c-draws` MENUS=andaluz → 5/5 · 5/5 · 5/5, zero flipping dims.** The held-out menu is perfect on every dimension. Gates unmoved: `score-c-dumps` 45/45 · range 44–45 · `replay-edge-c3` 45/45 · suite **246 passed / 1 pre-existing**.
- **What this run is and is NOT.** The three draws are the ORIGINAL held-out responses re-scored offline — no model call was repeated, and the two option rules plus this one were written AFTER seeing them. So 5/5 is an honest measure of the fixes on this menu, and NOT a second held-out measurement. **Andaluz has now spent its one held-out shot** (`scripts/fixtures/README.md`); it joins the permanent suite, and the next generalization evidence must come from a menu none of these rules has seen.
- ⛔ **NEXT: register andaluz in the default menu lists so all 10 run by default** (it still needs `MENUS=andaluz OCR_TAG=pt` today), then the remaining phase-close items: one real DEVICE scan against the deployed function, the cheap rotation re-probe under (c), suite consolidation, and the Stage-2 macro benchmark.
- $0. Running total ~**$2.66**.

## Eval 131 — 🔻 ROTATION IS NOT FREE UNDER (c): a sideways photo reads every DISH NAME and LOSES THE PRICES. H2.2 must be built (~$0.005)

- Date: 2026-08-03 | Santiago re-ordered the plan to put rotation before the device scan and asked whether it was already handled. **It is not: `grep -rln "rotate|rotation|Orientation"` over `src/` and `supabase/functions/` returns NOTHING**, and the launch plan's own diagram says `H2 (rotation) — NOT built`. H2.1 (the detector, eval 094) is done; H2.2 (do the rotation) and H2.3 (the gate) were never started, and eval 094 predates the Mistral migration.
- **CHEAP-FIRST DESIGN, and it paid.** Stage-1b only ever sees Stage-1a's TEXT, so if the OCR of a rotated photo matched the OCR of the upright one, no rotation code could possibly be needed and the question would be closed at ~$0.001 a call. So Stage-1a alone was probed first (`scripts/probe-rotation-c.ts`): the four landscape fixtures are the ones a diner turns the phone for; `sips -r` reproduces exactly what the camera then captures — a portrait frame with the text on its side.
- **FIRST READING (misleading on its own): the OCR DOES read rotated text.** Character counts are near-identical and word recall is 84–94%, so nothing looks broken.

  | case | chars (rot vs upright) | word recall | **printed numbers lost** |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | polloteria @90° | 7294 / 7507 | 84.4% | **9 of 93** |
  | guest-house @90° | 2945 / 2939 | 94.4% | 2 of 31 |
  | **bistro @90°** | 2082 / 2173 | 92.1% | **17 of 28** |
  | polloteria @270° | 7473 / 7507 | 92.7% | 6 of 93 |
  | polloteria @180° | 7486 / 7507 | 91.7% | 2 of 93 |

- **⚠ THE FREE $0 ANALYSIS IS WHAT FOUND THE DEFECT — a word-recall percentage would have shipped this.** Diffing WHICH tokens went missing shows the loss is not noise: **bistro @90° reads all 8 pizza names and all 8 descriptions perfectly and returns ZERO prices** (`$240 $262 $240 $272 $262 $240 $208 $262` — every one gone; verified by eye against both markdowns, not by regex alone). polloteria @90° additionally drops real dish words (`bolitas`, `bomba`, `elote`, `cubeta`, `comepollito`) and `$192/$2/$4/$7`.
- **Why this is the dangerous shape:** the names survive, so an item-COUNT check passes and the scan looks healthy — while the diner is shown a menu where nothing has a price. Same silent class as the guest-house short read, and the reason ruling 6 forbids a numeric pass from being a gate.
- **Severity is wildly direction- and menu-dependent** (bistro 61% of prices lost at 90°, polloteria 2% at 180°), so "it mostly works" is not available as a position.
- **VERDICT: H2.2 must be BUILT. Phase B (structuring + scoring the rotated text, ~$0.13) was NOT run — it would only measure how bad a known-broken input scores.** The rotated OCR is archived (`<menu>.mistral-rot{90,180,270}-r1.raw.json`) so the eventual H2.3 gate has its before-picture at $0.
- Cost ~$0.005 (5 OCR calls). Running total ~**$2.67**.

## Eval 132 — the H2 detector, built and CONFIRMED BY SANTIAGO'S EYE ($0). 16/16, and rendering it as pictures caught three bugs a table hid

- Date: 2026-08-03 | Spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-h2-rotation-design.md`, approved. **Santiago could not verify the spec's decision table and said so:** he asked for a folder of rotated menus he could look at instead. That instinct was right, and building it immediately paid for itself three times.
- **His question, and the answer that matters most: "maybe rotated as it currently is could be better for the system — my decision goes whatever is best for the system, not for me."** **There is no trade-off. Upright for a human IS upright for the system** — eval 131 measured a sideways read keeping all 8 bistro dish names and dropping all 8 prices. **So his eye is a VALID ORACLE for orientation, not a proxy for one.** Recorded because the next session will face the same question.
- **`supabase/functions/analyze-menu/orientation.ts`** — `detectOrientation` (wide-frac + reading-order drift), `correctionDegrees`, `isPositivelyUpright`, `printedNumbers`, `acceptRotation`. Direction PINNED by archived geometry: bistro's header box maps upright→rotated exactly under the clockwise model `(x,y)→(H−y,x)`, to the pixel (eval 094 recorded a previous session assuming this and having it backwards).
- **RESULT — 16/16, and Santiago confirmed every corrected picture reads left-to-right.** All 11 upright fixture pages untouched; all 5 rotations pixel-identical to the original upright photo after correction.
- **⚠ THREE BUGS THE PICTURES FOUND, ALL IN MY OWN DESIGN, NONE VISIBLE IN THE TABLE:**
  1. **`printedNumbers` required a `$` or a unit** — guest-house prints bare `150`/`280`, so it scored **3 numbers** on a full menu. Every comparison there would have tied and the rule would have silently refused every correction on that menu, forever. Now counts standalone numbers: 53.
  2. **Numbers alone cannot accept a rotation.** guest-house reads **54 sideways and 53 upright** — the CORRECT fix reads one fewer. Acceptance is now `isPositivelyUpright(geometry) AND numbers ≥ 95%`, two guards covering each other's blind spots (bistro's numbers crash 32→17 when turned; guest-house's barely move). Verified on all 5 archived rotations in BOTH directions: 5/5 real fixes accepted, 5/5 false positives rejected.
  3. **The gallery's own check was arithmetic, not evidence** — it computed `(turn + fix) % 360` and rotated the ORIGINAL by the total in one step, so it verified my addition rather than the operation. It now rotates the CROOKED FILE by the correction, exactly as the app will, and compares PIXELS (uncompressed BMP — a file-byte comparison called all 5 real corrections failures, because rotating a PNG twice re-encodes it).
- **Artifact lesson, worth more than the code:** the first folder held the crooked "before" and corrected "after" images side by side, told apart only by a suffix inside a long filename. Santiago opened it, saw sideways menus, and reasonably concluded the detector was broken. **A review artifact that is half deliberately-wrong data is not a review artifact.** Now two folders: `1-what-the-camera-saw/` and `2-what-the-system-will-use/`, and the second is the whole test. Also fixed: brasero-two's two pages shared a filename and one silently overwrote the other (15 of 16 cases reached the folder).
- **STILL THIN, unchanged from the spec's known limits:** the counter-clockwise direction rests on ONE observation. ~$0.01 of extra reads closes it and belongs in the implementation plan, before the live H2.3 gate.
- $0. Running total ~**$2.67**.

## Eval 133 — H2.2 BUILT: `runPagedExtraction` splits Stage-1a/1b, accepts a rotation only if it earns it, client rotates and resubmits ($0, Tasks 1-6 of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-08-03-h2-rotation.md`)

- Date: 2026-08-03/04 | Executed the plan task-by-task (TDD-first, verify-red-then-green, gates re-run after every task). All 6 code tasks are $0 — no live API calls.
- **Task 1 — pinned the detector in tests, and found a real bug the plan's own test exposed.** `printedNumbers`'s regex refused a digit run followed by a letter, so weight-suffixed numbers glued to their unit (`300gr`, confirmed present in `brasero-two`'s real archived OCR text via grep) were not counted as standalone numbers — undercounting the exact measure `acceptRotation` uses to decide whether a rotation kept its prices. Fixed the regex (dropped the trailing-letter guard, kept the trailing-digit guard). 12 synthetic unit tests + 2 real-data tests (11 upright fixtures never rotate, 5 archived rotations all correctly identified) — both pass.
- **Task 2 — exposed `MistralOcr.blocks`.** `pageBlocks(raw)` reads the boxes Mistral has always returned; `[]` when absent so the detector degrades to "cannot tell" rather than throwing. Fixed every other stub the interface change broke (`replay-edge-c3.ts` now derives real blocks from cached raw OCR JSON; 5 synthetic test stubs in `extract_test.ts` got `blocks: []`).
- **Task 3 — the first pass asks for rotation.** Split `runPagedExtraction` into Stage-1a-for-every-page-first, THEN check orientation, THEN structure — so a crooked page's Stage-1b is never paid for. **Found and fixed a second bug: the plan's own `sidewaysRead` test fixture only remapped the x-axis, leaving y constant across all 40 synthetic blocks — zero y-axis correlation, so the detector correctly REFUSED it (returned "upright"), not a code defect.** Fixed the fixture to vary both axes, matching how the real archived rotated fixtures (proven correct in Task 1) actually look. `replay-edge-c3` unmoved at 50/50 — the phase split is a strict no-op on the 10 real fixtures.
- **Task 4 — a rotation must earn its place.** `acceptRotation` (positively upright AND numbers ≥95%) gates whether the corrected read's markdown is used, or the first pass's `prior` text (echoed back by the client) is used instead — a rejected rotation costs no third API call. **Found and fixed a third bug: `fakeBlocks(40, true)`'s wide=true branch held x constant across every block, so `isPositivelyUpright` (which demands a REAL correlation on both axes, not just an absent verdict) had no evidence to confirm, and "a rotation that worked is kept" failed even though the production code was correct.** Added a small cross-axis drift to the fixture, matching how a real left margin is never pixel-exact.
- **Task 5 — carried `rotated`/`prior` through `index.ts`.** `rotated:true` is a hard stop with no loop path back to `needs_rotation` — verified by Task 3's own test ("the server never asks twice").
- **Task 6 — the client rotates and resubmits.** `rotateImage` (expo-image-manipulator, `context.rotate(degrees)` clockwise — same convention `correctionDegrees` uses, no sign flip) lives in `compressImage.ts`; the resubmit branch in `analyzeMenu.ts` sits BEFORE the `needs_crops` check, so a sideways DENSE menu is straightened before it is tiled. `analyzeMenu.ts` also carried a pre-existing, unrelated, uncommitted debug-logging change from an earlier session (STAGE1/STAGE2 item-count `console.log`s) — staged only this task's hunks via a hand-built patch (`git apply --cached`) rather than commit someone else's in-flight work.
- **All three bugs found were in synthetic TEST FIXTURES the plan itself wrote, never in the production code** — each confirmed against real archived data (a real grep hit, or Task 1's already-passing real-data test) before touching anything, per lesson 23 ("if a probe result surprises you, suspect the probe").
- Gates unmoved throughout, task by task: `score-c-dumps` 50/50 · `score-c-draws` 49-50 · `replay-edge-c3` 50/50 · suite 246→267 passed (added 21 new tests across 6 commits) / 1 pre-existing (`tile-cut_test.ts`).
- 6 commits, `f4585f0`..`e7a00e7`, pushed. $0. Running total ~**$2.67**.

## Eval 134 — H2.2 Task 7: close the thin direction, 9 more OCR reads, Santiago confirmed all 25 gallery images ($0.008)

- Date: 2026-08-04 | The counter-clockwise verdict (eval 132) rested on ONE observation (polloteria @270°). Extended `probe-rotation-c.ts`'s `CASES` to cover bistro/guest-house/el-marcos at 90/180/270 (polloteria already had all three) plus nikkori@90 and brasero@270 — two portrait menus turned sideways, since a diner can hold the phone wrong on any menu, not just wide ones.

The model's own draw-to-draw variance for these 3 menus is already the standing, larger-sample measurement in `score-c-draws.ts`, 49-50/50, unaffected by this change).

Backed up the pre-deploy function (v21) to `supabase/backups/deployed-fn-2026-08-04/` (primary folder) via `supabase functions download` run from THAT folder as cwd — **first attempt ran it from the worktree root by mistake and it overwrote 9 live TypeScript source files with the deployed bundle's stripped/decompiled JS** (comments and type annotations gone). Caught immediately via `git status`; all 9 were tracked and uncommitted-clean, so `git restore` recovered them byte-for-byte with zero loss — verified by re-running the full suite (267 passed / 1 pre-existing, same as before). **Lesson: `supabase functions download` always writes to `./supabase/functions/<name>/` relative to cwd — run it from an empty backup folder, never from a live source tree.**
